&\quad=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}}\,d\mu\,(\mu-\lambda\mp i0)^{-1}\big(\widetilde{h}(\mu,\,\cdot\,),\widetilde{g}(\mu,\,\cdot\,)\big)_{[L^{2}(S^{n-1})]^{N}},\quad g\in[S({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}.\end{split}

(3.66)

Since

\displaystyle\widetilde{h},\widetilde{g}\in L^{2}\big([0,\infty);d\lambda;L^{2}(S^{n-1})^{N/2}\big)\oplus\,L^{2}\big((-\infty,0]);d\lambda;L^{2}(S^{n-1})^{N/2}\big),

(3.67)

it suffices to estimate the integral in (
3.66
) over a compact neighborhood, say

X_{\lambda}
, of the point
\lambda
. By Proposition
3.3
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\big\|\widetilde{h}(\mu,\,\cdot\,)\big\|_{[L^{2}(S^{n-1})]^{N}}&=\big\|\widetilde{h}(\lambda,\,\cdot\,)-\widetilde{h}(\mu,\,\cdot\,)\big\|_{[L^{2}(S^{n-1})]^{N}}\\
&\leqslant C_{0}|\lambda-\mu|^{\tau-(1/2)}\|h\|_{[L_{\tau}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}},\end{split}

(3.68)

for some
C_{0}\in(0,\infty)
, and consequently,

\displaystyle\bigg|\int_{X_{\lambda}}\,d\mu\,(\mu-\lambda\mp i0)^{-1}\big(\widetilde{h}(\mu,\,\cdot\,),\widetilde{g}(\mu,\,\cdot\,)\big)_{[L^{2}(S^{n-1})]^{N}}\bigg|

(3.69)

\displaystyle\quad\leqslant C_{0}\|h\|_{[L_{\tau}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}}\int_{X_{\lambda}}\,d\mu\,|\lambda-\mu|^{\tau-(3/2)}\big\|\widetilde{g}(\mu,\,\cdot\,)\big\|_{[L^{2}(S^{n-1})]^{N}},\quad g\in[S({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}.

An application of Hölder’s inequality yields for any conjugate pair
p^{-1}+q^{-1}=1
the estimate

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int_{X_{\lambda}}\,d\mu\,|\lambda-\mu|^{\tau-(3/2)}\big\|\widetilde{g}(\mu,\,\cdot\,)\big\|_{[L^{2}(S^{n-1})]^{N}}\\
&\quad\leqslant\bigg(\int_{X_{\lambda}}\,d\mu\,|\lambda-\mu|^{-q((3/2)-\tau)}\bigg)^{q^{-1}}\bigg(\int_{X_{\lambda}}\,d\mu\,\big|\widetilde{g}(\mu,\,\cdot\,)\big\|_{[L^{2}(S^{n-1})]^{N}}^{p}\bigg)^{p^{-1}}.\end{split}

(3.70)

By (
3.57
) with
C_{2}=C_{2}(\alpha,p,X_{\lambda})
,

\int_{X_{\lambda}}\,d\mu\,\big\|\widetilde{g}(\mu,\,\cdot\,)\big\|_{[L^{2}(S^{n-1})]^{N}}^{p}\leqslant C_{2}\|g\|_{[L_{\widetilde{\tau}}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}}^{p},

(3.71)

where
p<2(1-2\widetilde{\tau})^{-1}
. Therefore, the conjugate exponent satisfies
q>2(1+2\widetilde{\tau})^{-1}
, and consequently
q((3/2)-\tau)>(3-2\tau)(1+2\widetilde{\tau})^{-1}
. Thus, if
\widetilde{\tau}>1-\tau
, that is,
if
\tau+\widetilde{\tau}>1
, then
(3-2\tau)(1+2\widetilde{\tau})^{-1}<1
, so
q((3/2)-\tau)
may be chosen to be smaller than
1
, rendering the first integral on the right-hand side in (
3.70
) finite. In conclusion, (
3.69
), (
3.70
), and (
3.71
) combine to yield the desired estimate.

Finally, we turn to the issue of absence of singular continuous spectrum for
H
. Introducing the set

{\mathcal{N}}:={\mathcal{N}}_{+}\cup{\mathcal{N}}_{-}
, so that

\sigma_{s}(H)\backslash\{0\}\subseteq{\mathcal{N}},

(3.72)

to prove that
\sigma_{sc}(H)=\emptyset
, it suffices to show that any
\lambda\in{\mathcal{N}}
must be an eigenvalue of
H
. To this end, let
\lambda\in{\mathcal{N}}
, so that there exists an
f\in[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}\backslash\{0\}
such that

-f=\overline{V_{2}(H_{0}-(\lambda\pm i0)I_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-1}V_{1}^{*}}f,

(3.73)

with
V_{1}
and
V_{2}
taken as in (
3.37
) with
\tau\in(1/2,\rho-(1/2))
. Introducing

g=\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle^{-\tau}f\in[L_{\tau}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N},

(3.74)

the equations for
f
in (
3.73
) may be recast as (
3.56
). In view of (
3.74
) and the fact that
\widetilde{g}(\lambda)=0
, the estimate in (
3.64
) applies to
h=g
:

(H_{0}-(\lambda\pm i0)I_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-1}g\in[L_{-\widetilde{\tau}}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N},\quad\widetilde{\tau}>1-\tau.

(3.75)

Then the condition in (
3.3
) and the identity in (
3.56
) combine to yield

g=-V(H_{0}-(\lambda\pm i0)I_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-1}g\in[L_{\nu}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N},\quad\nu<\tau+\rho-1.

(3.76)

Iterating the same argument
\ell\in{\mathbb{N}}
times yields

g\in[L_{\nu}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N},\quad\nu<\tau+\ell(\rho-1).

(3.77)

If
\ell
is chosen so that
\ell>(1-\tau)/(\rho-1)
, then
\tau+\ell(\rho-1)>1
. Therefore, (
3.77
) implies, in particular, that
g\in[L_{\widetilde{\tau}}^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}
for some
\widetilde{\tau}\in(1,3/2)
. Consequently, by Proposition
3.3
,
\widetilde{g}
is Hölder continuous of order
\widetilde{\tau}-(1/2)
. Next, the
function
\psi:=(H_{0}-(\lambda\pm i0)I_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-1}g
belongs to
\dom(H)=\dom(H_{0})=[W^{1,2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}

since
\widetilde{\psi}(\mu)=\widetilde{g}(\mu)(\mu-\lambda)^{-1}
,
\widetilde{g}(\lambda)=0
, and
\widetilde{\tau}-(1/2)>1/2
. By (
3.56
),
\psi
satisfies the Dirac equation
H\psi=\lambda\psi
. Moreover,
\psi
is a nontrivial solution. Indeed, if
\psi=0
, then
g=-V\psi=0
. Of course, one then obtains
f=\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle^{\tau}g=0
, which contradicts the assumption
f\in[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}\backslash\{0\}
. Therefore,
\psi
is an eigenfunction and
\lambda
is a corresponding eigenvalue. As a result,
{\mathcal{N}}\subseteq\sigma_{p}(H)
and
\sigma_{sc}(H)=\emptyset
.
∎

Remark 3.5
.

The fact that
\big\|\overline{V_{2}(H_{0}-(\lambda\pm i0)I_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-1}V_{1}^{*}}\big\|_{{\mathcal{B}}(L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3}))}
does not
decay as
\lambda\to\pm\infty
shows that in principle one cannot rule out eigenvalues of
H
running off to
\infty

and/or
-\infty
. In fact, it has been shown in
[
104
]
that for all
\tau>1/2
, there exists a constant
C_{\tau}\in(0,\infty)
such that

\sup_{z\in{\mathbb{C}}\backslash{\mathbb{R}}}\big\|\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle^{-\tau}(H_{0}-zI_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})]^{4}})^{-1}\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle^{-\tau}\big\|_{{\mathcal{B}}([L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})]^{4})}\leqslant C_{\tau}\quad

(3.78)

and that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\big\|\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle^{-\tau}(H_{0}-(\lambda\pm i0)I_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})]^{4}})^{-1}\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle^{-\tau}\big\|_{{\mathcal{B}}([L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})]^{4})}&\\
\text{ does not decay as $|\lambda|\to\infty$ for any $\tau>1/2$.}&\end{split}

(3.79)

In the case of massive Dirac operators (i.e., with
H_{0}
replaced by
H_{0}(m)
), the condition
\tau>1/2
needs to
be replaced by
\tau\geqslant 1
. For results in this direction we also refer to
[
130
]
,
[
131
]
,
[
132
]
,
[
188
]
.
This contrasts sharply with the case of Schrödinger operators where a Riemann–Lebesgue-type argument yields
decay of the underlying Birman–Schwinger operator (see, e.g.,
[
156
, Theorem III.13]
).
\diamond

Remark 3.6
.

The transformation in (
3.17
) employed to diagonalize
\alpha\cdot p
is similar to the celebrated Foldy–Wouthuysen transformation (see, e.g.,
[
46
]
,
[
161
]
,
[
164
, Sect. 5.6]
). The latter is well known to diagonalize
H_{0}
. In fact, introducing the unitary
N\times N
block operator matrix
U_{N}
in
[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}
,
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
n\geqslant 2
,
via

U_{N}=2^{-1/2}\big[I_{N}+\beta(\alpha\cdot(-i\nabla))|-i\nabla|^{-1}\big],\quad U_{N}^{-1}=2^{-1/2}\big[I_{N}-\beta(\alpha\cdot(-i\nabla))|-i\nabla|^{-1}\big],

(3.80)

one infers that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\widetilde{H}_{0}=U_{N}H_{0}U_{N}^{-1}=\begin{pmatrix}I_{N/2}(-\Delta)^{1/2}&0\\
0&-I_{N/2}(-\Delta)^{1/2}\end{pmatrix},\\
&\dom\big(\widetilde{H}_{0}\big)=[W^{1,2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}.\end{split}

(3.81)

It is worth pointing out that every result in this section has a verbatim analog for operators of the
type

\widetilde{H}_{0}+V,\,\text{ and }\,I_{N}\big(-\Delta+m^{2}I_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})}\big)^{1/2}+V,\;m\geqslant 0,

(3.82)

in
[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}
, with
V
satisfying (
3.3
). More generally,
(-\Delta)^{1/2}
can be replaced
by general fractional powers
(-\Delta)^{\gamma}
,
\gamma>0
, and even by more general
functions
h(-\Delta)
(cf.
[
21
]
). This comment is of some significance as a large body of
work went into studying
I_{N}(-\Delta)^{1/2}+V
(especially, in the scalar case
N=1
) over the past two decades. We refer, for instance, to
[
21
]
,
[
32
]
,
[
92
]
,
[
108
]
,
[
109
]
,

[
116
]
,
[
142
]
,
[
157
, p. 124]
,
[
171
]
,
[
172
]
,
[
173
]
,
[
174
]
,
[
175
]
,
[
176
]
,
[
181
]
.
\diamond

In the following section we will recall conditions on
V
that yield the absence of eigenvalues
of
H
(implying unitary equivalence of
H
and
H_{0}
via the wave operators
W_{\pm}(H,H_{0})

in Theorem
3.4
, see Remark
4.3
).

We conclude this section with some hints at additional literature (beyond

[
185
, Sects. 1.11, 2.1, 2.2]
) concerning the absence of singular continuous spectrum
and proofs of limiting absorption principles for operators of the form
H_{0}+V
.

In the the case of three-dimensional massless Dirac operators, the absence of singular continuous spectrum of
H
with scalar potentials
(i.e.,
V=v\,I_{N}
), including the case of long-range interactions
v
, was proved in
[
47
]
. The limiting absorption
principle for
H_{0}
in three dimensions was derived in
[
149
]
. For the proof of existence of absolutely continuous spectrum of massless Dirac operators for
n=3
, where
V=v\,\beta
, see
[
49
]
. To the best of our knowledge, these references in the special case
n=3
comprise all explicit statements about the absence of the singular continuous spectrum of
H
and/or the limiting absorption principle for
H_{0}
. So Theorem
3.4
is new for
n\in{\mathbb{N}}\backslash\{3\}
,
n\geqslant 2
, which is particularly interesting in the case
n=2
as the latter is related to applications involving graphene. On the other hand, we emphasize that Theorem
3.4
is a direct consequence of the material presented by Yafaev in
[
185
, Sect. 2.4]
. In the context of massless Dirac operators in dimension
n=2
we also refer to
[
60
]
(see also
[
59
]
).We also note that a global limiting absorption principle for
H_{0}
on
{\mathbb{R}}
for all
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
n\geqslant 2
, was proved in
[
40
]
,
[
104
]
,
[
22
]
.

For the case of massive Dirac operators
H(m)=H+m\,\beta
,
m>0
, we also refer to
[
18
]
,
[
33
]
,
[
34
]
,
[
65
]
,

]
,
[

]
,
[

]
,
[

]
,
[

]
,
[

]
,

[
133
]
,
[
148
]
,
[
166
]
,
[

]
,
[

, Sect. 1.12]
,
[

]
,
[

]
,
[

]
.

Finally, for scattering theory for Dirac operators we refer, for instance, to
[

]
,

,

,

.

4.
On the Absence of Eigenvalues for Interacting, Massless Dirac Operators

In this section we briefly comment on results concerning the absence of eigenvalues of
massless Dirac operators. Since we are particularly interested in potentials vanishing
at
\pm\infty
, implying the absence of spectral gaps of
H
,

\sigma_{ess}(H)={\mathbb{R}},

(4.1)

the absence of eigenvalues is equivalent to the absence of eigenvalues embedded into the
essential spectrum of
H
(a somewhat unusual situation from a quantum mechanical point
of view).

In the context of massive Dirac operators
H(m)=H+m\,\beta
, with mass parameter

m>0
and vanishing potentials at
\pm\infty
, there exists a fair number of papers
describing the absence of embedded eigenvalues in the essential spectrum of
H(m)
,

\sigma_{ess}(H(m))=(-\infty,-m]\cup[m,\infty)

(4.2)

(or in certain regions of the essential spectrum), especially in the three-dimensional case,
n=3
. Relevant references in this context are,
for instance,
[
9
]
,
[
25
]
,
[
99
]
,
[
100
]
,
[
103
]
,
[
125
]
,

[
144
]
(however, this reference is imprecise w.r.t. implicit smoothness assumptions on the electromagnetic potential coefficients),
[
167
]
,
[
178
]
.

The existence of threshold eigenvalues (and/or resonances) at
\pm m
are discussed, for
instance, in
[
56
]
,
[
151
]
.

In the massless case,
m=0
, zero eigenvalues and/or zero-energy resonances (as well as the absence zero-energy resonances) are treated in

[
2
]
,
[
3
]
,
[
4
]
,
[
5
]
,
[
7
]
,
[
8
]
,

[
13
]
,
[
14
]
,
[
15
]
,
[
16
, Ch. 4]
,
[
17
]
,
[
24
]
,

[
52
]
,
[
55
]
,
[
57
]
,
[
70
]
,
[
72
]
,
[
103
]
,
[
115
]
,

[
128
]
,
[
143
]
,
[
145
]
,
[
153
]
,
[
149
]
,
[
150
]
,
[
151
]
,

[
152
]
,
[
154
]
,
[
155
]
,
[
189
]
. A fair number of these references consider
the case of Pauli operators in three dimensions,
[\sigma\cdot(-i\nabla-A)]^{2}
, with

\sigma=(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3})
the standard Pauli matrices.

The subject of absence of zero modes, especially, zero-energy eigenvalues, for massless Dirac operators has hardly been studied. Exceptions are
[
48
]
(see also
[
101
]
),

[
102
]
,
[
103
]
, and properties
of the corresponding (generalized) eigenfunctions are discussed in

[
8
]
,
[
16
, Sect. 4.4]
,
[
17
]
,
[
149
]
,
[
150
]
,
[
189
]
.

Here we recall the following special cases of results in
[
103
, Theorems 2.1, 2.3]
:

Theorem 4.1
.

Let
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
n\geqslant 2
.

(i)
Assume that
V:{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\to{\mathbb{C}}^{N\times N}
is Lebesgue measurable and self-adjoint
a.e. on
{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
, satisfying

\esssup_{x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}|x|\|V(x)\|_{{\mathcal{B}}({\mathbb{C}}^{N})}\leqslant C\,\text{ for some $C\in(0,(n-1)/2)$,}

(4.3)

with
\|\,\cdot\,\|_{{\mathcal{B}}({\mathbb{C}}^{N})}
denoting the operator norm of an
N\times N
matrix in
{\mathbb{C}}^{N}
. Then any distributional solution

u\in[H^{1}_{loc}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}\cap[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n};|x|^{-1}d^{n}x)]^{N}
of

(H_{0}+V)u=0
vanishes identically.

(ii)
Suppose that

V:{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\to{\mathbb{C}}^{n\times n}
is Lebesgue measurable and self-adjoint
a.e. on
{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
, and that

for some
R>0
,
V\in\big[C^{1}(E_{R})\big]^{N\times N}
, where

E_{R}=\{x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\,|\,|x|>R\}
,

(4.4)

and

\displaystyle\begin{split}&|x|^{1/2}V_{\ell,m}(x)\underset{|x|\to\infty}{=}o(1),\quad(x\cdot\nabla V_{\ell,m})(x)\underset{|x|\to\infty}{=}o(1),\quad 1\leqslant\ell,m\leqslant N,\\
&\quad\text{uniformly with respect to directions.}\end{split}

(4.5)

Then if for some
\lambda\in{\mathbb{R}}\backslash\{0\}
,
u\in[L^{2}(E_{R})]^{N}
satisfies

(H_{0}+V)u=\lambda u
on
E_{R}
in the distributional sense, then
u
vanishes identically on

{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
.

(iii)
The self-adjoint realization
H
of
H_{0}+V
satisfying

\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x\,|x|^{-1}\|f(x)\|_{{\mathbb{C}}^{N}}^{2}<\infty,\quad f\in\dom(H),

(4.6)

has no eigenvalue zero in case
(i)
and no eigenvalue
\lambda\in{\mathbb{R}}\backslash\{0\}

in case
(ii)
.

Remark 4.2
.

We note that Theorem
4.1
due to
[
103
, Theorems 2.1, 2.3]
appears to have been the first, and up to now, the only result available proving absence of eigenvalues of
H
in the massless case. The results on global
H_{0}
-smoothness of
\langle Q\rangle^{-1}
proven in
[
40
]
now yields a second such result (and unitary equivalence of
H
and
H_{0}
) for
\|V\|_{{\mathcal{B}}(L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n}))}
sufficiently small.

\diamond

Remark 4.3
.

In the context of Theorem
4.1

(iii)
we note that if
V
satisfies (
3.3
) and hence

\dom(H)=[W^{1,2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}
, then Kato’s inequality (cf., e.g.,
[
16
, p. 19–20]
,

[
91
]
),

\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x\,|x|^{-1}|f(x)|^{2}\leqslant C_{n}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}p\,|p|\big|\widehat{f}(p)\big|^{2},\quad f\in S({\mathbb{R}}^{n}),\;n\in{\mathbb{N}},\;n\geqslant 2,

(4.7)

for appropriate constants
C_{n}\in(0,\infty)
,
n\geqslant 2
(Kato’s inequality extends to the homogeneous Sobolev space
D^{1/2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})
of order
1/2
), yields, in particular,

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x\,|x|^{-1}|f(x)|^{2}\leqslant C_{n}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}p\,|p|\big|\widehat{f}(p)\big|^{2}\leqslant C_{n}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}p\,\big[1+|p|^{2}\big]\big|\widehat{f}(p)\big|^{2},

\displaystyle\hskip 199.16928ptf\in H^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{n}),\;n\in{\mathbb{N}},\;n\geqslant 2.

(4.8)

Thus, under assumption (
3.3
) on
V
, condition (
4.6
) holds automatically.
\diamond

We summarize the discussion on absence of eigenvalues in this section as follows:

Corollary 4.4
.

(i)
In addition to Hypothesis
3.1
assume that
V
satisfies conditions (
4.4
), (
4.5
). Then

\sigma_{p}(H)\subseteq\{0\}.

(4.9)

(ii)
In addition to Hypothesis
3.1
assume that
V
satisfies conditions (
4.3
)–(
4.5
). Then

\sigma_{p}(H)=\emptyset.

(4.10)

Moreover,
H
and
H_{0}
are unitarily equivalent.

Proof.

The inclusion (
4.9
) follows from Theorem
4.1

(ii)
,
(iii)
and Remark
4.3
.
The fact (
4.10
) follows from Theorem
4.1

(i)
, Remark
4.3
, and (
4.9
). Unitary equivalence of
H
and
H_{0}

is a consequence of (
3.44
), (
3.50
), (
3.51
), and (
4.10
).
∎

5.
The Green’s Functions of
H_{0}(m)
and
H_{0}

In this section we study the Green’s function for
H_{0}
, that is, the integral kernel of the resolvent
of
H_{0}
.

We start, however, with the Green’s function of the Laplacian in
L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})
,

h_{0}=-\Delta,\quad\dom(h_{0})=H^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n}).

(5.1)

The Green’s function of
h_{0}
, denoted by
g_{0}(z;\,\cdot\,,\,\cdot\,)
, is of the form,

\displaystyle g_{0}(z;x,y):=(h_{0}-zI_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})})^{-1}(x,y)

\displaystyle\quad=\begin{cases}(i/2)z^{-1/2}e^{iz^{1/2}|x-y|},&n=1,\;z\in{\mathbb{C}}\backslash\{0\},\\[5.69054pt]
(i/4)\big(2\pi z^{-1/2}|x-y|\big)^{(2-n)/2}H^{(1)}_{(n-2)/2}\big(z^{1/2}|x-y|\big),&n\geqslant 2,\;z\in{\mathbb{C}}\backslash\{0\},\end{cases}

\displaystyle\hskip 179.25244pt\text{\rm Im}\big(z^{1/2}\big)>0,\;x,y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n},\;x\neq y,

(5.2)

and for
z=0
,
n\geqslant 3
,

g_{0}(0;x,y)=\displaystyle{\frac{1}{(n-2)\omega_{n-1}}|x-y|^{2-n}},\quad n\geqslant 3,\;x,y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n},\;x\neq y.

(5.3)

Here
H^{(1)}_{\nu}(\,\cdot\,)
denotes the Hankel function of the first kind
with index
\nu\geqslant 0
(cf.
[
1
, Sect. 9.1]
) and

\omega_{n-1}=2\pi^{n/2}/\Gamma(n/2)
(
\Gamma(\,\cdot\,)
the Gamma function,
cf.
[
1
, Sect. 6.1]
) represents the area of the unit sphere
S^{n-1}
in
{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
.

As
z\to 0
,
g_{0}(z;\,\cdot\,,\,\cdot\,)
is continuous on the off-diagonal for
n\geqslant 3
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\lim_{\begin{subarray}{c}z\to 0\\
z\in{\mathbb{C}}\backslash\{0\}\end{subarray}}g_{0}(z;x,y)=g_{0}(0;x,y)=\frac{1}{(n-2)\omega_{n-1}}|x-y|^{2-n},&\\
x,y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n},\;x\neq y,\;n\in{\mathbb{N}},\;n\geqslant 3,&\end{split}

(5.4)

but blows up for
n=1
as

\displaystyle g_{0}(z;x,y)\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}z\to 0\\
z\in{\mathbb{C}}\backslash\{0\}\end{subarray}}{=}(i/2)z^{-1/2}-2^{-1}|x-y|+O\big(z^{1/2}|x-y|^{2}\big),\quad x,y\in{\mathbb{R}},

(5.5)

and for
n=2
as

\displaystyle\begin{split}&g_{0}(z;x,y)\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}z\to 0\\
z\in{\mathbb{C}}\backslash\{0\}\end{subarray}}{=}-\frac{1}{2\pi}\text{\rm ln}\big(z^{1/2}|x-y|/2\big)\big[1+O\big(z|x-y|^{2}\big)\big]+\frac{1}{2\pi}\psi(1)\\
&\hskip 76.82234pt+O\big(|z||x-y|^{2}\big),\quad x,y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2},\;x\neq y.\end{split}

(5.6)

Here
\psi(w)=\Gamma^{\prime}(w)/\Gamma(w)
denotes the digamma function
(cf.
[
1
, Sect. 6.3]
).

For reasons of subsequent comparisons with the case of the free massive Dirac operator

H_{0}(m)=H_{0}+m\,\beta
,
m>0
, we now start with the latter and compute,

\displaystyle(H_{0}(m)-zI_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-1}=(H_{0}(m)+zI_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})\big(H_{0}(m)^{2}-z^{2}I_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}}\big)^{-1}

\displaystyle\quad=(-i\alpha\cdot\nabla+m\,\beta+zI_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})\big(h_{0}-\big(z^{2}-m^{2}\big)I_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}}\big)^{-1}I_{N},

(5.7)

employing

H_{0}(m)^{2}=(h_{0}+m^{2}I_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})I_{N}.

(5.8)

Assuming

m>0,\;z\in{\mathbb{C}}\backslash({\mathbb{R}}\backslash[-m,m]),\;\text{\rm Im}\big(z^{2}-m^{2}\big)^{1/2}>0,\;x,y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n},\;x\neq y,\;n\in{\mathbb{N}},\;n\geqslant 2,

(5.9)

and exploiting (
5.7
), one thus obtains for the Green’s function
G_{0}(m,z;\,\cdot\,,\,\cdot\,)

of
H_{0}(m)
,

\displaystyle G_{0}(m,z;x,y):=(H_{0}(m)-zI_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-1}(x,y)

\displaystyle\quad=i4^{-1}(2\pi)^{(2-n)/2}|x-y|^{2-n}(m\,\beta+zI_{N})

\displaystyle\qquad\times\big[\big(z^{2}-m^{2}\big)^{1/2}|x-y|\big]^{(n-2)/2}H_{(n-2)/2}^{(1)}\big(\big(z^{2}-m^{2}\big)^{1/2}|x-y|\big)

\displaystyle\qquad-4^{-1}(2\pi)^{(2-n)/2}|x-y|^{1-n}\,\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\times\big[\big(z^{2}-m^{2}\big)^{1/2}|x-y|\big]^{n/2}H_{n/2}^{(1)}\big(\big(z^{2}-m^{2}\big)^{1/2}|x-y|\big).

(5.10)

Here we employed the identity (
[
1
, p. 361]
),

\big[H_{\nu}^{(1)}(\zeta)\big]^{\prime}=-H_{\nu+1}^{(1)}(\zeta)+\nu\,\zeta^{-1}H_{\nu}^{(1)}(\zeta),\quad\nu,\zeta\in{\mathbb{C}}.

(5.11)

Equations (
B.9
), (
B.10
) reveal the facts (still assuming (
5.9
)),

\displaystyle\lim_{\begin{subarray}{c}z\to\pm m\\
z\in{\mathbb{C}}\backslash\{\pm m\}\end{subarray}}G_{0}(m,z;x,y)=4^{-1}\pi^{-n/2}\Gamma((n-2)/2)|x-y|^{2-n}(m\,\beta\pm mI_{N})

(5.12)

\displaystyle\quad+i2^{-1}\pi^{-n/2}\Gamma(n/2)\,\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|^{n}},\quad m>0,\;x,y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n},\;x\neq y,\;n\in{\mathbb{N}},\;n\geqslant 3,

\displaystyle G_{0}(m,z;x,y)\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}z\to\pm m\\
z\in{\mathbb{C}}\backslash\{\pm m\end{subarray}}{=}-(4\pi)^{-1}\text{\rm ln}\big(z^{2}-m^{2}\big)(m\,\beta\pm mI_{2})

\displaystyle\quad-(2\pi)^{-1}\text{\rm ln}(|x-y|)(m\,\beta\pm mI_{2})+i(2\pi)^{-1}\,\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|^{2}}

(5.13)

\displaystyle\quad+O\big(\big(z^{2}-m^{2}\big)\text{\rm ln}\big(z^{2}-m^{2}\big)\big),\quad m>0,\;x,y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2},\;x\neq y.

(Here the remainder term
O\big(\big(z^{2}-m^{2}\big)\text{\rm ln}\big(z^{2}-m^{2}\big)\big)
depends
on
x,y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
, but this is of no concern at this point.)
In particular,
G_{0}(m,z;\,\cdot\,,\,\cdot\,)
blows up logarithmically as
z\to\pm m

in two dimensions,
n=2
, just as
g_{0}(z,\,\cdot\,,\,\cdot\,)
does as
z\to 0
.

By contrast, the massless case is quite different and assuming

z\in{\mathbb{C}}_{+},\;x,y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n},\;x\neq y,\;n\in{\mathbb{N}},\;n\geqslant 2,

(5.14)

one computes in the case
m=0
for the Green’s function
G_{0}(z;\,\cdot\,,\,\cdot\,)
of
H_{0}
,

\displaystyle G_{0}(z;x,y):=(H_{0}-zI_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-1}(x,y)

\displaystyle\quad=i4^{-1}(2\pi)^{(2-n)/2}|x-y|^{2-n}z\,[z|x-y|]^{(n-2)/2}H_{(n-2)/2}^{(1)}(z|x-y|)I_{N}

(5.15)

\displaystyle\qquad-4^{-1}(2\pi)^{(2-n)/2}|x-y|^{1-n}[z|x-y|]^{n/2}H_{n/2}^{(1)}(z|x-y|)\,\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|}.

The Green’s function
G_{0}(z;\,\cdot\,,\,\cdot\,)
of
H_{0}
continuously extends to
z\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}}
. In addition, in the massless case
m=0
, the limit
z\to 0
exists
1
1

1

Our choice of notation
0+i\,0
in

G_{0}(0+i\,0;x,y)
indicates that the limit
\lim_{z\to 0}
is performed in the closed upper half-plane

\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}}
.
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\lim_{\begin{subarray}{c}z\to 0,\\
z\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}}\backslash\{0\}\end{subarray}}G_{0}(z;x,y):=G_{0}(0+i\,0;x,y)\\
&\quad=i2^{-1}\pi^{-n/2}\Gamma(n/2)\,\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|^{n}},\quad x,y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n},\;x\neq y,\;n\in{\mathbb{N}},\;n\geqslant 2,\end{split}

(5.16)

and no blow up occurs for all
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
n\geqslant 2
.

Remark 5.1
.

(i)
The observation of an absence of blow up in
G_{0}(z;\,\cdot\,,\,\cdot\,)
as
z\to 0
is consistent with the sufficient condition for the Dirac operator
H=H_{0}+V
(in dimensions
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
n\geqslant 2
), with
V
an appropriate self-adjoint
N\times N
matrix-valued potential, having no eigenvalues, as derived in
[
103
, Theorems 2.1, 2.3]
.

(ii)
The asymptotic behavior, for some
d_{n}\in(0,\infty)
,

\displaystyle\|G_{0}(0+i\,0;x,y)\|_{{\mathbb{C}}^{N}}\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}z\to 0,\\
z\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}}\backslash\{0\}\end{subarray}}{=}d_{n}|x-y|^{1-n},\quad x,y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n},\;x\neq y,\;n\in{\mathbb{N}},\;n\geqslant 2,

(5.17)

implies the absence of zero-energy resonances (cf. Section
10
for a detailed discussion) of
H
for
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
n\geqslant 3
, for sufficiently fast decaying short-range potentials
V
at infinity, as
|\,\cdot\,|^{1-n}
lies in

L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})
near infinity if and only if
n\geqslant 3
. This is consistent with observations in
[
8
]
,
[
16
, Sect. 4.4]
,
[
17
]
,
[
28
]
,
[
149
]
,
[
150
]
,
[
189
]
for
n=3
(see also Remark
10.8

(ii)
).
This should be contrasted with the behavior of Schrödinger operators where

\displaystyle\begin{split}\lim_{\begin{subarray}{c}z\to 0\\
z\in{\mathbb{C}}\backslash\{0\}\end{subarray}}g_{0}(z;x,y)=g_{0}(0;x,y)=\frac{1}{(n-2)\omega_{n-1}}|x-y|^{2-n},&\\
x,y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n},\;x\neq y,\;n\in{\mathbb{N}},\;n\geqslant 3,&\end{split}

(5.18)

implies the absence of zero-energy resonances of
h=h_{0}+w
for
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
n\geqslant 5
, again for sufficiently fast decaying short-range potentials
w
at infinity, as
|\,\cdot\,|^{2-n}
lies in
L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})
near infinity if and only if
n\geqslant 5
, as observed in
[
95
]
.

\diamond

Remark 5.2
.

In the special case
n=3
, the identities

\displaystyle H_{1/2}^{(1)}(\zeta)

\displaystyle=-i\bigg(\frac{2}{\pi}\bigg)^{1/2}\frac{e^{i\zeta}}{\zeta^{1/2}},\quad

(5.19)

\displaystyle H_{3/2}^{(1)}(\zeta)

\displaystyle=-\bigg(\frac{2}{\pi}\bigg)^{1/2}\frac{e^{i\zeta}(\zeta+i)}{\zeta^{3/2}},\quad\zeta\in{\mathbb{C}}\backslash\{0\},

(5.20)

combine in (
5.15
) to yield

\displaystyle G_{0}(z;x,y)

(5.21)

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{e^{iz|x-y|}}{4\pi|x-y|}\bigg[z\,I_{N}+z\,\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|}+i\,\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|^{2}}\bigg],\quad x,y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3},\;x\neq y,\;z\in{\mathbb{C}}_{+}.

\diamond

Remark 5.3
.

It is possible to expand the massless Dirac Green’s function
G_{0}(z;\,\cdot\,,\,\cdot\,)
in powers of
z
in such a way that several coefficients in the expansion vanish (the precise number of vanishing coefficients depending on the dimension
n
) for odd dimensions
n\geqslant 5
. This observation relies on the following connection between the modified Bessel and spherical Bessel functions (cf., e.g.,
[
1
, 10.1.1]
):

\displaystyle H_{j+(1/2)}^{(1)}(\zeta)=(2\pi^{-1}\zeta)^{1/2}h_{j}^{(1)}(\zeta),\quad\zeta\in{\mathbb{C}}\backslash\{0\},\;j\in{\mathbb{N}}.

(5.22)

Moreover, by
[
1
, 10.1.16]
,

\displaystyle h_{j}^{(1)}(\zeta)=i^{-(j+1)}\zeta^{-1}e^{i\zeta}\sum_{k=0}^{j}\frac{(j+k)!}{k!(j-k)!}(-2i\zeta)^{-k},\quad\zeta\in{\mathbb{C}}\backslash\{0\},\;j\in{\mathbb{N}}.

(5.23)

Upon combining (
5.22
) and (
5.23
), one obtains for odd dimensions
n\geqslant 3
,

\displaystyle H_{(n/2)-1}^{(1)}(\zeta)=H_{[(n-3)/2]+(1/2)}^{(1)}(\zeta)

(5.24)

\displaystyle\quad=2^{1/2}\pi^{-1/2}i^{(1-n)/2}\zeta^{-1/2}e^{i\zeta}\sum_{k=0}^{(n-3)/2}\frac{([(n-3)/2]+k)!}{k!([(n-3)/2]-k)!}(-2i\zeta)^{-k},\quad\zeta\in{\mathbb{C}}\backslash\{0\},

and

\displaystyle H_{(n/2)}^{(1)}(\zeta)=H_{[(n-1)/2]+(1/2)}^{(1)}(\zeta)

\displaystyle\quad=2^{1/2}\pi^{-1/2}i^{-(n+1)/2}\zeta^{-1/2}e^{i\zeta}\sum_{k=0}^{(n-1)/2}\frac{([(n-1)/2]+k)!}{k!([(n-1)/2]-k)!}(-2i\zeta)^{-k},

(5.25)

\displaystyle\hskip 256.0748pt\zeta\in{\mathbb{C}}\backslash\{0\}.

Thus, using the expansions (
5.24
) and (
5.25
) in (
5.15
), one obtains the following expansion for the massless Dirac Green’s function in odd dimensions
n\geqslant 3
:

\displaystyle G_{0}(z;x,y)=i(-1)^{(1-n)/2}2^{-(n+1)/2}\pi^{(1-n)/2}e^{iz|x-y|}I_{N}

\displaystyle\qquad\times\sum_{k=0}^{(n-3)/2}\frac{([(n-3)/2]+k)!}{k!([(n-3)/2]-k)!}(-2)^{-k}(iz)^{-k+[(n-1)/2]}|x-y|^{-k-[(n-1)/2]}

\displaystyle\quad+i(-1)^{(1-n)/2}2^{-(n+1)/2}\pi^{(1-n)/2}e^{iz|x-y|}\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|}

(5.26)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\sum_{k=0}^{(n-1)/2}\frac{([(n-1)/2]+k)!}{k!([(n-1)/2]-k)!}(-2)^{-k}(iz)^{-k+[(n-1)/2]}|x-y|^{-k-[(n-1)/2]},

\displaystyle\hskip 214.81839ptx,y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n},\;x\neq y,\;z\in{\mathbb{C}}\backslash{\mathbb{R}}.

Introducing the power series for the exponential in (
5.3
) and reordering the series to combine like powers of
iz
, one obtains

\displaystyle G_{0}(z;x,y)=(-1)^{(3-n)/2}2^{-(n+1)/2}\pi^{(1-n)/2}z|x-y|\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}d_{j}(iz)^{j}|x-y|^{-(n-1-j)}I_{N}

\displaystyle\quad+i(-1)^{(1-n)/2}2^{-(n+1)/2}\pi^{(1-n)/2}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}d_{j}^{\prime}(iz)^{j}|x-y|^{-(n-1-j)}\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|},

\displaystyle\hskip 199.16928ptx,y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n},\;x\neq y,\;z\in{\mathbb{C}}\backslash{\mathbb{R}},

(5.27)

where for each
j\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}
, the numerical coefficients
d_{j}
and
d_{j}^{\prime}
are given by

\displaystyle d_{j}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k=0\\
k\geqslant[(n-3)/2]-j\end{subarray}}^{(n-3)/2}\frac{([(n-3)/2]+k)!}{k!([(n-3)/2]-k)!}(-2)^{-k}\frac{1}{(j+k-[(n-3)/2])!},

(5.28)

\displaystyle d_{j}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k=0\\
k\geqslant[(n-1)/2]-j\end{subarray}}^{(n-1)/2}\frac{([(n-1)/2]+k)!}{k!([(n-1)/2]-k)!}(-2)^{-k}\frac{1}{(j+k-[(n-1)/2])!},\quad j\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}.

(5.29)

In odd dimensions
n\geqslant 5
, certain of the coefficients
d_{j}
and
d_{j}^{\prime}
in (
5.28
) and (
5.29
) vanish based on the following combinatorial identity:

Proposition
(
[
95
, Lemma 3.3]
).
If
m\in{\mathbb{N}}
and

c_{j}:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k=0\\
k\geqslant m-j\end{subarray}}^{m}\frac{(m+k)!}{k!(m-k)!}(-2)^{-k}\frac{1}{(k+j-m)!},\quad j\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0},

(5.30)

then
c_{j}=0
for
j=1,3,\ldots,2m-1
.

Applying this proposition with
m=(n-3)/2
and
m=(n-1)/2
, one infers that for
n\geqslant 5
is odd, the free massless Green’s function
G_{0}(z,\,\cdot\,,\,\cdot\,)
is given by (
5.3
)–(
5.29
) and

\displaystyle d_{j}

\displaystyle=0\quad\text{for all odd $j\in{\mathbb{N}}$ satisfying $1\leqslant j\leqslant n-4$},

(5.31)

\displaystyle d_{j}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=0\quad\text{for all odd $j\in{\mathbb{N}}$ satisfying $1\leqslant j\leqslant n-2$}.

(5.32)

\diamond

Since
H_{0}
has no spectral gap,
\sigma(H_{0})={\mathbb{R}}
, but
h_{0}
has the half-line

(-\infty,0)
in its resolvent set, a comparison of
h_{0}
with the massive free
Dirac operator
H_{0}(m)=H_{0}+m\,\beta
,
m>0
, with spectral gap
(-m,m)
, replacing the energy
z=0
by
z=\pm m
, is quite natural and then exhibits a similar logarithmic blowup behavior as
z\to 0

in dimensions
n=2
.

Returning to our analysis of the resolvent of
H_{0}
, the asymptotic behavior (
B.9
)–(
B.11
)
implies for some
c_{n}\in(0,\infty)
,

\displaystyle\|G_{0}(0+i\,0;x,y)\|_{{\mathcal{B}}({\mathbb{C}}^{N})}\leqslant c_{n}|x-y|^{1-n},\quad x,y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n},\;x\neq y,\;n\in{\mathbb{N}},\;n\geqslant 2,

(5.33)

and for given
R\geqslant 1
,

\displaystyle\|G_{0}(z;x,y)\|_{{\mathcal{B}}({\mathbb{C}}^{N})}\leqslant c_{n,R}(z)e^{-\text{\rm Im}(z)|x-y|}\begin{cases}|x-y|^{1-n},&|x-y|\leqslant 1,\;x\neq y,\\
1,&1\leqslant|x-y|\leqslant R,\\
|x-y|^{(1-n)/2},&|x-y|\geqslant R,\end{cases}

\displaystyle\hskip 132.30513ptz\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}},\;x,y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n},\;x\neq y,\;n\in{\mathbb{N}},\;n\geqslant 2,

(5.34)

for some
c_{n,R}(\,\cdot\,)\in(0,\infty)
continuous and locally bounded on
\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}}
.

For future purposes we now rewrite
G_{0}(z;\,\cdot\,,\,\cdot\,)
as follows:

\displaystyle G_{0}(z;x,y)=i4^{-1}(2\pi)^{(2-n)/2}|x-y|^{2-n}z\,[z|x-y|]^{(n-2)/2}H_{(n-2)/2}^{(1)}(z|x-y|)I_{N}

\displaystyle\qquad-4^{-1}(2\pi)^{(2-n)/2}|x-y|^{1-n}[z|x-y|]^{n/2}H_{n/2}^{(1)}(z|x-y|)\,\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|}

\displaystyle\quad=|x-y|^{1-n}f_{n}(z,x-y),

(5.35)

\displaystyle\hskip 25.60747ptz\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}},\;x,y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n},\;x\neq y,\;n\in{\mathbb{N}},\;n\geqslant 2,

where
f_{n}
is continuous and locally bounded on
\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
, in addition,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|f_{n}(z,x)\|_{{\mathcal{B}}({\mathbb{C}}^{N})}\leqslant c_{n}(z)e^{-\text{\rm Im}(z)|x|}\begin{cases}1,&0\leqslant|x|\leqslant 1,\\
|x|^{(n-1)/2},&|x|\geqslant 1,\end{cases}&\\
z\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}},\;x,y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n},&\end{split}

(5.36)

for some constant
c_{n}(\,\cdot\,)\in(0,\infty)
continuous and locally bounded on

\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}}
. In particular, decomposing
G_{0}(z;\,\cdot\,,\,\cdot\,)
into

\displaystyle G_{0}(z;x,y)

\displaystyle=G_{0}(z;x,y)\chi_{[0,1]}(|x-y|)+G_{0}(z;x,y)\chi_{[1,\infty)}(|x-y|)

\displaystyle=G_{0,<}(z;x-y)+G_{0,>}(z;x-y),

(5.37)

\displaystyle\hskip 17.07164ptz\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}},\;x,y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n},\;x\neq y,\;n\in{\mathbb{N}},\;n\geqslant 2,

where

\displaystyle G_{0,<}(z;x-y)

\displaystyle:=G_{0}(z;x,y)\chi_{[0,1]}(|x-y|),

(5.38)

\displaystyle G_{0,>}(z;x-y)

\displaystyle:=G_{0}(z;x,y)\chi_{[1,\infty)}(|x-y|),

(5.39)

\displaystyle\hskip 17.07164ptz\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}},\;x,y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n},\;x\neq y,\;n\in{\mathbb{N}},\;n\geqslant 2,

one verifies that

\displaystyle\begin{split}|G_{0,>}(z;x-y)_{j,k}|\leqslant\begin{cases}C_{n}|x-y|^{-(n-1)},&z=0,\\
C_{n}(z)|x-y|^{-(n-1)/2},&z\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}},\\
\end{cases}&\\
x,y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n},\;|x-y|\geqslant 1,\;1\leqslant j,k\leqslant N,&\end{split}

(5.40)

for some constants
C_{n},C_{n}(\,\cdot\,)\in(0,\infty)
, in particular,

G_{0,>}(z;\,\cdot\,)\in[L^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N\times N},\quad z\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}},

(5.41)

and that

G_{0,>}(\,\cdot\,;\,\cdot\,)
is continuous on

\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
.

(5.42)

In the next section, we will use the decomposition (
5.37
) to derive trace ideal properties of operators of the type

F_{1}(\,\cdot\,)(H_{0}-zI_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-1}F_{2}(\,\cdot\,)
, employing results of

[
26
, Subsection 5.4]

in the case
n\geqslant 3
. We also derive trace ideal properties of

\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle^{-\delta}(H_{0}-zI_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-1}\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle^{-\delta}

in the case
n\geqslant 2
using a different approach based on a combination of Sobolev’s inequality and complex interpolation.

6.
Trace Ideal Properties of
F_{1}(\,\cdot\,)(H_{0}-zI_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-1}F_{2}(\,\cdot\,)
and

\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle^{-\delta}(H_{0}-zI_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-1}\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle^{-\delta}

In the first part of this section we derive trace ideal properties of operators of the type

F_{1}(\,\cdot\,)(H_{0}-zI_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-1}F_{2}(\,\cdot\,)
, employing results of

[
26
, Subsection 5.4]

in the case
n\geqslant 3
. In the second part of this section we derive trace ideal properties of

\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle^{-\delta}(H_{0}-zI_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-1}\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle^{-\delta}

in the case
n\geqslant 2
by a different approach based on a combination of Sobolev’s inequality and complex interpolation. These two approaches are independent and complement each other.

The considerations (
5.35
)–(
5.41
) readily imply the following facts:

Lemma 6.1
.

Let
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
n\geqslant 2
, and
F,H\in[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N\times N}
. Introducing

R_{0,>,F,H}(z;x,y)=F(x)G_{0,>}(z;x-y)H(y),\quad z\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}},\;x,y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n},

(6.1)

the integral operator
R_{0,>,F,H}(z)
in
L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})
with integral kernel
R_{0,>,F,H}(z;\,\cdot\,,\,\cdot\,)
satisfies

R_{0,>,F,H}(z)\in{\mathcal{B}}_{2}\big([L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}\big),\quad z\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}},

(6.2)

and
R_{0,>,F,H}(\,\cdot\,)
is continuous on
\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}}
with respect to
the
\|\,\cdot\,\|_{{\mathcal{B}}_{2}([L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N})}
-norm.

In particular, this applies to
F,H
satisfying for some constant
C\in(0,\infty)
,

|F_{j,k}|,|H_{j,k}|\leqslant C\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle^{-\delta},\quad\delta>n/2,\;1\leqslant j,k\leqslant N.

(6.3)

Proof.

We apply Theorem
A.2

(iii)
and Lemma
A.4
.

Let
F,H\in[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N\times N}
and
z\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}}
be fixed. To prove (
6.2
), it suffices to show

\big\|R_{0,>,F,H}(z;\,\cdot\,,\,\cdot\,)\big\|_{{\mathcal{B}}_{2}({\mathbb{C}}^{N})}\in L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{2n};d^{n}x\,d^{n}y)

(6.4)

and apply
[
27
, Thm. 11.6]
(in the special case

L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{n};d^{n}x\,d^{n}y)
). To prove (
6.4
) we recall

\|D\|_{{\mathcal{B}}_{2}({\mathbb{C}}^{n})}\leqslant N^{1/2}\|D\|_{{\mathcal{B}}({\mathbb{C}}^{N})},\quad D\in{\mathbb{C}}^{N\times N}.

(6.5)

Then by (
5.41
) and (
6.4
),

\displaystyle\|R_{0,>,F,H}(z;x,y)\|_{{\mathcal{B}}_{2}({\mathbb{C}}^{N})}

\displaystyle\leqslant N^{1/2}\|F(x)\|_{{\mathcal{B}}({\mathbb{C}}^{N})}\|G_{0,>}(z;x-y)\|_{{\mathcal{B}}({\mathbb{C}}^{N})}\|H(y)\|_{{\mathcal{B}}({\mathbb{C}}^{N})}

\displaystyle\leqslant C(z)\|F(x)\|_{{\mathcal{B}}({\mathbb{C}}^{N})}\|H(y)\|_{{\mathcal{B}}({\mathbb{C}}^{N})},\quad x,y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n},

(6.6)

for an appropriate constant
C(z)>0
. Since by hypothesis
F,H\in[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N\times N}
, and hence,

\|F(\,\cdot\,)\|_{{\mathcal{B}}({\mathbb{C}}^{N})}^{2}\leqslant\|F(\,\cdot\,)\|_{{\mathcal{B}}_{2}({\mathbb{C}}^{N})}^{2}=\sum_{j,k=1}^{N}|F_{j,k}(\,\cdot\,)|^{2}\in L^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{n}),

(6.7)

and analogously for
H
, the estimate in (
6.6
) implies (
6.4
).

To prove the continuity claim, let
z,z^{\prime}\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}}
. One computes (cf.
[
27
, Thm. 11.6]
)

\displaystyle\big\|R_{0,>,F,H}(z)-R_{0,>,F,H}(z^{\prime})\big\|_{{\mathcal{B}}_{2}([L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N})}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x\,d^{n}y\,\big\|R_{0,>,F,H}(z;x,y)-R_{0,>,F,H}(z^{\prime};x,y)\big\|_{{\mathcal{B}}_{2}({\mathbb{C}}^{N})}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\leqslant N\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x\,d^{n}y\,\|F(x)\|_{{\mathcal{B}}({\mathbb{C}}^{N})}^{2}\big\|G_{0,>}(z;x-y)-G_{0,>}(z^{\prime};x-y)\big\|_{{\mathcal{B}}({\mathbb{C}}^{N})}^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 102.43008pt\times\|H(y)\|_{{\mathcal{B}}({\mathbb{C}}^{N})}^{2}.

(6.8)

An application of Lebesgue’s dominated convergence theorem, making use of (
5.41
),

F,H\in[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N\times N}
, and the continuity of
G_{0,>}(z;x-y)
with respect to
(z,x-y)

in
{\mathcal{B}}({\mathbb{C}}^{N})
(see (
5.42
)), then yields

\lim_{\begin{subarray}{c}z\to z^{\prime}\\
z,z^{\prime}\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}}\end{subarray}}\big\|R_{0,>,F,H}(z)-R_{0,>,F,H}(z^{\prime})\big\|_{{\mathcal{B}}_{2}([L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N})}=0.

(6.9)

∎

To improve upon Lemma
6.1
, we now recall the following version of Sobolev’s inequality (see, e.g.,
[
156
, Corollary I.14]
).

Theorem 6.2
.

Let
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
\lambda\in(0,n)
,
r,s\in(1,\infty)
,
r^{-1}+s^{-1}+\lambda n^{-1}=2
,
f\in L^{r}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})
,
h\in L^{s}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})
. Then, there
exists
C_{r,s,\lambda,n}\in(0,\infty)
such that

\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x\,d^{n}y\,\frac{|f(x)||h(y)|}{|x-y|^{\lambda}}\leqslant C_{r,s,\lambda,n}\|f\|_{L^{r}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})}\|h\|_{L^{s}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})}.

(6.10)

For subsequent purposes, we also recall some basic facts on
L^{p}
-properties of Riesz potentials (see, e.g.,

[
159
, Sect. V.1]
):

Theorem 6.3
.

Let
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
\alpha\in(0,n)
, and introduce the Riesz potential operator
{\mathcal{R}}_{\alpha,n}
as follows:

\displaystyle\begin{split}&({\mathcal{R}}_{\alpha,n}f)(x)=\big((-\Delta)^{-\alpha/2}f\big)(x)=\gamma(\alpha,n)^{-1}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}y\,|x-y|^{\alpha-n}f(y),\\
&\gamma(\alpha,n)=\pi^{n/2}2^{\alpha}\Gamma(\alpha/2)/\Gamma((n-\alpha)/2),\end{split}

(6.11)

for appropriate functions
f

(
see below
)
.

(i)
Let
p\in[1,\infty)
and
f\in L^{p}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})
. Then the integral
({\mathcal{R}}_{\alpha,n}f)(x)
converges for
(
Lebesgue
)
a.e.
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
.

(ii)
Let
1<p<q<\infty
,
q^{-1}=p^{-1}-\alpha n^{-1}
, and
f\in L^{p}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})
. Then there
exists
C_{p,q,\alpha,n}\in(0,\infty)
such that

\|{\mathcal{R}}_{\alpha,n}f\|_{L^{q}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})}\leqslant C_{p,q,\alpha,n}\|f\|_{L^{p}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})}.

(6.12)

We also note the
\beta
function-type integral (cf.
[
159
, p. 118]
),

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}y\,|e_{k}-y|^{\alpha-n}|y|^{\beta-n}=\gamma(\alpha,n)\gamma(\beta,n)/\gamma(\alpha+\beta,n),

\displaystyle 0<\alpha<n,\;0<\beta<n,\;\alpha+\beta<n,

(6.13)

\displaystyle e_{k}=(0,\dots,\underbrace{1}_{k},\dots,0),\;1\leqslant k\leqslant n.

and the Riesz composition formula (see
[
53
, Sects. 3.1, 3.2]
),

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}y\,|x_{1}-y|^{\alpha-n}|y-x_{2}|^{\beta-n}=[\gamma(\alpha,n)\gamma(\beta,n)/\gamma(\alpha+\beta,n)]|x_{1}-x_{2}|^{\alpha+\beta-n},

\displaystyle\hskip 108.12054pt0<\alpha<n,\;0<\beta<n,\;\alpha+\beta<n,\;x_{1},x_{2}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}.

(6.14)

For later use in Section
10
, we recall the following estimate taken from
[
63
, Lemma 6.3]
.

Lemma 6.4
.

Let
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
and
x_{1},x_{2}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
. If
\alpha,\beta\in(0,n]
,
\varepsilon,\gamma\in(0,\infty)
,
with
n+\gamma\geqslant\alpha+\beta
, and
\alpha+\beta\neq n
, then

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}y\,|x_{1}-y|^{\alpha-n}\langle y\rangle^{-\gamma-\varepsilon}|y-x_{2}|^{\beta-n}\\
&\quad\leqslant C_{n,\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\varepsilon}\begin{cases}|x_{1}-x_{2}|^{-\max\{0,n-\alpha-\beta\}},&|x_{1}-x_{2}|\leqslant 1,\\
|x_{1}-x_{2}|^{-\min\{n-\alpha,n-\beta,n+\gamma-\alpha-\beta\}},&|x_{1}-x_{2}|\geqslant 1,\end{cases}\end{split}

(6.15)

where
C_{n,\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\varepsilon}\in(0,\infty)
is an
x_{1},x_{2}
-independent constant.

Returning to
G_{0,>}(z;\,\cdot\,)
, we next combine the estimate (
5.40
) with Theorem
6.2
,
rather than just using the
L^{\infty}
-bound (
5.41
) on
G_{0,>}(z;\,\cdot\,)
in Lemma
6.1
,
yielding a considerable improvement of Lemma
6.1
.

Theorem 6.5
.

Let
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
n\geqslant 2
.

(i)
Let
z=0
and
F,H\in[L^{4n/(n+\varepsilon)}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N\times N}
for some

\varepsilon>0
. Introducing the integral operator
R_{0,>,F,H}(0)
in

L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})
with integral kernel
R_{0,>,F,H}(0;\,\cdot\,,\,\cdot\,)
as in (
6.1
), then

R_{0,>,F,H}(0)\in{\mathcal{B}}_{2}\big([L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}\big).

(6.16)

In particular, this applies to
F,H
satisfying for some constant
C\in(0,\infty)
,

|F_{j,k}|,|H_{j,k}|\leqslant C\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle^{-\delta},\quad\delta>n/4,\;1\leqslant j,k\leqslant N.

(6.17)

(ii)
Let
z\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}}
and
F,H\in[L^{4n/(n+1)}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N\times N}
.
Introducing the integral operator
R_{0,>,F,H}(z)
in
L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})
with
integral kernel
R_{0,>,F,H}(z;\,\cdot\,,\,\cdot\,)
as in (
6.1
), then

R_{0,>,F,H}(z)\in{\mathcal{B}}_{2}\big([L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}\big),\quad z\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}},

(6.18)

and
R_{0,>,F,H}(\,\cdot\,)
is continuous on
\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}}
with respect to
the
\|\,\cdot\,\|_{{\mathcal{B}}_{2}([L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N})}
-norm.

In particular, this applies to
F,H
satisfying for some constant
C\in(0,\infty)
,

|F_{j,k}|,|H_{j,k}|\leqslant C\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle^{-\delta},\quad\delta>(n+1)/4,\;1\leqslant j,k\leqslant N.

(6.19)

Proof.

Again, we apply Theorem
A.2

(iii)
and Lemma
A.4
.

If
z=0
, then
R_{0,>,F,H}(0;\,\cdot\,,\,\cdot\,)
generates a Hilbert–Schmidt operator in

L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})
upon applying the following modified
z=0
part in estimate (
5.40
),

|G_{0,>}(z;x-y)_{j,k}|\leqslant c_{n,\varepsilon}|x-y|^{-(n-\varepsilon)/2},\quad x,y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n},\;|x-y|\geqslant 1,\;1\leqslant j,k\leqslant N,

(6.20)

for some constants
c_{n,\varepsilon}\in(0,\infty)
, combined with Sobolev’s inequality
in the form

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}xd^{n}y\,\frac{|f(x)|^{2}|h(y)|^{2}}{|x-y|^{n-\varepsilon}}\chi_{[1,\infty)}(|x-y|)\\
&\quad\leqslant C_{n,\varepsilon}\|f^{2}\|_{L^{2n/(n+\varepsilon)}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})}\|h^{2}\|_{L^{2n/(n+\varepsilon)}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})},\end{split}

(6.21)

identifying
r=s=2n/(n+\varepsilon)
,
\lambda=n-\varepsilon
in (
6.10
). One verifies that

\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle^{-\delta}\in L^{4n/(n+\varepsilon)}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})
if
\delta>(n+\varepsilon)/4
,
and, since
\varepsilon>0
can be chosen arbitrarily small, if
\delta>n/4
.

The general case
z\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}}
follows along the same lines using the modified estimate
(
5.40
),

|G_{0,>}(z;x-y)_{j,k}|\leqslant c_{n}|x-y|^{-(n-1)/2},\quad x,y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n},\;|x-y|\geqslant 1,\;1\leqslant j,k\leqslant N,

(6.22)

for some constant
c_{n}\in(0,\infty)
, again combined with Sobolev’s inequality in the form

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}xd^{n}y\,\frac{|f(x)|^{2}|h(y)|^{2}}{|x-y|^{n-1}}\chi_{[1,\infty)}(|x-y|)\\
&\quad\leqslant C_{n}\|f^{2}\|_{L^{2n/(n+1)}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})}\|h^{2}\|_{L^{2n/(n+1)}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})},\end{split}

(6.23)

identifying
r=s=2n/(n+1)
,
\lambda=n-1
in (
6.10
). One verifies that

\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle^{-\delta}\in L^{4n/(n+1)}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})
if
\delta>(n+1)/4
.

Finally, continuity of
R_{0,>,F,H}(\,\cdot\,)
on
\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}}
with respect to the

\|\,\cdot\,\|_{{\mathcal{B}}_{2}([L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N})}
-norm follows again by applying Lebesgue’s dominated convergence theorem as in the proof of Lemma
6.1
.
∎

We recall the following interesting results of McOwen
[
119
]
and
Nirenberg–Walker
[
124
]
, which provide necessary and sufficient conditions for the boundedness of
certain classes of integral operators in
L^{p}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})
:

Theorem 6.6
.

Let
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
c,d\in{\mathbb{R}}
,
c+d>0
,
p\in(1,\infty)
, and
p^{\prime}=p/(p-1)
. Then the following items

(i)
and
(ii)
hold.

(i)
Consider

K_{c,d}(x,y)=|x|^{-c}|x-y|^{(c+d)-n}|y|^{-d},\quad x,y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n},\;x\neq x^{\prime},

(6.24)

then the integral operator
K_{c,d}
in
L^{p}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})
with integral kernel

K_{c,d}(\,\cdot\,,\,\cdot\,)

in (
6.24
) is bounded if and only if
c<n/p
and
d<n/p^{\prime}
.

(ii)
Consider

\widetilde{K}_{c,d}(x,y)=(1+|x|)^{-c}|x-y|^{(c+d)-n}(1+|y|)^{-d},\quad x,y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n},\;x\neq x^{\prime},

(6.25)

then the integral operator
\widetilde{K}_{c,d}
in
L^{p}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})
with integral kernel

\widetilde{K}_{c,d}(\,\cdot\,,\,\cdot\,)

in (
6.25
) is bounded if and only if
c<n/p
and
d<n/p^{\prime}
.

This result implies the following fact.

Theorem 6.7
.

Let
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
n\geqslant 2
.

(i)
Then the integral operator
R_{0,\delta}
in
[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}
with associated integral kernel
R_{0,\delta}(\,\cdot\,,\,\cdot\,)
bounded entrywise by

|R_{0,\delta}(\,\cdot\,,\,\cdot\,)_{j,k}|\leqslant C\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle^{-\delta}|G_{0}(0;\,\cdot\,,\,\cdot\,)_{j,k}|\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle^{-\delta},\quad\delta\geqslant 1/2,\;1\leqslant j,k\leqslant N,

(6.26)

for some
C\in(0,\infty)
, is bounded,

R_{0,\delta}\in{\mathcal{B}}\big([L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}\big).

(6.27)

(ii)
The integral operator
R_{0,\delta}(z)
in
[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}
, with associated integral
kernel
R_{0,\delta}(z;\,\cdot\,,\,\cdot\,)
bounded entrywise by

\displaystyle\begin{split}|R_{0,\delta}(z;\,\cdot\,,\,\cdot\,)_{j,k}|\leqslant C\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle^{-\delta}|G_{0}(z;\,\cdot\,,\,\cdot\,)_{j,k}|\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle^{-\delta},&\\
\delta\geqslant(n+1)/4,\;z\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}},\;1\leqslant j,k\leqslant N,\end{split}

(6.28)

for some
C\in(0,\infty)
, is bounded,

R_{0,\delta}(z)\in{\mathcal{B}}\big([L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}\big),\quad z\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}}.

(6.29)

(iii)
The integral operator
R_{0,\alpha,\beta}(z)
in
[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}
, with associated integral
kernel
R_{0,\alpha,\beta}(z;\,\cdot\,,\,\cdot\,)
bounded entrywise by

\displaystyle\begin{split}|R_{0,\alpha,\beta}(z;\,\cdot\,,\,\cdot\,)_{j,k}|\leqslant C\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle^{-\alpha}|G_{0}(z;\,\cdot\,,\,\cdot\,)_{j,k}|\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle^{-\beta},&\\
\alpha\geqslant(n-1)/2,\;\beta\geqslant 1,\;z\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}},\;1\leqslant j,k\leqslant N,\end{split}

(6.30)

for some
C\in(0,\infty)
, is bounded,

R_{0,\alpha,\beta}(z)\in{\mathcal{B}}\big([L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}\big),\quad z\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}}.

(6.31)

Proof.

(i)
The inclusion (
6.27
) is then an immediate consequence of (
5.16
) and hence the estimate

|G_{0}(0;x,y)_{j,k}|\leqslant C|x-y|^{1-n}
,
x,y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
,
x\neq y
,
1\leqslant j,k\leqslant N
,
Theorem
6.6
, choosing
c=d=1/2
in (
6.24
), and an application of
Theorem
A.2

(i)
and Lemma
A.4
.

(ii)
To prove the inclusion (
6.29
) we employ the estimates (
B.9
)–(
B.11
)
(cf. also (
5.34
)) to obtain

\displaystyle|G_{0}(z;x,y)_{j,k}|

\displaystyle\leqslant C(z)|x-y|^{1-n}\chi_{[0,1]}(|x-y|)

\displaystyle\quad+D(z)|x-y|^{(1-n)/2}\chi_{[1,\infty)}(|x-y|),

(6.32)

\displaystyle\hskip-5.69054ptz\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}},\;x,y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n},\;x\neq y,\;1\leqslant j,k\leqslant N,

for some
C,D(z)\in(0,\infty)
, and apply Theorems
6.6
(parts
(i)
or
(ii)
) and
A.2

(i)
(cf. also
Lemma
A.4
) to both terms on the right-hand sides of
(
6.32
). The part
0\leqslant|x-y|\leqslant 1
in (
6.32
) leads to
\delta\geqslant 1/2
, whereas the
part
|x-y|\geqslant 1
in (
6.32
) yields
\delta\geqslant(n+1)/4
, implying (
6.29
).

(iii)
Again we employ the estimate (
6.32
) and argue as in item
(ii)
for the part where
|x-y|\leqslant 1
.
For the part
|x-y|\geqslant 1
in (
6.32
) one employs Theorem
6.6
with
c=\alpha\geqslant(n-1)/2
and

d=\beta\geqslant 1
.
∎

Given the fact (
6.18
), we will now focus on
G_{0,<}(z;\,\cdot\,)
,
z\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}}
. We begin by recalling that
a(-i\nabla)
is a convolution-type operator of the form,

(a(-i\nabla)\varphi)(x):=\big(\big({\mathcal{F}}^{-1}a\big)*\varphi\big)(x)=(2\pi)^{-n/2}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}y\,a^{\vee}(x-y)\varphi(y),\quad\varphi\in{\mathcal{S}}({\mathbb{R}}^{n}),

(6.33)

given
a\in{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})
,
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
. We are particularly interested in operators of the type

b(Q)a(-i\nabla),

(6.34)

with
Q
abbreviating the operator of multiplication by the independent variable
x
, such that
b(Q)a(-i\nabla)
extends to a bounded, actually, compact operator in
L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})
, in fact, we will focus on its membership in certain Schatten–von Neumann classes. The prime result we will employ from
[
26
, Subsection 5.4]
in this context can be formulated as follows:

Theorem 6.8

(
[
26
, Subsection 5.4, p. 103]
)
.

Let
2<r<s
, and suppose that
a,\psi\in L^{r}_{weak}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})
,

\psi>0
,
\|\psi\|_{L^{r}_{weak}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})}\leqslant 1
, and let
b
be a measurable function such that
b/\psi\in L^{s}_{weak}\big({\mathbb{R}}^{n};\psi^{r}d^{n}x\big)
. Then

b(Q)a(-i\nabla)\in{\mathcal{B}}_{s}\big(L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})\big),

(6.35)

and for some constant
C(r,s)\in(0,\infty)
,

\|b(Q)a(-i\nabla)\|_{{\mathcal{B}}_{s}(L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n}))}\leqslant C(r,s)\|a\|_{L^{r}_{weak}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})}\bigg(\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x\,b(x)^{s}\psi(x)^{r-s}\bigg)^{1/s}.

(6.36)

Next, we recall (with
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
n\geqslant 2
) that

\displaystyle|\,\cdot\,|^{\gamma-n}\in L^{n/(n-\gamma)}_{weak}({\mathbb{R}}^{n}),\quad 0<\gamma<n,

(6.37)

\displaystyle\big(\big(|\,\cdot\,|^{\gamma-n}\big)^{\wedge}\big)(\xi)=c_{n}|\xi|^{-\gamma},\quad\big(|\,\cdot\,|^{\gamma-n}\big)^{\wedge}\in L^{n/\gamma}_{weak}({\mathbb{R}}^{n}),\quad 0<\gamma<n,

(6.38)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&G_{0,<}(z;\,\cdot\,)_{j,k}=|\,\cdot\,|^{1-n}f_{n}(z,\,\cdot\,)_{j,k}\chi_{[0,1]}(|\,\cdot\,|)\in L^{n/(n-1)}_{weak}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})\subset L^{p}({\mathbb{R}}^{n}),\\
&\hskip 166.44879pt1\leqslant j,k\leqslant N,\;p\in(0,n/(n-1)),\end{split}

(6.39)

where
f_{n}(z,\,\cdot\,)
and
G_{0,<}(z;\,\cdot\,)
are defined by (
5.36
) and (
5.38
), respectively. In addition, we recall the Hausdorff–Young inequality and its weak
analog (cf., e.g.,
[
139
, p. 32]
),

\displaystyle\big\|f^{\wedge}\big\|_{L^{p/(p-1)}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})}\leqslant D_{p,n}\|f\|_{L^{p}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})},\quad p\in[1,2],

(6.40)

\displaystyle\big\|f^{\wedge}\big\|_{L^{p/(p-1)}_{weak}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})}\leqslant C_{p,n}\|f\|_{L^{p}_{weak}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})},\quad p\in(1,2),

(6.41)

noting that
p/(p-1)\in(2,\infty)
if
p\in(1,2)
. In particular, since

p=n/(n-1)\in(1,2)
for
n\geqslant 3
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}[G_{0,<}(z;\,\cdot\,)_{j,k}]^{\wedge}=[|\,\cdot\,|^{1-n}f_{n}(z,\,\cdot\,)_{j,k}\chi_{[0,1]}(|\,\cdot\,|)]^{\wedge}\in L^{n}_{weak}({\mathbb{R}}^{n}),&\\
1\leqslant j,k\leqslant N,\;n\in{\mathbb{N}},\;n\geqslant 3.&\end{split}

(6.42)

Thus, an application of Theorem
6.8
and yields the following result.

Theorem 6.9
.

Let
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
n\geqslant 3
,
\varepsilon>0
, and assume that for some
q\in(n,\infty)
,
F\in[L^{q}({\mathbb{R}}^{n};(1+|x|)^{(q-n)(1+\varepsilon)}d^{n}x)]^{N\times N}
. Then,

F(Q)G_{0,<}(z;-i\nabla)^{\wedge}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{q}\big([L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}\big),\quad z\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}},

(6.43)

and

\displaystyle\big\|F(Q)_{j,\ell}[G_{0,<}(z;-i\nabla)_{\ell,k}]^{\wedge}\big\|_{{\mathcal{B}}_{q}(L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n}))}\leqslant C_{\ell,k}(n,q)\big\|[G_{0,<}(z,\,\cdot\,)_{\ell,k}]^{\wedge}\big\|_{L^{n}_{weak}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})}

\displaystyle\quad\times\bigg(\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}(1+|x|)^{(q-n)(1+\varepsilon)}d^{n}x\,|F(x)_{j,\ell}|^{q}\bigg)^{1/q},\quad 1\leqslant j,k,\ell\leqslant N.

(6.44)

In addition, the operator
F(Q)G_{0,<}(z;-i\nabla)^{\wedge}
is continuous with respect to

z\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}}
in the
{\mathcal{B}}_{q}\big([L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}\big)
-norm.

Proof.

Pick
k,\ell\in\{1,\dots,N\}
.
Introducing
\psi(x)=c(1+|x|)^{-1-\varepsilon}
,
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
,

c>0
, one infers that
\psi\in L^{n}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})
and choosing
c=\big\|(1+|\,\cdot\,|)^{-1-\varepsilon}\big\|_{L^{n}_{weak}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})}^{-1}
yields
\|\psi\|_{L^{n}_{weak}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})}\leqslant 1
. Identifying

a^{\vee}
in Theorem
6.8
and

G_{0,<}(z;\,\cdot\,)_{\ell,k}\in L^{n/(n-1)}_{weak}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})\subset L^{p}({\mathbb{R}}^{n}),\quad p\in(0,n/(n-1)),

(6.45)

the inclusion (
6.42
) yields

a=\big(a^{\vee}\big)^{\wedge}=[G_{0,<}(z;\,\cdot\,)_{\ell,k}]^{\wedge}\in L^{n}_{weak}({\mathbb{R}}^{n}).

(6.46)

Identifying
b
in Theorem
6.8
with
F_{j,\ell}\in L^{q}({\mathbb{R}}^{n};(1+|x|)^{(q-n)(1+\varepsilon)}d^{n}x)
,
r
with
n\geqslant 3
, and
s
with
q>n
, one verifies that
F_{j,\ell}/\psi\in L^{n}\big({\mathbb{R}}^{n};\psi^{n}d^{n}x\big)
and all hypotheses of Theorem
6.8
are satisfied. Hence, the inclusion (
6.43
) and the estimate (
6.44
) hold.

Continuity of
F(Q)G_{0,<}(z;-i\nabla)^{\wedge}
with respect to
z\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}}
in the

{\mathcal{B}}_{q}\big([L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}\big)
-norm follows from the estimate (
6.44
) (replacing

G_{0,<}(z;-i\nabla)
by
G_{0,<}(z;-i\nabla)-G_{0,<}(z^{\prime};-i\nabla)
),
the explicit structure of
G_{0,<}(z;\,\cdot\,)
in (
5.35
), (
5.37
),
and the continuity of
f_{n}(\,\cdot\,,\,\cdot\,)
, combined with the weak Hausdorff–Young
inequality (
6.41
) and the fact that
L^{q}({\mathbb{R}}^{n};d\rho)\subset L^{q}_{weak}({\mathbb{R}}^{n};d\rho)
with

\|g\|_{L^{q}_{weak}({\mathbb{R}}^{n};d\rho)}\leqslant\|g\|_{L^{p}({\mathbb{R}}^{n};d\rho)}
,
g\in L^{q}({\mathbb{R}}^{n};d\rho)
,

q\in(0,\infty)
. Indeed, with
C_{n}\in(0,\infty)
some universal constant,

\displaystyle\big\|[G_{0,<}(z;\,\cdot\,)_{\ell,k}]^{\wedge}-G_{0,<}(z^{\prime};\,\cdot\,)_{\ell,k}]^{\wedge}\big\|_{L^{n}_{weak}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})}

\displaystyle\quad\leqslant C_{n}\|G_{0,<}(z;\,\cdot\,)_{\ell,k}-G_{0,<}(z^{\prime};\,\cdot\,)_{\ell,k}\|_{L^{n/(n-1)}_{weak}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})}

\displaystyle\quad\leqslant C_{n}\|G_{0,<}(z;\,\cdot\,)_{\ell,k}-G_{0,<}(z^{\prime};\,\cdot\,)_{\ell,k}\|_{L^{n/(n-1)}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})}\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}z\to z^{\prime}\\[1.42262pt]
z,z^{\prime}\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}}\end{subarray}}{\longrightarrow}0,

(6.47)

applying the dominated convergence theorem.
∎

A combination of Theorems
6.5
and
6.9
then yields the first principal result of this section, which strengthens a part of Theorem
3.4
(see (
3.47
)) and shows that the Birman–Schwinger operators
\overline{V_{2}(H_{0}-zI_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-1}V_{1}^{*}}
are continuous in the closed upper half-plane in an appropriate Schatten norm, provided that
V_{j}
,
j=1,2
satisfy appropriate boundedness and decay hypotheses.

Theorem 6.10
.

Let
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
n\geqslant 3
,
\varepsilon>0
, and suppose that

F_{1}\in[L^{q}({\mathbb{R}}^{n};(1+|x|)^{(q-n)(1+\varepsilon)}d^{n}x)]^{N\times N}\,\text{ for some $q\in(n,\infty)$,}

(6.48)

and

F_{\ell}\in[L^{4n/(n+1)}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N\times N}\cap[L^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N\times N},\quad\ell=1,2.

(6.49)

Introducing

R_{0,F_{1},F_{2}}(z,x,y)=F_{1}(x)G_{0}(z;x,y)F_{2}(y),\quad z\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}},\;x,y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n},\;x\neq y,

(6.50)

the integral operator
R_{0,F_{1},F_{2}}(z)
in
[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}
with integral kernel

R_{0,F_{1},F_{2}}(z,\,\cdot\,,\,\cdot\,)
satisfies

R_{0,F_{1},F_{2}}(z)\in{\mathcal{B}}_{q}\big([L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}\big),\quad z\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}},

(6.51)

and
R_{0,F_{1},F_{2}}(\,\cdot\,)
is continuous on
\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}}
with respect to
the
\|\,\cdot\,\|_{{\mathcal{B}}_{q}([L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N})}
-norm.

In particular, this applies to
F_{\ell}
,
\ell=1,2
, satisfying for some constant
C\in(0,\infty)
,

|F_{\ell,j,k}|\leqslant C\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle^{-\delta},\quad\delta>(n+1)/4,\;1\leqslant j,k\leqslant N,\;\ell=1,2.

(6.52)

Proof.

Recalling the decomposition (
5.37
),

G_{0}(z;x,y)=G_{0,<}(z;x-y)+G_{0,>}(z;x-y),\quad x,y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n},\;x\neq y,

(6.53)

(now employed for
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
n\geqslant 3
), one applies Theorem
6.5
to
G_{0,>}(z;\,\cdot\,)
and Theorem
6.9
to
G_{0,<}(z;\,\cdot\,)
.

One readily verifies that if
\delta>(n+1)/4
, then
\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle^{-\delta}I_{N}
satisfies the conditions assumed on
F_{\ell}
,
\ell=1,2
,
∎

This handles the case
n\geqslant 3
. Due to the condition
s>r>2
(in the underlying concrete case,
r=n
) in Theorem
6.8
, the special case
n=2
in connection with
G_{0,<}(z;\,\cdot\,)
does not subordinate to these techniques and hence will be treated using an alternative approach next (which actually applies to all dimensions
n\geqslant 2
). While Theorem
6.10
only handles the case

n\geqslant 3
, it has the advantage that it yields continuity of
R_{0,F_{1},F_{2}}(\,\cdot\,)
on
\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}}
(and hence, particularly along the real axis) in a straightforward manner.

To describe an alternative approach to this circle of ideas, we start with some preparatory material
on the following trace ideal interpolation result, see, for instance,
[
84
, Theorem III.13.1]
,
[
185
, Theorem 0.2.6]
(see also
[
79
]
,
[
85
, Theorem III.5.1]
).

Theorem 6.11
.

Let
p_{j}\in[1,\infty)\cup\{\infty\}
,
\Sigma=\{\zeta\in{\mathbb{C}}\,|\,\text{\rm Re}(\zeta)\in(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})\}
,

\xi_{j}\in{\mathbb{R}}
,
\xi_{1}<\xi_{2}
,
j=1,2
. Suppose that
A(\zeta)\in{\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{H}})
,
\zeta\in\overline{\Sigma}

and that
A(\,\cdot\,)
is analytic on
\Sigma
, continuous up to
\partial\Sigma
, and that

\|A(\,\cdot\,)\|_{{\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{H}})}
is bounded on
\overline{\Sigma}
. Assume that for some
C_{j}\in(0,\infty)
,

\sup_{\eta\in{\mathbb{R}}}\|A(\xi_{j}+i\eta)\|_{{\mathcal{B}}_{p_{j}}({\mathcal{H}})}\leqslant C_{j},\quad j=1,2.

(6.54)

Then

A(\zeta)\in{\mathcal{B}}_{p(\text{\rm Re}(\zeta))}({\mathcal{H}}),\quad\frac{1}{p(\text{\rm Re}(\zeta))}=\frac{1}{p_{1}}+\frac{\text{\rm Re}(\zeta)-\xi_{1}}{\xi_{2}-\xi_{1}}\bigg[\frac{1}{p_{2}}-\frac{1}{p_{1}}\bigg],\quad\zeta\in\overline{\Sigma},

(6.55)

and

\|A(\zeta)\|_{{\mathcal{B}}_{p(\text{\rm Re}(\zeta))}({\mathcal{H}})}\leqslant C_{1}^{(\xi_{2}-\text{\rm Re}(\zeta))/(\xi_{2}-\xi_{1})}C_{2}^{(\text{\rm Re}(\zeta)-\xi_{1})/(\xi_{2}-\xi_{1})},\quad\zeta\in\overline{\Sigma}.

(6.56)

In case
p_{j}=\infty
,
{\mathcal{B}}_{\infty}({\mathcal{H}})
can be replaced by
{\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{H}})
.

A combination of Theorems
6.6
,
6.2
and
6.11
then yields the following fact
(cf.
[
82
]
).

Theorem 6.12
.

Let
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
n\geqslant 2
,
0<2\gamma<n
,
\delta>\gamma
, and suppose that

T_{\gamma,\delta}
is an integral operator in
L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})
whose integral kernel

T_{\gamma,\delta}(\,\cdot\,,\,\cdot\,)
satisfies the estimate

|T_{\gamma,\delta}(x,y)|\leqslant C\langle x\rangle^{-\delta}|x-y|^{2\gamma-n}\langle y\rangle^{-\delta},\quad x,y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n},\;x\neq y

(6.57)

for some
C\in(0,\infty)
. Then,

T_{\gamma,\delta}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{p}\big(L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})\big),\quad p>n/(2\gamma),\;p\geqslant 2,

(6.58)

and

\displaystyle\|T_{\gamma,\delta}\|_{{\mathcal{B}}_{n/(2\gamma-\varepsilon)}(L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n}))}

\displaystyle\leqslant\sup_{\eta\in{\mathbb{R}}}\big[\|T_{\gamma,\delta}(-2\gamma+\varepsilon+i\eta)\|_{{\mathcal{B}}(L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n}))}\big]^{2[-2\gamma+(n/2)+\varepsilon]/n}

\displaystyle\quad\times\sup_{\eta\in{\mathbb{R}}}\big[\|T_{\gamma,\delta}(-2\gamma+(n/2)+\varepsilon+i\eta)\|_{{\mathcal{B}}_{2}(L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n}))}\big]^{2(2\gamma-\varepsilon)/n}

(6.59)

for
0<\varepsilon
sufficiently small.

Proof.

Following the idea behind Yafaev’s proof of
[
185
, Lemma 0.13.4]
, we
introduce the analytic family of integral operators

T_{\gamma,\delta}(\,\cdot\,)
in
L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})
generated by the integral kernel

T_{\gamma,\delta}(\zeta;x,y)=T_{\gamma,\delta}(x,y)\,\langle x\rangle^{-(\zeta/2)}|x-y|^{\zeta}\langle y\rangle^{-(\zeta/2)},\quad x,y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n},\;x\neq y,

(6.60)

noting
T_{\gamma,\delta}(0)=T_{\gamma,\delta}
.

By Theorems
6.6

(ii)
and
A.2

(i)
(for
N=1
),

T_{\gamma,\delta}(\zeta)\in{\mathcal{B}}\big(L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})\big),\quad 0<\text{\rm Re}(\zeta)+2\gamma<n,\;\delta\geqslant\gamma.

(6.61)

To check the Hilbert–Schmidt property of
T_{\gamma,\delta}(\,\cdot\,)
one
estimates for the square of
|T_{\gamma,\delta}(\,\cdot\,;\,\cdot\,,\,\cdot\,)|
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}|T_{\gamma,\delta}(\zeta;x,y)|^{2}\leqslant\langle x\rangle^{-2\delta-\text{\rm Re}(\zeta)}|x-y|^{2\text{\rm Re}(\zeta)+4\gamma-2n}\langle y\rangle^{-2\delta-\text{\rm Re}(\zeta)},&\\
x,y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n},\;x\neq y,&\end{split}

(6.62)

and hence one can apply Theorem
6.2
upon identifying

\lambda=2n-4\gamma-2\text{\rm Re}(z)
,
r=s=n/[\text{\rm Re}(\zeta)+2\gamma]
, and

f=h=\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle^{-[2\delta+\text{\rm Re}(\zeta)]}
, to verify that

0<\lambda<n
translates into
n/2<\text{\rm Re}(\zeta)+2\gamma<n
, and

f\in L^{r}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})
holds with
r\in(1,2)
if
\delta>\gamma
. Hence,

T_{\gamma,\delta}(\zeta)\in{\mathcal{B}}_{2}\big(L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})\big),\quad n/2<\text{\rm Re}(\zeta)+2\gamma<n,\;\delta>\gamma.

(6.63)

It remains to interpolate between the
{\mathcal{B}}\big(L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})\big)
and

{\mathcal{B}}_{2}\big(L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})\big)
property, employing Theorem
6.11
as follows.
Choosing
0<\varepsilon
sufficiently small, one identifies

\xi_{1}=-2\gamma+\varepsilon
,
\xi_{2}=-2\gamma+(n/2)+\varepsilon
,

p_{1}=\infty
,
p_{2}=2
, and hence obtains

p(\text{\rm Re}(\zeta))=n/[\text{\rm Re}(\zeta)+2\gamma-\varepsilon],

(6.64)

in particular,
p(0)>n/(2\gamma)
(and of course,
p(0)\geqslant 2
). Since
\varepsilon
may be taken arbitrarily small, (
6.58
) follows from (
6.64
) and (
6.59
) is a direct consequence of
(
6.56
).
∎

One notes that while subordination in general only applies to
{\mathcal{B}}_{p}
-ideals with
p
even (see the
discussion in
[
158
, p. 24 and Addendum E]
), the use of complex interpolation in Theorem
6.12
(and the focus on bounded and Hilbert–Schmidt operators) permits one to avoid this restriction.

Combining Theorems
6.2
,
6.6

(ii)
,
6.11
, and
6.12
then yields the second principal result of this section.

Theorem 6.13
.

Let
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
n\geqslant 2
. Then the integral operator
R_{0,\delta}
in
[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}
with integral kernel
R_{0,\delta}(\,\cdot\,,\,\cdot\,)
permitting the entrywise bound

|R_{0,\delta}(\,\cdot\,,\,\cdot\,)_{j,k}|\leqslant C\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle^{-\delta}|G_{0}(0+i\,0;\,\cdot\,,\,\cdot\,)_{j,k}|\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle^{-\delta},\quad\delta>1/2,\;1\leqslant j,k\leqslant N,

(6.65)

for some
C\in(0,\infty)
, satisfies

R_{0,\delta}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{p}\big([L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}\big),\quad p>n.

(6.66)

In a similar fashion, the integral operator
R_{0,\delta}(z)
in
[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}
with integral
kernel
R_{0,\delta}(z;\,\cdot\,,\,\cdot\,)
permitting the entrywise bound

\displaystyle\begin{split}|R_{0,\delta}(z;\,\cdot\,,\,\cdot\,)_{j,k}|\leqslant C\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle^{-\delta}|G_{0}(z;\,\cdot\,,\,\cdot\,)_{j,k}|\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle^{-\delta},&\\
z\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}},\;\delta>(n+1)/4,\;1\leqslant j,k\leqslant N,&\end{split}

(6.67)

for some
C\in(0,\infty)
, satisfies

R_{0,\delta}(z)\in{\mathcal{B}}_{p}\big([L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}\big),\quad p>n,\;z\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}}.

(6.68)

Proof.

We will apply the fact (
A.5
).

The inclusion (
6.66
) is immediate from (
5.16
) (employing the elementary estimate

|G_{0}(0;x,y)_{j,k}|\leqslant C|x-y|^{1-n}
,
x,y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
,
x\neq y
,
1\leqslant j,k\leqslant N
)
and Theorem
6.12
(with
\gamma=1/2
).

To prove the inclusion (
6.68
) we again employ the estimate (
6.32
).
An application of Theorem
6.12
to both terms in (
6.32
), then yields for the part
where
0\leqslant|x-y|\leqslant 1
that
\gamma=1/2
and hence
\delta>1/2
and
p>n
. Similarly,
for the part where
|x-y|\geqslant 1
one infers
\gamma=(n+1)/4
and hence
\delta>(n+1)/4
and

p>2n/(n+1)
,
p\geqslant 2
, and thus one concludes
\delta>(n+1)/4
and
p>n
.
∎

Remark 6.14
.

Continuity of
R_{0,\delta}(\,\cdot\,)
on
\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}}
with respect to
the
\|\,\cdot\,\|_{{\mathcal{B}}_{p}([L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N})}
-norm,
p>n
, appears to be more difficult to
prove within this complex interpolation approach. In this context the first approach described in this
section is by far simpler to apply, but in turn it is restricted to the case
n\geqslant 3
. In fact, as recorded
in Theorem
6.10
, if
\delta>(n+1)/4
, then
\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle^{-\delta}I_{N}
satisfies the conditions assumed on
F_{\ell}
,
\ell=1,2
, in Theorem
6.10
, implying the fact,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\text{For $n\geqslant 3$, $\delta>(n+1)/4$, $R_{0,\delta}(\,\cdot\,)$ is continuous on $\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}}$}\\
&\quad\text{with respect to
the $\|\,\cdot\,\|_{{\mathcal{B}}_{p}([L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N})}$-norm, $p>n$.}\end{split}

(6.69)

Fortunately, the remaining case
n=2
can easily be handled directly as we demonstrate next.

\diamond

Corollary 6.15
.

Let
n=2
,
\delta>3/4
, and
z_{0}\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}}
. Then
R_{0,\delta}(\,\cdot\,)
, as introduced in Theorem
6.13
, satisfies

[R_{0,\delta}(z_{1})-R_{0,\delta}(z_{2})]\in{\mathcal{B}}_{2}\big([L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})]^{N}\big),\quad z_{j}\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}},\;j=1,2,

(6.70)

and

\lim_{\begin{subarray}{c}z\to z_{0}\\
z\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}}\backslash\{z_{0}\}\end{subarray}}\|R_{0,\delta}(z)-R_{0,\delta}(z_{0})\|_{{\mathcal{B}}_{2}([L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})]^{N})}=0.

(6.71)

Proof.

Once more we will apply the fact (
A.5
) (for
p=2
).

By Theorem
6.5

(ii)
it suffices to focus on
G_{0,<}(z;\,\cdot\,)
. The explicit formula
(see (
C.26
), (
C.27
)),

\displaystyle G_{0}(z;x,y)

\displaystyle=i4^{-1}z\,H_{0}^{(1)}(z|x-y|)I_{N}

\displaystyle\quad-4^{-1}|x-y|^{-1}[z|x-y|]H_{1}^{(1)}(z|x-y|)\,\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|},

(6.72)

\displaystyle\hskip 118.07875ptz\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}},\;x,y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2},\;x\neq y,

together with the
z\to 0,\,z\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}}\backslash\{0\}
limit (
C.28
), then permit the following conclusions: Only if
z\to 0
(
z\in{\mathbb{C}}_{+}\backslash\{0\}
) and/or if
|x-y|\to 0
, can
G_{0}(z;x,y)
develop a singularity which then is of the form
\text{\rm ln}(z|x-y|)
and
|x-y|^{-1}
. (In all other circumstances
G_{0}
is continuous
on
{\mathbb{C}}_{+}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
.) However, the
|x-y|^{-1}
-singularity is
z
-independent and hence drops out in differences of the form
R_{0,\delta}(z_{1})-R_{0,\delta}(z_{2})
,
z_{j}\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}}
,
j=1,2
. Thus one can safely ignore the
|x-y|^{-1}
-singularity. Thus, this only leaves the
\text{\rm ln}(z_{j}|x-y|)
-singularity,
j=1,2
, when considering
G_{0,<}(z_{1};x,y)-G_{0,<}(z_{2};x,y)
. This then yields the estimate (see also (
5.35
) and (
5.36
)),

\displaystyle|G_{0,<}(z;x,y)_{j,k}-G_{0,<}(z_{0};x,y)_{j,k}|

\displaystyle\quad\leqslant\begin{cases}C[|z|+|z_{0}|]|\text{\rm ln}(|x-y|)|+D(z,z_{0}),&z,z_{0}\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}}\big\backslash\{0\},\\
C|z||\text{\rm ln}(|x-y|)|+D(z),&z\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}}\big\backslash\{0\},\;z_{0}=0,\end{cases}

(6.73)

\displaystyle\hskip 105.2751ptx,y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2},\;0<|x-y|\leqslant 1,\;1\leqslant j,k\leqslant N,

with
C\in(0,\infty)
, and
D(\,\cdot\,,z_{0}),D(\,\cdot\,)\in(0,\infty)
continuous and locally
bounded on
\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}}
. The logarithmic-type integral kernel in (
6.72
) can now be handled
as in
[
185
, Proposition 7.1.17]
(upon multiplying
R_{0}(z)
by a factor of
z
if
z_{0}=0
, and choosing
|z|=1
in equation (7.1.25) in
[
185
, p. 272]
), implying the asserted Hilbert–Schmidt property. Alternatively, one can use the very rough estimate (for some
c_{0}\in(0,\infty)
)

|\text{\rm ln}(|x-y|)|^{2}\leqslant c_{0}|x-y|^{-1},\quad 0<|x-y|\leqslant 1,

(6.74)

and apply the Sobolev inequality in the form of (
6.10
) with
n=2
,
\lambda=1
,
r=s=4/3
, recalling that

\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle^{-2\delta}\in L^{4/3}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})
if
\delta>3/4
.
∎

Combining Theorems
6.10
,
6.13
, Remark
6.14
, and Corollary
6.15
, we finally
summarize the principal results of this section as follows:

Theorem 6.16
.

Let
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
n\geqslant 2
and consider the integral operator

R_{0,\delta}(z)
in
[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}
with integral
kernel
R_{0,\delta}(z;\,\cdot\,,\,\cdot\,)
permitting the entrywise bound

\displaystyle\begin{split}|R_{0,\delta}(z;\,\cdot\,,\,\cdot\,)_{j,k}|\leqslant C\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle^{-\delta}|G_{0}(z;\,\cdot\,,\,\cdot\,)_{j,k}|\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle^{-\delta},&\\
z\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}},\;\delta>(n+1)/4,\;1\leqslant j,k\leqslant N,&\end{split}

(6.75)

for some
C\in(0,\infty)
. Then
R_{0,\delta}(z)
satisfies

R_{0,\delta}(z)\in{\mathcal{B}}_{p}\big([L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}\big),\quad p>n,\;z\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}}.

(6.76)

Moreover, if
n\geqslant 3
,
\delta>(n+1)/4
, then
R_{0,\delta}(\,\cdot\,)
is continuous on

\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}}
with respect to the

\|\,\cdot\,\|_{{\mathcal{B}}_{p}([L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N})}
-norm for
p>n
. Finally, if
n=2
,
\delta>3/4
, then

[R_{0,\delta}(z_{1})-R_{0,\delta}(z_{2})]\in{\mathcal{B}}_{2}\big([L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})]^{N}\big),\quad z_{j}\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}},\;j=1,2,

(6.77)

and

\lim_{\begin{subarray}{c}z\to z_{0}\\
z\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}}\backslash\{z_{0}\}\end{subarray}}\|R_{0,\delta}(z)-R_{0,\delta}(z_{0})\|_{{\mathcal{B}}_{2}([L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})]^{N})}=0.

(6.78)

7.
Powers of Resolvents and Trace Ideals

We now introduce the following considerably strengthened set of assumptions on the short-range potential
V
:

Hypothesis 7.1
.

Let
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
and suppose that
V
satisfies for some constant
C\in(0,\infty)
and
\rho\in(n,\infty)
,

V\in[L^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N\times N},\quad|V_{\ell,m}(x)|\leqslant C\langle x\rangle^{-\rho}\,\text{ for a.e.~$x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}$, $1\leqslant\ell,m\leqslant N$.}

(7.1)

Given Hypothesis
7.1
, the principal purpose of this section is to prove that for
k\geqslant n
,

\big[(H-zI_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-k}-(H_{0}-zI_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-k}\big]\in{\mathcal{B}}_{1}\big([L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}\big),\quad z\in{\mathbb{C}}\backslash{\mathbb{R}}.

(7.2)

Here
H=H_{0}+V
is defined according to (
3.4
), but we do not assume self-adjointness of the
N\times N
matrix
V
in this section.

The following arguments are straightforward generalizations of the arguments in
[
184
]
in the
three-dimensional context
n=3
. We start with a study of
H_{0}
:

Lemma 7.2
.

Let
r\in(0,\infty)
,
k\in{\mathbb{N}}
, and define
p(r,k):=n/\min\{r,k\}
. If
p>p(r,k)
,
p\geqslant 1
, then

\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle^{-r}(H_{0}-zI_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-k}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{p}\big([L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}\big),\quad z\in{\mathbb{C}}\backslash{\mathbb{R}}.

(7.3)

In particular, choosing
r=n+\varepsilon
for some
\varepsilon>0
,
k=n+1
, then

p(n+\varepsilon,n+1)<1
, and hence

\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle^{-n-\varepsilon}(H_{0}-zI_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-n-1}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{1}\big([L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}\big),\quad z\in{\mathbb{C}}\backslash{\mathbb{R}}.

(7.4)

Proof.

Since

\displaystyle\big(H_{0}-zI_{[L_{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}}\big)^{-k}

\displaystyle=\big(H_{0}+zI_{[L_{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}}\big)^{k}\big(H_{0}^{2}-z^{2}I_{[L_{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}}\big)^{-k}

\displaystyle=\big(h_{0}-z^{2}I_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})}\big)^{-k/2}I_{N}

\displaystyle\quad\times\big(H_{0}+zI_{[L_{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}}\big)^{k}\big(H_{0}^{2}-z^{2}I_{[L_{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}}\big)^{-k/2},

(7.5)

\displaystyle\hskip 174.98427ptz\in{\mathbb{C}}\backslash{\mathbb{R}},

and the operator
\big(H_{0}+zI_{[L_{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}}\big)^{k}\big(H_{0}^{2}-z^{2}I_{[L_{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}}\big)^{-k/2}
is bounded, it is sufficient to prove the assertion for the operator
\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle^{-r}\big(h_{0}-z^{2}I_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})}\big)^{-k/2}
. The latter follows from
[
185
, Lemma 4.3, p. 145]
.
∎

Turning from
H_{0}
to
H=H_{0}+V
then yields the following result.

Lemma 7.3
.

Assume that
V\in[L^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N\times N}
, let
r\in(0,\infty)
,
k\in{\mathbb{N}}
, and define
p(r,k):=n/\min\{r,k\}
. If
p>p(r,k)
,
p\geqslant 1
, then

\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle^{-r}(H-zI_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-k}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{p}\big([L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}\big),\quad z\in{\mathbb{C}}\backslash{\mathbb{R}}.

(7.6)

In particular, choosing
r=n+\varepsilon
for some
\varepsilon>0
,
k=n+1
, then

p(n+\varepsilon,n+1)<1
, and hence

\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle^{-n-\varepsilon}(H-zI_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-n-1}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{1}\big([L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}\big),\quad z\in{\mathbb{C}}\backslash{\mathbb{R}}.

(7.7)

Proof.

Let
z\in{\mathbb{C}}\backslash{\mathbb{R}}
and
r\in(0,\infty)
. The proof employs induction on
k\in{\mathbb{N}}
. In the base case,
k=1
, one writes

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle^{-r}(H-zI_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-1}\\
&\quad=\big[\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle^{-r}(H_{0}-zI_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-1}\big]\big[(H_{0}-zI_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})(H-zI_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-1}\big].\end{split}

(7.8)

The first factor on the right-hand side in (
7.8
) belongs to
{\mathcal{B}}_{p}\big([L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}\big)
for
p>p(r,1)
,
p\geqslant 1
by Lemma
7.2
. Since the second factor on the right-hand side in (
7.8
) is a bounded operator, (
7.6
) holds with
k=1
.

Suppose that (
7.6
) holds for
k\in{\mathbb{N}}
. Multiplying throughout the commutator identity

(H-zI_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle^{-r}-\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle^{-r}(H-zI_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})=[H_{0},\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle^{-r}]

(7.9)

from the left by
(H-zI_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-1}
and right by
(H-zI_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-k-1}
, one obtains

\displaystyle\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle^{-r}(H-zI_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-k-1}=(H-zI_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-1}\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle^{-r}(H-zI_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-k}

\displaystyle\quad+(H-zI_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-1}[H_{0},\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle^{-r}](H-zI_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-k-1}.

(7.10)

One has

\displaystyle(H-zI_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-1}\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle^{-r}(H-zI_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-k}

(7.11)

\displaystyle\quad=\big[(H-zI_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-1}\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle^{-r(k+1)^{-1}}\big]\big[\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle^{-kr(k+1)^{-1}}(H-zI_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-k}\big].

Now, by the base case,

(H-zI_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-1}\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle^{-r(k+1)^{-1}}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{p}\big([L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}\big),\quad p>p\big(r(k+1)^{-1},1\big),

(7.12)

and by the induction step

\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle^{-kr(k+1)^{-1}}(H-zI_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-k}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{p}\big([L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}\big),\quad p>p\big(kr(k+1)^{-1},k\big).

(7.13)

Therefore, the product on the right-hand side in (
7.11
) belongs to the trace ideal
{\mathcal{B}}_{p}\big([L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}\big)
for
p\geqslant 1
, with

p^{-1}<p\big(r(k+1)^{-1},1\big)^{-1}+p\big(kr(k+1)^{-1},k\big)^{-1}.

(7.14)

To compute the right-hand side of (
7.14
), one distinguishes the two possible cases:

(i)

r(k+1)^{-1}<1
or
(ii)

r(k+1)^{-1}\geqslant 1
.

In case
(i)
,
r<k+1
, and

\displaystyle p\big(kr(k+1)^{-1},k\big)

\displaystyle=n(k+1)r^{-1},

(7.15)

\displaystyle p\big(kr(k+1)^{-1},k\big)^{-1}

\displaystyle=n(k+1)r^{-1}k^{-1}.

(7.16)

Hence, the right-hand side of (
7.14
) equals

n^{-1}(k+1)^{-1}r+n^{-1}(k+1)^{-1}kr=n^{-1}r=n^{-1}\min\{r,k+1\}=p(r,k+1)^{-1},

(7.17)

so the right-hand side in (
7.11
) belongs to
{\mathcal{B}}_{p}\big([L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}\big)
for all indices
p>p(r,k+1)
.

In case
(ii)
,
r\geqslant k+1
, and

\displaystyle p\big(kr(k+1)^{-1},k\big)

\displaystyle=n,

(7.18)

\displaystyle p\big(kr(k+1)^{-1},k\big)^{-1}

\displaystyle=nk^{-1}.

(7.19)

Hence the right-hand side of (
7.14
) equals

n^{-1}(k+1)=p(r,k+1)^{-1},

(7.20)

so the right-hand side of (
7.11
), and hence the first term on the right-hand side in (
7.10
), belongs to
{\mathcal{B}}_{p}\big([L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}\big)
for all indices
p>p(r,k+1)
. To treat the second term in (
7.10
), one uses

[H_{0},\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle^{-r}]=V_{0},

(7.21)

where

V_{0}(x)=-r\langle x\rangle^{-(r+2)}(\alpha\cdot x),\quad x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n},

(7.22)

so that

\|V_{0}(x)\|_{{\mathcal{B}}({\mathbb{C}}^{N})}\leqslant C\langle x\rangle^{-(r+1)},\quad x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n},

(7.23)

for an
x
-independent constant
C>0
. Thus, the second term on the right-hand side in (
7.10
) belongs to
{\mathcal{B}}_{p}\big([L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}\big)
for all indices
p>p(r,k+1)
by the same argument used to treat the first term.
∎

Given these preparations, the principal result of this section reads as follows.

Theorem 7.4
.

Let
k\in{\mathbb{N}}
with
k\geqslant n
and suppose that
V
satisfies Hypothesis
7.1
. Then

\big[(H-zI_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-k}-(H_{0}-zI_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-k}\big]\in{\mathcal{B}}_{1}\big([L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}\big),\quad z\in{\mathbb{C}}\backslash{\mathbb{R}}.

(7.24)

Proof.

Let
k\geqslant n
. By the first resolvent equation,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&(H-zI_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-1}-(H_{0}-zI_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-1}\\
&\quad=-(H-zI_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-1}V(H_{0}-zI_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-1},\quad z\in{\mathbb{C}}\backslash{\mathbb{R}}.\end{split}

(7.25)

Differentiation of (
7.25
) with respect to
z
yields

\displaystyle\begin{split}&(H-zI_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-k}-(H_{0}-zI_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-k}\\
&\quad=-\sum_{j=1}^{k}(H-zI_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-j}V(H_{0}-zI_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{j-k-1},\quad z\in{\mathbb{C}}\backslash{\mathbb{R}}.\end{split}

(7.26)

From this point on, let
z\in{\mathbb{C}}\backslash{\mathbb{R}}
be fixed and write

\displaystyle(H-zI_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-j}V(H_{0}-zI_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{j-k-1}

\displaystyle\quad=\big[(H-zI_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-j}\langle x\rangle^{-j\rho(k+1)^{-1}}\big]\big[\langle x\rangle^{\rho}V\big]

(7.27)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\big[\langle x\rangle^{-(k+1-j)\rho(k+1)^{-1}}(H_{0}-zI_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{j-k-1}\big],\quad j\in{\mathbb{N}},\;1\leqslant j\leqslant k.

By Lemma
7.3
, for a fixed
j\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
1\leqslant j\leqslant k
,

(H-zI_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-j}\langle x\rangle^{-j\rho(k+1)^{-1}}\in{\mathcal{B}}_{p}\big([L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}\big),\quad p>p\big(j\rho(k+1)^{-1},j\big),

(7.28)

and by Lemma
7.2
,
