one obtains (for some constant
\widetilde{d}_{n}\in(0,\infty)
)

\displaystyle\|\psi_{0}(x)\|_{{\mathbb{C}}^{N}}

\displaystyle\leqslant d_{n}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}y\,|x-y|^{1-n}\langle y\rangle^{-1}\|\phi_{0}(y)\|_{{\mathbb{C}}^{N}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\widetilde{d}_{n}{\mathcal{R}}_{1,n}\big(\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle^{-1}\|\phi_{0}(\,\cdot\,)\|_{{\mathbb{C}}^{N}}\big)(x),\quad x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n},

(10.70)

and hence (
6.12
) implies (for some constant
\widetilde{C}_{p,q,n}\in(0,\infty)
)

\displaystyle\|\psi_{0}\|_{[L^{q}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\widetilde{d}_{n}\big\|{\mathcal{R}}_{1,n}\big(\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle^{-1}\|\phi_{0}(\,\cdot\,)\|_{{\mathbb{C}}^{N}}\big)\big\|_{L^{q}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})}

\displaystyle\leqslant\widetilde{C}_{p,q,n}\big\|\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle^{-1}\|\phi_{0}(\,\cdot\,)\|_{{\mathbb{C}}^{N}}\big\|_{L^{p}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})}

\displaystyle\leqslant\widetilde{C}_{p,q,n}\big\|\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle^{-1}\big\|_{L^{s}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})}\|\|\phi_{0}(\,\cdot\,)\|_{{\mathbb{C}}^{N}}\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{C}_{p,q,n}\big\|\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle^{-1}\big\|_{L^{s}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})}\|\phi_{0}\|_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}},

(10.71)

\displaystyle\hskip-96.73918pt1<p<q<\infty,\;p^{-1}=q^{-1}+n^{-1},\;s=2qn[2n+2q-qn]^{-1}\geqslant 1.

In particular,

p=qn/(n+q),\quad 2n+2q-qn>0.

(10.72)

(a)
The case
n=2
: Then one can choose
q\in(2,\infty)
, hence

p=2q/(q+2)\in(1,2)
, and
s=q>2
. Thus, (
10.71
) and

\big\|\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle^{-1}\big\|_{L^{s}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})}<\infty
imply

\psi_{0}\in[L^{q}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})]^{N},\quad q\in(2,\infty).

(10.73)

Recalling
R_{0,0}(x-y)
in (
10.57
), this implies

\displaystyle\begin{split}-i\alpha\cdot\nabla_{x}R_{0,0}(x-y)=-\Delta_{x}g_{0}(0;x,y)I_{N}=\delta(x-y)I_{N},&\\
x,y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n},\;x\neq y,\;n\geqslant 2,&\end{split}

(10.74)

in the sense of distributions. Here we abused notation a bit and denoted also in the case
n=2
,

g_{0}(0;x,y)=-(2\pi)^{-1}\text{\rm ln}(|x-y|),\quad x,y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2},\;x\neq y,\;n=2.

(10.75)

Thus, one obtains

\displaystyle i\alpha\cdot(\nabla\psi_{0})(x)=-i\alpha\cdot\nabla_{x}[R_{0,0}*(V_{1}^{*}\phi_{0})](x)=-i\alpha\cdot\nabla_{x}[-i(\alpha\cdot\nabla_{x}g_{0}*(V_{1}^{*}\phi_{0})](x)

\displaystyle\quad=[(-\Delta_{x}g_{0}I_{N})*(V_{1}^{*}\phi_{0})](x)=(V_{1}^{*}\phi_{0})(x)\in[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})]^{2},

(10.76)

proving
\nabla\psi_{0}\in[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})]^{2\times 2}
, upon employing the fact that
[\alpha\cdot p]^{2}=I_{N}|p|^{2}
,

p\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
.

To prove that
\psi_{0}\in[L^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})]^{2}
in (
10.60
) and (
10.61
), one applies (
10.68
) to the inequality in (
10.69
), and then employs the condition
\|V_{2}(\,\cdot\,)\|_{{\mathbb{C}}^{2\times 2}}\leqslant C\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle^{-1}
for some constant
C\in(0,\infty)
to obtain

\|\psi_{0}(x)\|_{{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}\leqslant\widetilde{d}_{2}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2}}d^{2}y\,|x-y|^{-1}\langle y\rangle^{-2}\|\psi_{0}(y)\|_{{\mathbb{C}}^{2}},\quad x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2},

(10.77)

where
\widetilde{d}_{2}\in(0,\infty)
is an appropriate
x
-independent constant. By Hölder’s inequality,

\|\psi_{0}(x)\|_{{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}\leqslant\widetilde{d}_{2}\bigg(\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2}}d^{2}y\,|x-y|^{-3/2}\langle y\rangle^{-3}\bigg)^{2/3}\bigg(\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2}}d^{2}y\,\|\psi_{0}(y)\|_{{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}^{3}\bigg)^{1/3},\quad x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}.

(10.78)

The second integral on the right-hand side in (
10.78
) is finite since
\psi_{0}\in[L^{3}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})]^{2}
. Choosing
x_{1}=x
,
\alpha=n-(3/2)
,
\beta=n
,
\gamma=2
, and
\varepsilon=1
in Lemma
6.4
, one infers that

\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2}}d^{2}y\,|x-y|^{-3/2}\langle y\rangle^{-3}\leqslant C_{2,3/2,0,2,1},\quad x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}.

(10.79)

Hence, the containment
\psi_{0}\in[L^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})]^{2}
follows from (
10.78
) and (
10.79
).

(b)
The case
n\geqslant 3
: An application of Theorem
6.6

(ii)
with
c=0
,
d=1
,
p=p^{\prime}=2
, and the inequality
1<n/2
, combined with

\|\phi_{0}(\,\cdot\,)\|_{{\mathbb{C}}^{N}}\in L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})
, yield

\|\psi_{0}(\,\cdot\,)\|_{{\mathbb{C}}^{N}}\in L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})\,\text{ and hence, }\,\psi_{0}\in[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N},\quad n\geqslant 3.

(10.80)

To prove that actually
\psi_{0}\in\dom(H)=[W^{1,2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}
, it suffices to argue as follows:

i\alpha\cdot\nabla\psi_{0}=-V\psi_{0}\in[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}

(10.81)

in the sense of distributions since
V\in[L^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N\times N}
and
\psi_{0}\in[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}
.
Given the fact
\dom(H_{0})=\big[W^{1,2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})\big]^{N}
(cf. (
3.2
)), one concludes

\psi_{0}\in\big[W^{1,2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})\big]^{N},\quad n\geqslant 3.

(10.82)

By (
10.82
) we know that
\psi_{0}\in\big[W^{1,2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})\big]^{N}
. Employing
the fact that
\phi_{0}=V_{2}\psi_{0}
in the first line of (
10.70
), one obtains

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|\psi_{0}(x)\|_{{\mathbb{C}}^{N}}&\leqslant\widetilde{D}_{n}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}y\,|x-y|^{1-n}\langle y\rangle^{-2}\|\psi_{0}(y)\|_{{\mathbb{C}}^{N}}\\
&=D_{n}{\mathcal{R}}_{1,n}\big(\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle^{-2}\|\psi_{0}(\,\cdot\,)\|_{{\mathbb{C}}^{N}}\big)(x),\quad x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n},\end{split}

(10.83)

for some constants
\widetilde{D}_{n},D_{n}\in(0,\infty)
. Thus, as in (
10.71
), (
6.12
) implies for
n\geqslant 3
,

\displaystyle\|\psi_{0}\|_{[L^{q}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}}

\displaystyle\leqslant D_{n}\big\|{\mathcal{R}}_{1,n}\big(\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle^{-2}\|\psi_{0}(\,\cdot\,)\|_{{\mathbb{C}}^{N}}\big)\big\|_{L^{q}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})}

\displaystyle\leqslant\widetilde{D}_{p,q,n}\big\|\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle^{-2}\|\psi_{0}(\,\cdot\,)\|_{{\mathbb{C}}^{N}}\big\|_{L^{p}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})}

\displaystyle\leqslant\widetilde{D}_{p,q,n}\big\|\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle^{-2}\big\|_{L^{s}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})}\|\|\psi_{0}(\,\cdot\,)\|_{{\mathbb{C}}^{N}}\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{D}_{p,q,n}\big\|\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle^{-2}\big\|_{L^{s}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})}\|\psi_{0}\|_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}},

(10.84)

\displaystyle\hskip-78.24507pt1<p<q<\infty,\;p^{-1}=q^{-1}+n^{-1},\;s=2qn[2n+2q-qn]^{-1}\geqslant 1,

for some constant
\widetilde{D}_{p,q,n}\in(0,\infty)
. In particular, one again has
p=qn/(n+q)
and

2n+2q-qn>0
. The latter condition implies
q<2n/(n-2)
. The requirement
p>1
results in
q>n/(n-1)
, and the condition
s\geqslant 1
yields
q\geqslant 2n/(3n-2)
which, however, is superseded by
q>p>1
.
Moreover, the requirement
\big\|\langle\,\cdot\,\rangle^{-2}\big\|_{L^{s}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})}<\infty
yields
q>2n/(n+2)
.
Putting it all together implies (
10.62
).

To prove the containment
\psi\in[L^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})]^{4}
in (
10.62
), one invokes the inequality in (
10.83
) with
n=3
. Indeed, applying Hölder’s inequality (with conjugate exponents
q^{\prime}=27/20
and
q=27/7
) to the integral on the right-hand side of the inequality in (
10.83
), one infers that

\displaystyle\|\psi_{0}(x)\|_{{\mathbb{C}}^{4}}\leqslant d_{3}\bigg(\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}d^{3}y\,|x-y|^{-27/10}\langle y\rangle^{-27/10}\bigg)^{20/27}\bigg(\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}d^{3}y\,\|\psi_{0}(y)\|_{{\mathbb{C}}^{4}}^{2/77}\bigg)^{7/27},

\displaystyle\hskip 275.99164ptx\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}.

(10.85)

The second integral in (
10.85
) is finite since
\psi_{0}\in[L^{27/7}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})]^{4}
, and the first integral in (
10.85
) may be estimated by taking
x_{1}=x
,
\alpha=n-(27/10)
,
\beta=n
,
\gamma=2
, and

\varepsilon=7/10
in Lemma
6.4
,

\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{3}}d^{3}y\,|x-y|^{-\frac{27}{10}}\langle y\rangle^{-\frac{27}{10}}\leqslant C_{3,\frac{27}{10},0,2,7/10},\quad x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}\backslash\{0\}.

(10.86)

Hence, the containment
\psi_{0}\in[L^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{3})]^{4}
follows from (
10.85
) and (
10.86
).

Returning to arbitrary
n\geqslant 2
, we show (following the proof of
[
60
, Lemma 7.4]
) that if
\ker(H)\supsetneqq\{0\}
then also

\ker\big(\big[I_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}}+\overline{V_{2}(H_{0}-(0+i0)I_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-1}V_{1}^{*}}\big]\big)\supsetneqq\{0\}.

(10.87)

Indeed, if
0\neq\psi_{0}\in\ker(H)
, then

\phi_{0}:=V_{2}\psi_{0}=U_{V}V_{1}\psi_{0}\in[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}
and hence

V_{1}^{*}\phi_{0}\in[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}
. Then,
H\psi_{0}=0
yields

i\alpha\cdot\nabla\psi_{0}=V\psi_{0}=V_{1}^{*}V_{2}\psi_{0}=V_{1}^{*}\phi_{0}
.

Thus, applying (
10.57
), (
10.74
)–(
10.75
) once again, one obtains for all
n\geqslant 2
,

\displaystyle-i\alpha\cdot\nabla\big[\psi_{0}+(H_{0}-(0+i0)I_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-1}V_{1}^{*}\phi_{0}\big](x)

\displaystyle\quad=-i[\alpha\cdot\nabla\psi_{0}](x)-i\alpha\cdot\nabla_{x}[R_{0,0}*(V_{1}^{*}\phi_{0})](x)

\displaystyle\quad=-i[\alpha\cdot\nabla\psi_{0}](x)-i\alpha\cdot\nabla_{x}[-i(\alpha\cdot\nabla_{x}g_{0}*(V_{1}^{*}\phi_{0})](x)

\displaystyle\quad=-i[\alpha\cdot\nabla\psi_{0}](x)+(-\Delta_{x}g_{0}I_{N})*(V_{1}^{*}\phi_{0})](x)

\displaystyle\quad=-i[\alpha\cdot\nabla\psi_{0}](x)+(V_{1}^{*}\phi_{0})(x)

\displaystyle\quad=-V(x)\psi_{0}(x)+V(x)\psi_{0}(x)=0.

(10.88)

Consequently,

-i\alpha\cdot\nabla[\psi_{0}+(H_{0}-(0+i0)I_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-1}V_{1}^{*}\phi_{0}]=0,

(10.89)

implying

\psi_{0}+(H_{0}-(0+i0)I_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-1}V_{1}^{*}\phi_{0}=c^{\top},

(10.90)

for some
c\in{\mathbb{C}}^{N}
. Since
\psi_{0}\in[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}
, and by exactly the same arguments employed in
(
10.69
)–(
10.80
), also
R_{0,0}*(V_{1}^{*}\phi_{0})\in[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}
, one concludes that
c=0
and hence

\psi_{0}=-(H_{0}-(0+i0)I_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-1}V_{1}^{*}\phi_{0}.

(10.91)

Thus,
\phi_{0}\neq 0
, and

\displaystyle 0=

\displaystyle V_{2}\psi_{0}+V_{2}(H_{0}-(0+i0)I_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-1}V_{1}^{*}\phi_{0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\big[I_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}}+\overline{V_{2}(H_{0}-(0+i0)I_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-1}V_{1}^{*}}\big]\phi_{0},

(10.92)

that is,

0\neq\phi_{0}\in\ker\big(\big[I_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}}+\overline{V_{2}(H_{0}-(0+i0)I_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-1}V_{1}^{*}}\big]\big).

(10.93)

This concludes the proof.
∎

Recalling results of
[
60
]
, we will revisit the basic elements in the proof of item
(i)
of
Theorem
10.7
in Lemma
10.12
.

Remark 10.8
.

(i)
In physical notation, the zero-energy resonances in Cases
(II)
and
(IV)
for
n=2
correspond to eigenvalues
\pm 1/2
of the spin-orbit operator (cf. the operator
S
in
[
103
]
,
[
105
]
) when

V
is spherically symmetric, see the discussion in
[
60
]
.

(ii)
For basics on the Birman–Schwinger principle in an abstract context, especially, if
0\in\rho(H_{0})
, we refer to
[
78
]
(cf. also
[
23
]
,
[
76
]
) and the extensive literature cited therein.
In the concrete case of Schrödinger operators, relations (
10.66
), (
10.68
) are discussed at length in
[
10
]
,
[
29
]
,
[
30
]
,
[
58
]
,
[
64
]
,
[
68
]
,
[
69
]
,

[
75
]
,
[
95
]
,
[
96
]
,
[
97
]
,
[
98
]
,
[
121
]
,
[
168
]
(see also the list of references quoted therein), and in
[
65
]
,
[
59
]
,
[
60
]
,
[
67
]
,
[
66
]
in the case of (massive and massless) Dirac operators.

(iii)
As mentioned in Remark
5.1

(ii)
, the absence of zero-energy resonances is well-known in the
three-dimensional case
n=3
, see
[
8
]
,
[
16
, Sect. 4.4]
,
[
17
]
,
[

]
,
[

]
,

[
150
]
,
[

]
. In fact, for
n=3
the absence of zero-energy resonances has been shown
under the weaker decay
|V_{j,k}|\leqslant C\langle x\rangle^{-1-\varepsilon}
,
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}
, in
[
8
]
.
The absence of zero-energy resonances for massless Dirac operators in dimensions
n\geqslant 4
as contained in Theorem
10.7

(ii)
appears to have gone unnoticed in the literature and was only recently observed in
[
83
]
.

\diamond

To determine the leading order behavior of

\big[U_{V}I_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}}+\overline{V_{1}(H_{0}-zI_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-1}V_{1}^{*}}\big]^{-1}\,\text{ as $z\to 0$, $z\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}}$,}

(10.94)

in all possible cases discussed in Theorem
10.7
, it is convenient to introduce some more notation:

\displaystyle T(z):=U_{V}I_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}}+V_{1}(H_{0}-zI_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-1}V_{1}^{*},\quad z\in{\mathbb{C}}_{+},

(10.95)

\displaystyle T(\lambda):=U_{V}I_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}}+\overline{V_{1}(H_{0}-(\lambda+i0)I_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-1}V_{1}^{*}},\quad\lambda\in{\mathbb{R}}.

(10.96)

Next, we split
P_{0}
in (
10.40
) according to all possible cases in Theorem
10.7
as follows: If
n=2
, we write

\displaystyle P_{0}=P_{0,1}\oplus P_{0,2},

(10.97)

where
P_{0,1}
represents case
(II)
,
P_{0,2}
represents case
(III)
, and if
P_{0,1}
and
P_{0,2}
are both nonzero,
P_{0}
represents case
(IV)
. Similarly, if
n\geqslant 3
,
P_{0}\neq 0
represents case
(II)
. (Again, we remark that we will
discuss in Lemma
10.12

(i)
that
\dim(\ran(P_{0,1}))\leqslant 2
.)

In the following we denote the integral operators in
[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}
generated by the integral
kernels
R_{j,k}(\,\cdot\,,\,\cdot\,)
in (
10.57
)–(
10.59
) by
R_{j,k}
,
j,k\in\{0,1\}
. In particular,

T(0)=U_{V}I_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}}+\overline{V_{1}R_{0,0}V_{1}^{*}}.

(10.98)

In order to study asymptotics as
z\to 0
of the Birman–Schwinger-type operators, we strengthen Hypothesis
10.5
as follows.

Hypothesis 10.9
.

Let
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
n\geqslant 2
, and
\varepsilon>0
. Assume the a.e. self-adjoint matrix-valued potential

V=\{V_{\ell,m}\}_{1\leqslant\ell,m\leqslant N}
satisfies for some fixed
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
,
C\in(0,\infty)
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&V\in[L^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N\times N},\\
&|V_{\ell,m}(x)|\leqslant C\langle x\rangle^{-n(1+\varepsilon)}\,\text{ for a.e.~$x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}$, $1\leqslant\ell,m\leqslant N$.}\end{split}

(10.99)

In accordance with the factorization based on the polar decomposition of
V
discussed in (
10.32
) we
suppose that

V=V_{1}^{*}V_{2}=|V|^{1/2}U_{V}|V|^{1/2},\,\text{ where }\,V_{1}=V_{1}^{*}=|V|^{1/2},\;V_{2}=U_{V}|V|^{1/2}.

(10.100)

We note that, in accordance with (
10.99
), the entries of
V_{1}(\,\cdot\,)
satisfy

|(V_{1})_{\ell,m}(x)|\leqslant\widetilde{C}\langle x\rangle^{-n(1+\varepsilon)/2}\,\text{ for a.e.~$x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}$, $1\leqslant\ell,m\leqslant N$,}

(10.101)

for a constant
\widetilde{C}\in(0,\infty)
.

Lemma 10.10
.

Assume Hypothesis
10.9
. Then
(
cf. (
10.57
)–(
10.59
)
)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\overline{V_{1}(H_{0}-zI_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-1}V_{1}^{*}}\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}z\to 0\\
z\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}}\backslash\{0\}\end{subarray}}{=}\overline{V_{1}R_{0,0}V_{1}^{*}}+z\overline{V_{1}R_{1,0}V_{1}^{*}}\\
&\quad+z\big[-(2\pi)^{-1}\text{\rm ln}(z/2)-(2\pi)^{-1}\gamma_{E-M}+i4^{-1}\big]\delta_{n,2}\overline{V_{1}R_{1,1}V_{1}^{*}}+E(z),\end{split}

(10.102)

where

\|E(z)\|_{{\mathcal{B}}([L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N})}\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}z\to 0\\
z\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}}\backslash\{0\}\end{subarray}}{=}O\big(|z|^{1+\varepsilon}\big)

(10.103)

(
with
0<\varepsilon
taken as in Hypothesis
10.9
)
.

Proof.

In order to prove (
10.102
) and (
10.103
) it suffices to show

\displaystyle\big\|V_{1}(x)G_{0}(z;x,y)V_{1}^{*}(y)-V_{1}(x)R_{0,0}(x-y)V_{1}^{*}(y)-zV_{1}(x)R_{1,0}(x-y)V_{1}^{*}(y)\big\|_{{\mathcal{B}}({\mathbb{C}}^{N})}

\displaystyle\quad\leqslant c_{0}|z|^{1+\varepsilon}k(x,y),\quad x,y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n},\,x\neq y,\,z\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}}\backslash\{0\},|z|\leqslant 1,

(10.104)

for some positive
(z,x,y)
-independent constant
c_{0}
and for some
z
-independent function
k(\,\cdot\,,\,\cdot\,)
which generates a bounded integral operator in
L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})
. In the following we treat separately the cases
(I)

n
odd and
(II)

n
even.

(I)

n
odd. In order to prove (
10.104
), we estimate

\begin{split}G_{0}(z;x,y)-R_{0,0}(x-y)-zR_{1,0}(x-y),&\\
x,y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n},\,x\neq y,\,z\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}}\backslash\{0\},|z|\leqslant 1,&\end{split}

(10.105)

separately in the regimes
|z||x-y|\leqslant 1
and
|z||x-y|>1
.

The expansion (
C.5
) implies

\displaystyle\big\|G_{0}(z;x,y)-R_{0,0}(x-y)-zR_{1,0}(x-y)\big\|_{{\mathcal{B}}({\mathbb{C}}^{N})}

\displaystyle\quad\leqslant c_{1}\big[|z|^{2}+|z|^{2}|z-y|^{3-n}\big]

\displaystyle\quad\leqslant c_{1}\big[|z|^{2}+|z|^{1+\varepsilon}|z-y|^{(2+\varepsilon)-n}\big]

\displaystyle\quad\leqslant c_{1}|z|^{1+\varepsilon}\big[1+|x-y|^{(2+\varepsilon)-n}\big],

(10.106)

\displaystyle x,y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n},\,x\neq y,\,z\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}}\backslash\{0\},|z|\leqslant 1,\,|z||x-y|\leqslant 1,

for some
(z,x,y)
-independent constant
c_{1}\in(0,\infty)
. By Lemma
B.6
,

\displaystyle G_{0}(z;x,y)

\displaystyle\quad=i4^{-1}(2\pi)^{(2-n)/2}|x-y|^{(2-n)/2}z^{n/2}e^{iz|x-y|}\omega_{\frac{n-2}{2}}(z|x-y|)I_{N}

(10.107)

\displaystyle\qquad-4^{-1}(2\pi)^{(2-n)/2}|x-y|^{(2-n)/2}z^{n/2}e^{iz|x-y|}\omega_{\frac{n}{2}}(z|x-y|)\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|},

\displaystyle\hskip 179.25244ptx,y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n},\,x\neq y,\,z\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}}\backslash\{0\},

with

\displaystyle|x-y|^{(2-n)/2}|z|^{n/2}\big|\omega_{\nu}(z|x-y|)\big|

\displaystyle\leqslant c_{2}|x-y|^{1-\frac{n}{2}}|z|^{n/2}(1+|z||x-y|)^{-1/2}

\displaystyle\leqslant c_{2}|z|^{n-1}(1+|z||x-y|)^{-1/2}

\displaystyle\leqslant c_{2}|z|^{2}(1+|z||x-y|)^{-1/2},

(10.108)

\displaystyle\hskip-105.2751ptx,y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n},\,x\neq y,\,z\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}}\backslash\{0\},|z|\leqslant 1,\,|z||x-y|\geqslant 1,

for some
(z,x,y)
-independent constant
c_{2}\in(0,\infty)
. The representation (

) and the estimate (
10.108
) combine to yield

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\big\|G_{0}(z;x,y)\big\|_{{\mathcal{B}}({\mathbb{C}}^{N})}\leqslant c_{3}|z|^{2}(1+|z||x-y|)^{-1/2},\\
&x,y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n},\,x\neq y,\,z\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}}\backslash\{0\},|z|\leqslant 1,\,|z||x-y|\geqslant 1,\end{split}

(10.109)

for some
(z,x,y)
-independent constant
c_{3}\in(0,\infty)
, and it follows that

\displaystyle\big\|G_{0}(z;x,y)-R_{0,0}(x-y)-zR_{1,0}(x-y)\big\|_{{\mathcal{B}}({\mathbb{C}}^{N})}

\displaystyle\quad\leqslant c_{4}\big(|z|^{2}(1+|z||x-y|)^{-1/2}+|x-y|^{1-n}+|z||x-y|^{2-n}\big)

\displaystyle\quad\leqslant c_{4}|z|^{2},

(10.110)

\displaystyle\text{for a.e.}\,x,y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n},\,x\neq y,\,z\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}}\backslash\{0\},|z|\leqslant 1,\,|z||x-y|\geqslant 1,

for some
(z,x,y)
-independent constant

.

By combining (

\displaystyle\big\|G_{0}(z;x,y)-R_{0,0}(x-y)-zR_{1,0}(x-y)\big\|_{{\mathcal{B}}({\mathbb{C}}^{N})}

\displaystyle\quad\leqslant c_{5}\big[|z|^{2}+|z|^{1+\varepsilon}|x-y|^{(2+\varepsilon)-n}\big]

\displaystyle\quad\leqslant c_{5}|z|^{1+\varepsilon}\big[1+|x-y|^{(2+\varepsilon)-n}\big],

(10.111)

\displaystyle\hskip-5.69046pt\text{for a.e.}\,x,y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n},\,x\neq y,\,z\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}}\backslash\{0\},|z|\leqslant 1,

for some
(z,x,y)
-independent constant
c_{5}\in(0,\infty)
. Hence, (
10.104
) holds for some constant

and

\displaystyle k(x,y)

\displaystyle=\langle x\rangle^{-n(1+\varepsilon)/2}\langle y\rangle^{-n(1+\varepsilon)/2}

(10.112)

\displaystyle\quad+[1+|x|]^{-n(1+\varepsilon)/2}|x-y|^{(2+\varepsilon)-n}[1+|y|]^{-n(1+\varepsilon)/2},\quad x,y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n},\,x\neq y.

In deducing the form of

in (

\displaystyle\|V_{1}(x)\|_{{\mathcal{B}}({\mathbb{C}}^{N})}

\displaystyle\leqslant C^{\prime}\langle x\rangle^{-n(1+\varepsilon)/2}

\displaystyle\leqslant C^{\prime\prime}[1+|x|]^{-1-(\varepsilon/2)}\,\text{ for a.e.~$x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}$}

(10.113)

for appropriate

-independent constants

.

The first term on the right-hand side in (

, since

with the choices

.

(II)

n

, so we consider

\displaystyle\big\|G_{0}(z;x,y)-R_{0,0}(x-y)-zR_{1,0}(x-y)\big\|_{{\mathcal{B}}({\mathbb{C}}^{N})}

\displaystyle\quad\leqslant c_{1}\big[|z|^{n-1}+|z|^{1+\varepsilon}|x-y|^{(2+\varepsilon)-n}+|z|^{n-1}|\text{\rm ln}(z|x-y|)|\big]

(10.114)

\displaystyle x,y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n},\,x\neq y,\,z\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}}\backslash\{0\},|z|\leqslant 1,\,|z||x-y|\leqslant 1,

for some
(z,x,y)

(10.115)

\displaystyle\text{for a.e.}\,x,y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n},\,x\neq y,\,z\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}}\backslash\{0\},|z|\leqslant 1,

\displaystyle k(x,y)

Lemma 10.11
.

Assume Hypothesis
10.9
. If

\displaystyle\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}z\to 0\\

\displaystyle\hskip 39.83368pt-z\big[-(2\pi)^{-1}\text{\rm ln}(z/2)-(2\pi)^{-1}\gamma_{E-M}+i4^{-1}\big]

\displaystyle\hskip 39.83368pt\quad\times\delta_{n,2}[T(0)+P_{0}]^{-1}\overline{V_{1}R_{1,1}V_{1}^{*}}[T(0)+P_{0}]^{-1}

(10.117)

\displaystyle\hskip 39.83368pt-z[T(0)+P_{0}]^{-1}\overline{V_{1}R_{1,0}V_{1}^{*}}[T(0)+P_{0}]^{-1}+E_{1}(z),

where

\begin{split}&\|E_{1}(z)\|_{{\mathcal{B}}([L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N})}\\
&\quad\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}z\to 0\\

O\big(|z|\big),&n\geqslant 3\end{cases}\end{split}

(10.118)

(

)
.

Proof.

, Lemma 5.2]
, so we consider

here. By Lemma

,

\displaystyle[T(z)+P_{0}]^{-1}

\displaystyle=\Big[T(0)+P_{0}+z\overline{V_{1}R_{1,0}V_{1}^{*}}+E(z)\Big]^{-1}

\displaystyle=\Big(I_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}}+[T(0)+P_{0}]^{-1}z\overline{V_{1}R_{1,0}V_{1}^{*}}+[T(0)+P_{0}]^{-1}E(z)\Big)^{-1}

\displaystyle\qquad\times[T(0)+P_{0}]^{-1},\quad z\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}}\backslash\{0\},\,0<|z|\ll 1,

(10.119)

where

satisfies (
10.103
). By (

\displaystyle\quad\leqslant c_{1}|z|\Big[V_{1}(x)V_{1}^{*}(y)+[1+|x|]^{(1-n)/2}|x-y|^{-1}[1+|y|]^{(1-n)/2}

\displaystyle\hskip 48.36958pt+[1+|x|]^{-1}|x-y|^{2-n}[1+|y|]^{-1}\Big],

(10.120)

\displaystyle\hskip-5.69046pt\text{for a.e.}\,x,y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n},\,x\neq y,\,z\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}}\backslash\{0\},|z|\leqslant 1,

for some
(z,x,y)
-independent constant

. The kernel

\displaystyle\begin{split}k(x,y)&=V_{1}(x)V_{1}^{*}(y)+[1+|x|]^{(1-n)/2}|x-y|^{-1}[1+|y|]^{(1-n)/2}\\

generates a bounded integral operator in

with

. It follows that

\displaystyle\|T(z)-T(0)\|_{{\mathcal{B}}([L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N})}\leqslant c_{2}|z|,\quad z\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}},\,|z|\leqslant 1,

Lemma 10.12

(
[
60
, Lemmas 5.2, 7.1–7.6]
)
.

Assume Hypothesis
10.9
and
n=2
. The following statements
(i)
–
(iv)
hold.

(i)
If
\phi_{0}\in\ker(T(0))
, then
\phi_{0}=U_{V}V_{1}\psi_{0}
, with
\psi_{0}
a distributional solution of
H\psi_{0}=0
satisfying

\psi_{0}\in[L^{p}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})]^{2}
for all
p\in(2,\infty)\cup\{\infty\}
. Moreover,

\psi_{0}(x)=-i\alpha\cdot x\big[2\pi\langle x\rangle^{2}\big]^{-1}(R_{1,1}V_{1}^{*}\phi_{0})+\psi_{1}(x),

(10.127)

where

(R_{1,1}V_{1}^{*}\phi_{0})=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2}}d^{2}y\,V_{1}^{*}(y)\phi_{0}(y)\,\text{ and }\,\psi_{1}\in[H^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})]^{2}.

(10.128)

In particular,

\psi_{0}\in[H^{1}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})]^{2}\,\text{ if and only if }\,(R_{1,1}V_{1}^{*}\phi_{0})=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2}}d^{2}y\,V_{1}^{*}(y)\phi_{0}(y)=0.

(10.129)

Moreover, the rank of
P_{0}
is at most two plus the dimension of the eigenspace of
H
at energy zero, that is,

\displaystyle P_{0}=P_{0,1}\oplus P_{0,2},\,\text{ with }\,\dim(\ran(P_{0,1}))\leqslant 2

(10.130)

in (
10.97
).

(ii)
If
\psi_{0}\in[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})]^{2}+\bigcap_{p\in(2,\infty)\cup\{\infty\}}[L^{p}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})]^{2}
, then

\phi_{0}=U_{V}V_{1}\psi_{0}\in\ker(T(0)).

(10.131)

(iii)
If
\phi_{0}=U_{V}V_{1}\psi_{0}\in\ker(T(0))
, then
\phi_{0}\in\ran(P_{0,2})
if and only if

\psi_{0}\in[H^{1}(L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{2}))]^{2}
. Thus,
\phi_{0}\in\ran(P_{0,2})
if and only if

\phi_{0}\in\ker(P_{0}V_{1}R_{1,1}V_{1}^{*}P_{0})
.

(iv)
If
n=2
and
\phi_{0}\in\ran(P_{0,2})
, then

(R_{0,0}V_{1}^{*}\phi_{0},R_{0,0}V_{1}^{*}\phi_{0})_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})]^{2}}=(V_{1}^{*}\phi_{0},R_{1,0}V_{1}^{*}\phi_{0})_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})]^{2}}

(10.132)

and

\ker(P_{0,2}V_{1}R_{1,0}V_{1}^{*}P_{0,2})=\{0\}.

(10.133)

Lemma 10.13
.

Assume Hypothesis
10.9
and
n\geqslant 3
. The following statements
(i)
and
(ii)
hold.

(i)

\phi_{0}=U_{V}V_{1}\psi_{0}\in\ker(T(0))

(
i.e.,
\phi_{0}\in\ran(P_{0})
)
if and only if

\psi_{0}\in[H^{1}(L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{2}))]^{2}
. Thus,
\phi_{0}\in\ran(P_{0})
if and only if

\phi_{0}\in\ker(P_{0}V_{1}R_{1,1}V_{1}^{*}P_{0})
.

(ii)

\phi_{0}\in\ran(P_{0})
, then

(R_{0,0}V_{1}^{*}\phi_{0},R_{0,0}V_{1}^{*}\phi_{0})_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}}=(V_{1}^{*}\phi_{0},R_{1,0}V_{1}^{*}\phi_{0})_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}}

(10.134)

and

\ker(P_{0}V_{1}R_{1,0}V_{1}^{*}P_{0})=\{0\}.

(10.135)

Proof.

Item
(i)
is just a rephrasing of the proof of Theorem
10.7
for
n\geqslant 3
. Item
(ii)
is proved exactly along the lines of
[
60
, Lemma 7.6]
; we briefly sketch the argument. By item
(i)
,

\psi_{0}=-R_{0,0}V_{1}^{*}\phi_{0}\in[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{2})]^{2}
and hence, applying Fourier transforms,

\displaystyle(R_{0,0}V_{1}^{*}\phi_{0},R_{0,0}V_{1}^{*}\phi_{0})_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}}=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}p\,|p|^{-4}\big((\alpha\cdot p)(V_{1}^{*}\phi_{0})^{\wedge},(\alpha\cdot p)(V_{1}^{*}\phi_{0})^{\wedge}\big)_{{\mathbb{C}}^{N}}

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}p\,|p|^{-2}\big((V_{1}^{*}\phi_{0})^{\wedge},(V_{1}^{*}\phi_{0})^{\wedge}\big)_{{\mathbb{C}}^{N}}.

(10.136)

On the other hand, employing the monotone convergence theorem,

\displaystyle(V_{1}^{*}\phi_{0},R_{1,0}V_{1}^{*}\phi_{0})_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}}=(V_{1}^{*}\phi_{0},(-\Delta)V_{1}^{*}\phi_{0})_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}}

\displaystyle\quad=\lim_{\varepsilon\downarrow 0}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}p\,\big[|p|^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\big]^{-1}\big((V_{1}^{*}\phi_{0})^{\wedge},(V_{1}^{*}\phi_{0})^{\wedge}\big)_{{\mathbb{C}}^{N}}

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}p\,|p|^{-2}\big((V_{1}^{*}\phi_{0})^{\wedge},(V_{1}^{*}\phi_{0})^{\wedge}\big)_{{\mathbb{C}}^{N}},

(10.137)

proving (
10.134
). Finally, assume that
\phi_{0}\in\ker(P_{0}V_{1}R_{1,0}V_{1}^{*}P_{0})
. Then (
10.134
) yields

\|\psi_{0}\|_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}}=(R_{0,0}V_{1}^{*}\phi_{0},R_{0,0}V_{1}^{*}\phi_{0})_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}}=0,

(10.138)

implying
\psi_{0}=0
and thus
\phi_{0}=U_{V}V_{1}\psi_{0}=0
.
∎

One of the principal results of this section then reads as follows:

Theorem 10.14
.

\displaystyle T(z)^{-1}=\big[U_{V}I_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}}+\overline{V_{1}(H_{0}-zI_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-1}V_{1}^{*}}\big]^{-1}

Proof.

outlined in Lemmas
10.1
,
10.2
, Remarks
10.3
,
10.4
, and our summary in items
(\alpha)
–
(\delta)

following Remark
10.3
. Item
(ii)
for
n\geqslant 3
parallels Cases
(I)
and
(III)
for
n=2
.
∎

Remark 10.15
.

A comparison of the threshold behavior of massless Dirac operators (
[
60
]
, Theorem 9.10
(i)
) and
Schrödinger operators (
[
29
]
,
[
30
]
,
[
98
]
,
[
121
]
) demonstrates that in both situations zero-energy resonances produce a logarithmically weaker singularity of the order
O\big(|z\text{\rm ln}(z)|^{-1}\big)
than the zero-energy eigenvalues which produce the expected
O\big(|z|^{-1}\big)
singularity.

\diamond

Finally, returning to
F_{H,H_{0}}
, we again introduce the strengthened assumptions made in Hypothesis
7.1
and Corollary
4.4

(ii)
.

Hypothesis 10.16
.

Let
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
and suppose that
V=\{V_{\ell,m}\}_{1\leqslant\ell,m\leqslant N}
satisfies for some constants

C\in(0,\infty)
and
\varepsilon>0
,

V\in[L^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N\times N},\quad|V_{\ell,m}(x)|\leqslant C\langle x\rangle^{-n-\varepsilon}\,\text{ for a.e.~$x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}$, $1\leqslant\ell,m\leqslant N$.}

(10.145)

In addition, assume that
V(x)=\{V_{\ell,m}(x)\}_{1\leqslant\ell,m\leqslant N}
is self-adjoint for a.e.
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
.
In accordance with the factorization based on the polar decomposition of
V
discussed in (
10.32
) we
suppose that
V=V_{1}^{*}V_{2}=|V|^{1/2}U_{V}|V|^{1/2}
, where
V_{1}=V_{1}^{*}=|V|^{1/2}
,
V_{2}=U_{V}|V|^{1/2}
.

In addition we assume that
V
satisfies (
4.4
) and (
4.5
)
4
4

4

The first condition in (
4.5
) is superseded by assumption (
10.145
).
.

According to Remark
9.7
, we now use the symmetrized version of the Birman–Schwinger operator in connection
with (
9.20
) and hence write

\displaystyle F_{H,H_{0}}(z)

\displaystyle=\text{\rm ln}\big({\det}_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N},n}\big((H-zI_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})(H_{0}-zI_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-1}\big)\big)

\displaystyle=\text{\rm ln}\big({\det}_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N},n+1}\big(I_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}}+V(H_{0}-zI_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-1}\big)\big)

\displaystyle=\text{\rm ln}\big({\det}_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N},n+1}\big(I_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}}+V_{2}(H_{0}-zI_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-1}V_{1}^{*}\big)\big)

\displaystyle=\text{\rm ln}\big({\det}_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N},n+1}\big(U_{V}\big\{U_{V}I_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}}+V_{1}(H_{0}-zI_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-1}V_{1}^{*}\big\}\big)\big)

\displaystyle=\text{\rm ln}({\det}_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N},n+1}(U_{V}T(z))),\quad z\in{\mathbb{C}}_{\pm},

(10.146)

employing
U_{V}^{2}=I_{N}
.

Next, we briefly recall a few facts on continuous (resp., analytic) logarithms and continuous arguments of complex-valued functions (see
[
12
, p. 40–46]
for details):

If
S\subseteq{\mathbb{C}}
and

f\colon S\to{\mathbb{C}}\backslash\{0\}
, then
g
is called a

continuous logarithm
of
f
on
S
if
g
is continuous on
S
and
f(z)=e^{g(z)}
,
z\in S
. Similarly,

\theta\colon S\to{\mathbb{R}}
is called a
continuous argument
of
f
on
S
if
\theta
is continuous on
S
and
f(z)=|f(z)|e^{i\theta(z)}
,
z\in S
.

\bullet
If
g
is a continuous logarithm of
f
on
S
, then
\text{\rm Im}(g)
is a continuous argument of
f
on
S
.

\bullet
If
\theta
is a continuous argument of
f
, then
\text{\rm ln}(|f|)+i\theta
is a continuous logarithm of
f
on
S
.

\bullet
Thus,
f
has a continuous logarithm on
S
if and only if
f
has a continuous argument on
S
.

If
\Omega\subseteq{\mathbb{C}}
is open and
f\colon\Omega\to{\mathbb{C}}\backslash\{0\}
is analytic, then

g\colon\Omega\to{\mathbb{C}}
is called an analytic logarithm of
f
on
\Omega
if
g
is analytic on
\Omega
and

f(z)=e^{g(z)}
,
z\in\Omega
.

\bullet
If
\Omega\subseteq{\mathbb{C}}
is open and starlike and
f\colon\Omega\backslash\{0\}
is analytic, then
f
has an analytic logarithm on
\Omega
.

\bullet
Suppose
\Omega
is open and
f\colon\Omega\to{\mathbb{C}}\backslash\{0\}
is analytic with
g
a continuous logarithm of
f
on
\Omega
. Then
g
is analytic on
\Omega
.

\bullet
Let
a,b,c,d\in{\mathbb{R}}
,
R=\{z=x+iy\,|\,a\leqslant x\leqslant b,\;c\leqslant y\leqslant d\}
, and

f\colon R\to{\mathbb{C}}\backslash\{0\}
continuous. Then
f
has a continuous logarithm on
R
.

\bullet

f\colon{\mathbb{C}}_{+}\to{\mathbb{C}}\backslash\{0\}
analytic,
f\colon\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}}\to{\mathbb{C}}\backslash\{0\}

continuous, then
f
has an analytic logarithm on
{\mathbb{C}}_{+}
which is continuous on
\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}}
. More generally,

f\colon{\mathbb{C}}_{+}\to{\mathbb{C}}\backslash\{0\}
analytic,
f\colon\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}}\to{\mathbb{C}}

continuous, then
f
has an analytic logarithm on
{\mathbb{C}}_{+}
which is continuous at
x_{0}\in{\mathbb{R}}
if

f(x_{0})\neq 0
.

This yields the final and principal result of this section.

Theorem 10.17
.

Let
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
n\geqslant 2
, and assume Hypothesis
10.16
. Then
F_{H,H_{0}}
,
z\in{\mathbb{C}}_{\pm}
, has normal boundary values on
{\mathbb{R}}\backslash\{0\}
. In addition, the boundary values to
{\mathbb{R}}

of the function
\text{\rm Im}(F_{H,H_{0}}(z))
,
z\in{\mathbb{C}}_{+}
, are continuous on
(-\infty,0)\cup(0,\infty)
,

\text{\rm Im}(F_{H,H_{0}}(\lambda+i0))\in C((-\infty,0)\cup(0,\infty)),

(10.147)

and the left and right limits at zero,

\text{\rm Im}(F_{H,H_{0}}(0_{\pm}+i0))=\lim_{\varepsilon\downarrow 0}\text{\rm Im}(F_{H,H_{0}}(\pm\varepsilon+i0)),

(10.148)

exist. In particular, if
0
is a regular point for
H
according to Definition
10.6

(iii)
and Theorem

10.7

(iii)

(
this corresponds to case
(I)
in Theorem
10.7

(i),(ii)
)
, then

\text{\rm Im}(F_{H,H_{0}}(\lambda+i0))\in C({\mathbb{R}}).

(10.149)

Proof.

Applying Theorem
3.4
, Corollary
4.4

(i)
, and Theorem
6.16
, the function

{\det}_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N},n+1}(U_{V}T(z))
,
z\in{\mathbb{C}}_{\pm}
, in
(
10.146
) continuously extends to
z\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{\pm}}\big\backslash\{0\}
and does not vanish there.
In particular,
F_{H,H_{0}}
has normal boundary values on
{\mathbb{R}}\backslash\{0\}
. Moreover, combining
Theorem
6.16
and
[
12
, Theorem 3.1.7]
, and especially, by
[
12
, Exercise 3.2.6 on p. 46]
, the function

\displaystyle{\det}_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N},n+1}(U_{V}T(z))

\displaystyle\quad={\det}_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N},n+1}\big(U_{V}\big\{U_{V}I_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}}+\overline{V_{1}(H_{0}-zI_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-1}V_{1}^{*}}\big\}\big),

(10.150)

\displaystyle\hskip 254.65207ptz\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}}\big\backslash\{0\},

has a continuous argument in any rectangle of the form

\{z=x+iy\,|\,x\in[a,b]\subset(-\infty,0)\cup(0,\infty),\;y\in[0,c]\},\quad c>0,

(10.151)

in
\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}}\big\backslash\{0\}
, proving (
10.147
). Thus
\lambda=0
is the only possible exception to continuity of

\text{\rm Im}(F_{H,H_{0}}(\,\cdot\,+i0))
on
{\mathbb{R}}
.

If
0
is a regular point for
H
, that is, if

\ker\big(\big[I_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}}+\overline{V_{2}(H_{0}-(0+i0)I_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-1}V_{1}^{*}}\big]\big)=\{0\}.

(10.152)

then

{\det}_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N},n+1}(U_{V}T(0))\neq 0

(10.153)

and hence
{\det}_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N},n+1}\big(U_{V}T(z)\big)
has a continuous argument in any
rectangle of the form

\{z=x+iy\,|\,x\in[a,b]\subset{\mathbb{R}},\;y\in[0,c]\},\quad c>0,

(10.154)

proving (
10.149
).

If

\ker\big(\big[I_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}}+\overline{V_{2}(H_{0}-(0+i0)I_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-1}V_{1}^{*}}\big]\big)\supsetneqq\{0\},

(10.155)

denote by
P_{0,+}
the projection onto the (finite-dimensional) eigenspace of the compact
operator
\overline{V_{2}(H_{0}-(0+i0)I_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-1}V_{1}^{*}}
corresponding to the eigenvalue
-1
.
By Lemma
10.1

(iii)
,

\big(I_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}}+\overline{V_{2}(H_{0}-(0+i0)I_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-1}V_{1}^{*}}+P_{0,+}\big)^{-1}\in{\mathcal{B}}\big([L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}\big)

(10.156)

and hence,

{\det}_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N},n+1}\big(I_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}}+\overline{V_{2}(H_{0}-(0+i0)I_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-1}V_{1}^{*}}+P_{0,+}\big)\neq 0

(10.157)

and

\displaystyle{\det}_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N},n+1}\big(I_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}}+\overline{V_{2}(H_{0}-(0+i0)I_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-1}V_{1}^{*}}\big)

\displaystyle\quad={\det}_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N},n+1}\big(I_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}}+\overline{V_{2}(H_{0}-(0+i0)I_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-1}V_{1}^{*}}

\displaystyle\hskip 93.89418pt+P_{0,+}-P_{0,+}\big)

\displaystyle\quad={\det}_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N},n+1}\Big(\big[I_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}}+\overline{V_{2}(H_{0}-(0+i0)I_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-1}V_{1}^{*}}+P_{0,+}\big]

\displaystyle\qquad\times\Big\{I_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}}-\big[I_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}}+\overline{V_{2}(H_{0}-(0+i0)I_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-1}V_{1}^{*}}+P_{0,+}\big]^{-1}\Big\}

\displaystyle\qquad\times P_{0,+}\Big).

(10.158)

Applying Theorem
D.1
in (
10.158
) one obtains

\displaystyle{\det}_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N},n+1}\big(I_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}}+\overline{V_{2}(H_{0}-(0+i0)I_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-1}V_{1}^{*}}\big)

\displaystyle\quad={\det}_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N},n+1}\big(I_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}}+\overline{V_{2}(H_{0}-(0+i0)I_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-1}V_{1}^{*}}+P_{0,+}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\times{\det}_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N},n+1}\Big(I_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt-\big[I_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}}+\overline{V_{2}(H_{0}-(0+i0)I_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-1}V_{1}^{*}}+P_{0,+}\big]^{-1}P_{0,+}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\times e^{{\tr}_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}}(X_{n+1})}

\displaystyle\quad={\det}_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N},n+1}\big(I_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}}+\overline{V_{2}(H_{0}-(0+i0)I_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-1}V_{1}^{*}}+P_{0,+}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\times{\det}_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N},n+1}\Big(I_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}}

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt-P_{0,+}\big[I_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}}+\overline{V_{2}(H_{0}-(0+i0)I_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-1}V_{1}^{*}}+P_{0,+}\big]^{-1}P_{0,+}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\exp({\tr}_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}}(X_{n+1})).

(10.159)

Here
{\tr}_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}}(X_{n+1})
is a finite sum of traces of products of the operators

\big[\overline{V_{2}(H_{0}-(0+i0)I_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-1}V_{1}^{*}}+P_{0,+}\big]

(10.160)

and

-\big[I_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}}+\overline{V_{2}(H_{0}-(0+i0)I_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-1}V_{1}^{*}}+P_{0,+}\big]^{-1}P_{0,+}

(10.161)

of at least
n+1
factors (in various orders) as described in detail in Appendix
D
, in particular,

\exp({\tr}_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}}(X_{n+1}))\neq 0.

(10.162)

Thus, the structure of the zero of the modified Fredholm determinant

{\det}_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N},n+1}\big(I_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}}+\overline{V_{2}(H_{0}-(0+i0)I_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-1}V_{1}^{*}}\big)

(10.163)

as
z\to 0
,
z\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}}\backslash\{0\}
, is identical to the structure of the zero of the modified Fredholm determinant (see, e.g.,
[
158
, Theorem 9.2(d)]
)

\displaystyle{\det}_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N},n+1}\Big(I_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}}

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt-P_{0,+}\big[I_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}}+\overline{V_{2}(H_{0}-(0+i0)I_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-1}V_{1}^{*}}+P_{0,+}\big]^{-1}P_{0,+}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad={\det}_{[P_{0,+}L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}}\Big(P_{0,+}I_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}}

\displaystyle\hskip 36.98866pt-P_{0,+}\big[I_{[P_{0,+}L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}}+\overline{V_{2}(H_{0}-(0+i0)I_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-1}V_{1}^{*}}+P_{0,+}\big]^{-1}P_{0,+}\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\exp{\bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{n}j^{-1}{\tr}_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}}\Big(\Big[P_{0,+}}

\displaystyle\hskip 36.98866pt\times{\big[I_{[P_{0,+}L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}}+\overline{V_{2}(H_{0}-(0+i0)I_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-1}V_{1}^{*}}+P_{0,+}\big]^{-1}P_{0,+}\Big]^{j}\Big)\bigg)},

(10.164)

which now reduces to a finite-dimensional determinant. The behavior of the latter as
z\to 0
,
z\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}}\backslash\{0\}
,

\displaystyle{\det}_{[P_{0,+}L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}}\Big(P_{0,+}I_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}}

\displaystyle\hskip 36.98866pt-P_{0,+}\big[I_{[P_{0,+}L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}}+\overline{V_{2}(H_{0}-zI_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-1}V_{1}^{*}}+P_{0,+}\big]^{-1}P_{0,+}\Big)

(10.165)

in turn, is governed by Lemma
10.11
and hence in leading order
is a polynomial
{\mathcal{P}}(\,\cdot\,,\,\cdot\,)
in the two variables
z\text{\rm ln}(z)
and
z
(the
z\text{\rm ln}(z)
part being absent in odd space dimensions). By (
10.155
),
{\mathcal{P}}(\,\cdot\,,\,\cdot\,)
has no constant term and hence its leading order is of the form

{\mathcal{P}}(z\text{\rm ln}(z),z)\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}z\to 0\\
z\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}}\backslash\{0\}\end{subarray}}{=}cz^{M_{1}}[\text{\rm ln}(z)]^{M_{2}}[1+o(1)],\quad M_{1}\in{\mathbb{N}},\;M_{2}\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0},\;c\in{\mathbb{C}}.

(10.166)

Setting
z=\varepsilon e^{i\varphi}
,
\varphi\in[0,\pi]
, and letting
\varepsilon\downarrow 0
in
(
10.166
) then readily yields

\text{\rm Im}(\text{\rm ln}({\mathcal{P}}(z\text{\rm ln}(z),z)))\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}z\to 0\\
z\in{\mathbb{R}}\backslash\{0\}\end{subarray}}{=}\text{\rm Im}(\text{\rm ln}(c))+\begin{cases}0,&\varphi=0,\\
M_{1}\pi,&\varphi=\pi,\end{cases}

(10.167)

and hence proves the claim (
10.148
).
∎

11.
Analysis of
G_{H,H_{0}}

In this section we analyze
G_{H,H_{0}}(z)
,
z\in{\mathbb{C}}\backslash{\mathbb{R}}
, and its limiting behavior on
{\mathbb{R}}
.

One recalls from (
9.24
) (with
S=H
,
S_{0}=H_{0}
, and
m=n
, cf. Remark
8.4

(iii)
), that
G_{H,H_{0}}
is of the form

\displaystyle\frac{d^{n}}{dz^{n}}G_{H,H_{0}}(z)=\tr_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}}\Bigg(\frac{d^{n-1}}{dz^{n-1}}\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}(-1)^{n-j}(H_{0}-zI_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-1}A(z)^{n-j}\Bigg),

\displaystyle\hskip 270.30118ptz\in{\mathbb{C}}\backslash{\mathbb{R}},

(11.1)

where

A(z)=V(H_{0}-zI_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-1},\quad z\in{\mathbb{C}}\backslash{\mathbb{R}}.

(11.2)

To analyze the trace in (
11.1
), we use multi-indices (see (
9.26
) and (
9.27
)). For each fixed
j\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}
with
0\leqslant j\leqslant n-1
,

\displaystyle\frac{d^{n-1}}{dz^{n-1}}(H_{0}-zI_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-1}A(z)^{n-j}

(11.3)

\displaystyle\quad=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\underline{k}\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}^{n-j+1}\\
|\underline{k}|=n-1\end{subarray}}c_{j,\underline{k}}(H_{0}-zI_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-(k_{1}+1)}\prod_{\ell=2}^{n-j+1}V(H_{0}-zI_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-(k_{\ell}+1)},

for an appropriate set of
z
-independent scalars

c_{j,\underline{k}}\in{\mathbb{R}},\quad\underline{k}\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}^{n-j+1},\;|\underline{k}|=n-1.

(11.4)

Therefore, applying the cyclicity property of the trace, one infers

\displaystyle\frac{d^{n}}{dz^{n}}G_{H,H_{0}}(z)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}(-1)^{n-j}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\underline{k}\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}^{n-j+1}\\
|\underline{k}|=n-1\end{subarray}}c_{j,\underline{k}}

\displaystyle\quad\times\tr_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}}\Bigg(\Bigg[\prod_{\ell=2}^{n-j}V(H_{0}-zI_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-(k_{\ell}+1)}\Bigg]

(11.5)

\displaystyle\hskip 76.82234pt\times V(H_{0}-zI_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-(k_{1}+k_{n-j+1}+2)}\Bigg),

and hence it suffices to analyze the trace

\displaystyle\tr_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}}\Bigg(\Bigg[\prod_{\ell=2}^{n-j}V(H_{0}-zI_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-(k_{\ell}+1)}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\times V(H_{0}-zI_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-(k_{1}+k_{n-j+1}+2)}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{1}\cdots\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{n-j}\,V(x_{1})\frac{1}{{k_{2}}!}\bigg[\frac{\partial^{k_{2}}}{\partial z^{k_{2}}}G_{0}(z;x_{1},x_{2})\bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad\,\cdots\times V(x_{n-j-1})\frac{1}{k_{n-j}!}\bigg[\frac{\partial^{k_{n-j}}}{\partial z^{k_{n-j}}}G_{0}(z;x_{n-j-1},x_{n-j})\bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad\,\cdots\times V(x_{n-j})\frac{1}{(k_{1}+k_{n-j+1}+1)!}\bigg[\frac{\partial^{k_{1}+k_{n-j+1}+1}}{\partial z^{k_{1}+k_{n-j+1}+1}}G_{0}(z;x_{n-j},x_{1})\bigg],

(11.6)

\displaystyle\hskip 95.88564ptz\in{\mathbb{C}}_{+},\;\underline{k}\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}^{n-j+1},\;|\underline{k}|=n-1,\;0\leqslant j\leqslant n-1,

and its properties as
\text{\rm Im}(z)\downarrow 0
.

Here we employed the fact that the integral kernel of

(H_{0}-zI_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-(1+r)}=\frac{1}{r!}\frac{d^{r}}{dz^{r}}(H_{0}-zI_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-1},\quad z\in{\mathbb{C}}_{+},\;r\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0},

(11.7)

is of the form

\frac{1}{r!}\frac{\partial^{r}}{\partial z^{r}}G_{0}(z;x,y),\quad z\in{\mathbb{C}}_{+},\;r\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0},\;x,y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n},\;x\neq y.

(11.8)

Next, we recall the asymptotic relations proved in Appendix
C
and the estimates (
C.33
), (
C.34
). In particular, the estimates (
C.33
) and (
C.34
) as
|z||x-y|\geqslant 1
necessitate the following
strengthening of the estimate (
10.145
) in Hypothesis
10.16
:

Hypothesis 11.1
.

Let
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
and suppose that
V=\{V_{\ell,m}\}_{1\leqslant\ell,m\leqslant N}
satisfies for some constant

C\in(0,\infty)
and
\varepsilon>0
,

V\in[L^{\infty}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N\times N},\quad|V_{\ell,m}(x)|\leqslant C\langle x\rangle^{-n-1-\varepsilon}\,\text{ for a.e.~$x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}$, $1\leqslant\ell,m\leqslant N$.}

(11.9)

This yields the following result.

Theorem 11.2
.

Assume Hypothesis
11.1
,

(i)
Let
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
be odd,
n\geqslant 3
. Then
\frac{d^{n}}{dz^{n}}G_{H,H_{0}}(\,\cdot\,)
is analytic in
{\mathbb{C}}_{+}
and
continuous in
\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}}
.

(ii)
Let
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
be even. Then
\frac{d^{n}}{dz^{n}}G_{H,H_{0}}(\,\cdot\,)
is analytic in
{\mathbb{C}}_{+}
, continuous in
\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}}\backslash\{0\}
. If
n\geqslant 4
, then

\displaystyle\bigg\|\frac{d^{n}}{dz^{n}}G_{H,H_{0}}(\,\cdot\,)\bigg\|_{{\mathcal{B}}({\mathbb{C}}^{N})}\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}z\to 0,\\
z\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}}\backslash\{0\}\end{subarray}}{=}O\big(|z|^{-[n-(n/(n-1))]}\big).

(11.10)

If
n=2
, then for any
\delta\in(0,1)
,

\displaystyle\bigg\|\frac{d^{2}}{dz^{2}}G_{H,H_{0}}(\,\cdot\,)\bigg\|_{{\mathcal{B}}({\mathbb{C}}^{2})}\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}z\to 0,\\
z\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}}\backslash\{0\}\end{subarray}}{=}O\big(|z|^{-(1+\delta)}\big).

(11.11)

Proof.

By Lemma
9.5
it suffices to focus on the boundary values of
\frac{d^{n}}{dz^{n}}G_{H,H_{0}}(z)
as

\text{\rm Im}(z)\downarrow 0
. Utilizing the asymptotic relations
(
C.6
), (
C.12
), (
C.13
), (
C.14
), (
C.18
), (
C.22
), (
C.23
), (
C.24
), (
C.27
), (
C.31
), (
C.32
), and the fact that

\frac{\partial^{k}}{\partial z^{k}}G_{0}(z;x,y)
,
k\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}
,
0\leqslant k\leqslant n
, is continuous in
z\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}}
,

x,y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
,
x\neq y
, the stated continuity of
\frac{d^{n}}{dz^{n}}G_{H,H_{0}}(\,\cdot\,)
in
\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}}
follows once we derive a
z
-independent integrable majorant of the integrand in (
11.6
), appealing to Lebesgue’s dominated convergence theorem.

(i)
Specializing to
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
odd,
n\geqslant 3
, and employing (
11.9
) and (
C.33
), one obtains
from (
11.6
),

\displaystyle\bigg\|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{1}\cdots\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{n-j-1}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{n-j}\,V(x_{1})\frac{1}{{k_{2}}!}\bigg[\frac{\partial^{k_{2}}}{\partial z^{k_{2}}}G_{0}(z;x_{1},x_{2})\bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad\,\cdots\times V(x_{n-j-1})\frac{1}{k_{n-j}!}\bigg[\frac{\partial^{k_{n-j}}}{\partial z^{k_{n-j}}}G_{0}(z;x_{n-j-1},x_{n-j})\bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad\,\cdots\times V(x_{n-j})\frac{1}{(k_{1}+k_{n-j+1}+1)!}\bigg[\frac{\partial^{k_{1}+k_{n-j+1}+1}}{\partial z^{k_{1}+k_{n-j+1}+1}}G_{0}(z;x_{n-j},x_{1})\bigg]\bigg\|_{{\mathcal{B}}({\mathbb{C}}^{N})}

\displaystyle\quad\leqslant C_{n}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{1}\cdots\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{n-j-1}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{n-j}

\displaystyle\hskip 45.52458pt\times\langle x_{1}\rangle^{-n-1-\varepsilon}\big\{|x_{1}-x_{2}|^{k_{2}+1-n}\chi_{[0,1]}(|z||x_{1}-x_{2}|)

\displaystyle\hskip 45.52458pt\quad+|z|^{(n-1)/2}\big[|x_{1}|^{(2k_{2}+1-n)/2}+|x_{2}|^{(2k_{2}+1-n)/2}\big]\chi_{[1,\infty)}(|z||x_{1}-x_{2}|)\big\}

\displaystyle\hskip 45.52458pt\;\,\vdots

\displaystyle\hskip 45.52458pt\times\langle x_{n-j-1}\rangle^{-n-1-\varepsilon}\big\{|x_{n-j-1}-x_{n-j}|^{k_{n-j}+1-n}\chi_{[0,1]}(|z||x_{n-j-1}-x_{n-j}|)

\displaystyle\hskip 45.52458pt\quad+|z|^{(n-1)/2}\big[|x_{n-j-1}|^{(2k_{n-j}+1-n)/2}+|x_{n-j}|^{(2k_{n-j}+1-n)/2}\big]

\displaystyle\hskip 45.52458pt\qquad\times\chi_{[1,\infty)}(|z||x_{n-j-1}-x_{n-j}|)\big\}

\displaystyle\hskip 45.52458pt\times\langle x_{n-j}\rangle^{-n-1-\varepsilon}\big\{|x_{n-j}-x_{1}|^{k_{1}+k_{n-j+1}+2-n}\chi_{[0,1]}(|z||x_{n-j}-x_{1}|)

\displaystyle\hskip 45.52458pt\quad+|z|^{(n-1)/2}\big[|x_{n-j}|^{(2k_{1}+2k_{n-j+1}+3-n)/2}+|x_{1}|^{(2k_{1}+2k_{n-j+1}+3-n)/2}\big]

\displaystyle\hskip 45.52458pt\qquad\times\chi_{[1,\infty)}(|z||x_{n-j}-x_{1}|)\big\}

\displaystyle\quad\leqslant\widetilde{C}_{n}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{1}\cdots\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{n-j-1}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{n-j}

\displaystyle\hskip 45.52458pt\times\langle x_{1}\rangle^{-n-1-\varepsilon}\big\{|x_{1}-x_{2}|^{k_{2}+1-n}

\displaystyle\hskip 45.52458pt\quad+|z|^{(n-1)/2}[1+|x_{1}|]^{(2k_{2}+1-n)/2}[1+|x_{2}|]^{(2k_{2}+1-n)/2}\big\}

\displaystyle\hskip 45.52458pt\;\,\vdots

\displaystyle\hskip 45.52458pt\times\langle x_{n-j-1}\rangle^{-n-1-\varepsilon}\big\{|x_{n-j-1}-x_{n-j}|^{k_{n-j}+1-n}

\displaystyle\hskip 45.52458pt\quad+|z|^{(n-1)/2}[1+|x_{n-j-1}|]^{(2k_{n-j}+1-n)/2}[1+|x_{n-j}|]^{(2k_{n-j}+1-n)/2}\big\}

\displaystyle\hskip 45.52458pt\times\langle x_{n-j}\rangle^{-n-1-\varepsilon}\big\{|x_{n-j}-x_{1}|^{k_{1}+k_{n-j+1}+2-n}

\displaystyle\hskip 45.52458pt\quad+|z|^{(n-1)/2}[1+|x_{n-j}|]^{(2k_{1}+2k_{n-j+1}+3-n)/2}

\displaystyle\hskip 106.69783pt\times[1+|x_{1}|]^{(2k_{1}+2k_{n-j+1}+3-n)/2}\big\},

(11.12)

\displaystyle\hskip 36.98866ptz\in{\mathbb{C}}_{+},\;\underline{k}\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}^{n-j+1},\;|\underline{k}|=n-1,\;0\leqslant j\leqslant n-2,

where
C_{n},\widetilde{C}_{n}\in(0,\infty)
are suitable constants and we removed all characteristic functions in the last step (a very crude estimate, but sufficient for our purpose).

We postpone a discussion of the case
j=n-1
to the end of the proof of part
(i)
.

Next, one notes that all terms originally multiplied by an “exterior” characteristic function
\chi_{[1,\infty)}(|z||\,\cdot\,|)
, that is, all terms of the type

\displaystyle|z|^{(n-1)/2}[1+|x_{n-j-1}|]^{(2k_{n-j}+1-n)/2}[1+|x_{n-j}|]^{(2k_{n-j}+1-n)/2},

(11.13)

\displaystyle\quad\dots|z|^{(n-1)/2}[1+|x_{n-j}|]^{(2k_{1}+2k_{n-j+1}+3-n)/2}[1+|x_{1}|]^{(2k_{1}+2k_{n-j+1}+3-n)/2},

(11.14)

can be grouped together with

\langle x_{n-j-1}\rangle^{-n-1-\varepsilon},\langle x_{n-j}\rangle^{-n-1-\varepsilon},\dots,\langle x_{n-j}\rangle^{-n-1-\varepsilon},\langle x_{1}\rangle^{-n-1-\varepsilon},

(11.15)

due to the decay assumptions imposed in (
11.9
), and hence we can simply disregard all these contributions in the following as they lead to finite integrals. To illustrate this fact we look at the extreme case where only these terms are considered. Indeed, ignoring all numerical constants and the factors
|z|^{(n-1)/2}
for simplicity, this leads to the integral,

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{1}\cdots\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{n-j-1}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{n-j}

\displaystyle\qquad\times\langle x_{1}\rangle^{-n-1-\varepsilon}[1+|x_{1}|]^{(2k_{2}+1-n)/2}[1+|x_{2}|]^{(2k_{2}+1-n)/2}

\displaystyle\qquad\;\,\vdots

\displaystyle\qquad\times\langle x_{n-j-1}\rangle^{-n-1-\varepsilon}[1+|x_{n-j-1}|]^{(2k_{n-j}+1-n)/2}[1+|x_{n-j}|]^{(2k_{n-j}+1-n)/2}

\displaystyle\qquad\times\langle x_{n-j}\rangle^{-n-1-\varepsilon}[1+|x_{n-j}|]^{(2k_{1}+2k_{n-j+1}+3-n)/2}

\displaystyle\hskip 95.3169pt\times[1+|x_{1}|]^{(2k_{1}+2k_{n-j+1}+3-n)/2}

\displaystyle\quad\leqslant\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{1}\,\langle x_{1}\rangle^{-n-1-\varepsilon}[1+|x_{1}|]^{k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{n-j+1}+2-n}

\displaystyle\qquad\times\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{2}\,\langle x_{2}\rangle^{-n-1-\varepsilon}[1+|x_{2}|]^{k_{2}+k_{3}+1-n}

\displaystyle\qquad\;\,\vdots

\displaystyle\qquad\times\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{n-j-1}\,\langle x_{n-j-1}\rangle^{-n-1-\varepsilon}[1+|x_{n-j-1}|]^{k_{n-j-1}+k_{n-j}+1-n}

\displaystyle\qquad\times\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{n-j}\,\langle x_{n-j}\rangle^{-n-1-\varepsilon}[1+|x_{n-j}|]^{k_{1}+k_{n-j}+k_{n-j+1}+2-n}

\displaystyle\quad\leqslant\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{1}\,\langle x_{1}\rangle^{-n-1-\varepsilon}[1+|x_{1}|]\bigg[\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}y\,\langle y\rangle^{-n-1-\varepsilon}\bigg]^{n-j-2}

\displaystyle\qquad\times\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{n-j}\,\langle x_{n-j}\rangle^{-n-1-\varepsilon}[1+|x_{n-j}|]

\displaystyle\quad<\infty,\quad\underline{k}\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}^{n-j+1},\;|\underline{k}|=n-1,\;0\leqslant j\leqslant n-2,

(11.16)

employing (
11.9
).

Thus, without loss of generality, we now focus on the terms originally multiplied by an “interior” characteristic
function
\chi_{[0,1]}(|z||\,\cdot\,|)
and hence arrive at the need to estimate the integral

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{1}\cdots\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{n-j-1}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{n-j}\,\langle x_{1}\rangle^{-n-1-\varepsilon}|x_{1}-x_{2}|^{k_{2}+1-n}

\displaystyle\qquad\;\,\vdots

\displaystyle\qquad\times\langle x_{n-j-1}\rangle^{-n-1-\varepsilon}|x_{n-j-1}-x_{n-j}|^{k_{n-j}+1-n}

\displaystyle\qquad\times\langle x_{n-j}\rangle^{-n-1-\varepsilon}|x_{n-j}-x_{1}|^{k_{1}+k_{n-j+1}+2-n},

(11.17)

\displaystyle\hskip 32.72049pt\underline{k}\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}^{n-j+1},\;|\underline{k}|=n-1,\;0\leqslant j\leqslant n-2.

For this purpose we recall the following special case of Lemma
6.4
,

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}y\,|y_{1}-y|^{\alpha-n}\langle y\rangle^{-\gamma-\varepsilon}|y-y_{2}|^{\beta-n}

\displaystyle\quad\leqslant\widetilde{C}_{n,\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\varepsilon}\begin{cases}|y_{1}-y_{2}|^{\min(n,\alpha+\beta)-n},&|y_{1}-y_{2}|\leqslant 1,\\
|y_{1}-y_{2}|^{\max(\alpha,\beta)-n},&|y_{1}-y_{2}|\geqslant 1,\end{cases}

\displaystyle\quad\leqslant\widetilde{C}_{n,\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\varepsilon}\begin{cases}|y_{1}-y_{2}|^{\min(n,\alpha+\beta)-n},&|y_{1}-y_{2}|\leqslant 1,\\
1,&|y_{1}-y_{2}|\geqslant 1,\end{cases}

\displaystyle\quad\leqslant C_{n,\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\varepsilon}\big[|y_{1}-y_{2}|^{\min(n,\alpha+\beta)-n}+1\big],

(11.18)

\displaystyle\hskip 21.33955pt\alpha,\beta\in(0,n],\;\gamma>(\alpha+\beta)-n,\;\varepsilon>0,

for appropriate constants

\widetilde{C}_{n,\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\varepsilon},C_{n,\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\varepsilon}\in(0,\infty)
.

Hence,

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{2}\,|x_{1}-x_{2}|^{k_{2}+1-n}\langle x_{2}\rangle^{-n-1-\varepsilon}|x_{2}-x_{3}|^{k_{3}+1-n}

\displaystyle\quad\leqslant c_{2,n}\big[|x_{1}-x_{3}|^{\min(n,k_{2}+k_{3}+2)-n}+1\big],

(11.19)

for some
c_{2,n}\in(0,\infty)
. For precisely the same reason as in the context of (
11.16
), we will simply
disregard the additive term
+1
on the right-hand side of (
11.19
) as the latter is bounded and we want to
focus on the possibly most singular contribution to the integral in (
11.17
) when probing whether or not this integral is finite.

Thus, with these simplifications of ignoring
1
’s and at the same time focusing on the possibly most singular contribution, the next integral over
x_{3}
becomes

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{3}\,|x_{1}-x_{3}|^{k_{2}+k_{3}+2-n}\langle x_{3}\rangle^{-n-1-\varepsilon}|x_{3}-x_{4}|^{k_{4}+1-n}

\displaystyle\quad\leqslant c_{3,n}\big[|x_{1}-x_{4}|^{\min(n,k_{2}+k_{3}+k_{4}+3)-n}+1\big],

(11.20)

for some
c_{3,n}\in(0,\infty)
. Repeating this process (again disregarding
1
’s at each step and focusing on the possibly most singular contributions only) leads to

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{n-j-1}\,|x_{1}-x_{n-j-1}|^{k_{2}+k_{3}+\cdots+k_{n-j-1}+(n-j-2)-n}\langle x_{n-j-1}\rangle^{-n-1-\varepsilon}

\displaystyle\qquad\times|x_{n-j-1}-x_{n-j}|^{k_{n-j}+1-n}

\displaystyle\quad\leqslant c_{n-j-1,n}\big[|x_{1}-x_{n-j}|^{\min(n,k_{2}+k_{3}+\cdots+k_{n-j}+(n-j-1))-n}+1\big],

(11.21)

for some
c_{n-j-1,n}\in(0,\infty)
. Thus, disregarding once more the additive constant
+1
in (
11.21
) results in the following integral over
x_{n-j}
,
0\leqslant j\leqslant n-2
,

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{n-j}\,\Bigg[\begin{cases}|x_{1}-x_{n-j}|^{k_{2}+k_{3}+\cdots+k_{n-j}-j-1},&\big(\sum_{q=2}^{n-j}k_{q}\big)-j-1\leqslant 0,\\
1,&\big(\sum_{q=2}^{n-j}k_{q}\big)-j-1\geqslant 0\end{cases}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad\times\langle x_{n-j}\rangle^{-n-1-\varepsilon}|x_{n-j}-x_{1}|^{k_{1}+k_{n-j+1}+2-n}

\displaystyle\quad\leqslant c\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{n-j}\,\langle x_{n-j}\rangle^{-n-1-\varepsilon}|x_{1}-x_{n-j}|^{k_{1}+k_{2}+\cdots+k_{n-j+1}-j+1-n}

\displaystyle\qquad+d\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{n-j}\,\langle x_{n-j}\rangle^{-n-1-\varepsilon}|x_{n-j}-x_{1}|^{k_{1}+k_{n-j+1}+2-n}

\displaystyle\quad\leqslant C\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{n-j}\,\langle x_{n-j}\rangle^{-n-1-\varepsilon}\big[|x_{1}-x_{n-j}|^{-j}+|x_{1}-x_{n-j}|^{-m}\big]

\displaystyle\hskip 29.87538pt\text{for some $-1\leqslant m\leqslant n-2$, where $m=k_{1}+k_{n-j+1}+2-n$},

(11.22)

for appropriate
c,d,C\in(0,\infty)
, employing again that

\underline{k}=(k_{1},\dots,k_{n-j+1})\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}^{n-j+1},\quad|\underline{k}|=k_{1}+k_{2}+\cdots k_{n-j+1}=n-1.

(11.23)

At this point we invoke the special case
\alpha=n
in (
11.18
), resulting in

\displaystyle\begin{split}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{n-j}\,\langle x_{n-j}\rangle^{-n-1-\varepsilon}\big[|x_{1}-x_{n-j}|^{-j}+|x_{1}-x_{n-j}|^{-m}\big]\leqslant C_{j,m},&\\
0\leqslant j\leqslant n-2,\;0\leqslant m\leqslant n-2,&\end{split}

(11.24)

for some
C_{j,m}\in(0,\infty)
. The remaining case
m=-1
in (
11.22
) leads to

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{n-j}\,\langle x_{n-j}\rangle^{-n-1-\varepsilon}|x_{1}-x_{n-j}|

\displaystyle\quad\leqslant\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{n-j}\,\langle x_{n-j}\rangle^{-n-1-\varepsilon}[1+|x_{1}|+|x_{n-j}|]

\displaystyle\quad\leqslant C_{n}+D_{n}[1+|x_{1}|],

(11.25)

for some
C_{n},D_{n}\in(0,\infty)
, since

\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}y\,\langle y\rangle^{-n-1-\varepsilon}[1+|y|]<\infty.

(11.26)

Thus, altogether, (
11.22
)–(
11.25
) finally yield

\eqref{11.20}\leqslant C_{0}[1+|x_{1}|],\quad 0\leqslant j\leqslant n-2,

(11.27)

for appropriate
C_{0}\in(0,\infty)
. Hence, applying (
11.26
) once more, the integral (
11.17
) is finite.

If
j=n-1
in (
11.3
), (
11.5
) one is left to consider
\underline{k}=(k_{1},k_{2})
,
|\underline{k}|=k_{1}+k_{2}=n-1
, and hence obtains

\displaystyle\tr_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}}\bigg(\frac{d^{n-1}}{dz^{n-1}}(H_{0}-zI_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-1}A(z)\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad=\tr_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}}\bigg(\frac{d^{n-1}}{dz^{n-1}}(H_{0}-zI_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-1}V(H_{0}-zI_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-1}\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad=\tr_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}}\bigg(V(H_{0}-zI_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-(k_{1}+k_{2}+2)}\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad=\tr_{[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}}\bigg(V(H_{0}-zI_{L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}})^{-(n+1)}\bigg),\quad z\in{\mathbb{C}}_{+}.

(11.28)

Since by (
C.9
)–(
C.11
)

\frac{d^{n}}{dz^{n}}G_{0}(z;x,y)\underset{|x-y|\to 0}{=}O(|x-y|),

(11.29)

the trace in (
11.25
) vanishes and hence extends continuously to
z\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}}
.

(ii)
Next, we specialize to
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
even. We investigate each term in (
11.5
) separately. To this end, let
0\leqslant j\leqslant n-1
and
\underline{k}\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}^{n-j+1}
with
|\underline{k}|=n-1
be fixed. We distinguish the following cases:

Case 1:

n\geqslant 4
with
0\leqslant j\leqslant n-3
and
k_{1}+k_{n-j+1}\neq n-1
.

Case 2:

n\geqslant 4
with
0\leqslant j\leqslant n-3
and
k_{1}+k_{n-j+1}=n-1
.

Case 3:

n\geqslant 2
with
j=n-2
and
k_{1}+k_{3}\neq n-1
.

Case 4:

n\geqslant 2
with
j=n-2
and
k_{1}+k_{3}=n-1
.

Case 5:

n\geqslant 2
with
j=n-1
.

We begin with
Case 1
. The assumptions in
Case 1
imply

\text{$0\leqslant k_{\ell}\leqslant n-1$ for all $2\leqslant\ell\leqslant n-j$ and $k_{1}+k_{n-j+1}+1<n$}.

(11.30)

Define the quantity
\delta=\delta(n,j)
by

\delta:=\frac{n-j-2}{n-j-1},

(11.31)

so that
\delta\in(0,1)
and

\delta\geqslant\frac{n-j-\ell}{n-j-\ell+1},\quad 2\leqslant\ell\leqslant n-j-1.

(11.32)

Invoking the final estimate in (
C.34
), one obtains

\displaystyle\bigg\|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{1}\cdots\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{n-j-1}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{n-j}\,V(x_{1})\frac{1}{{k_{2}}!}\bigg[\frac{\partial^{k_{2}}}{\partial z^{k_{2}}}G_{0}(z;x_{1},x_{2})\bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad\,\cdots\times V(x_{n-j-1})\frac{1}{k_{n-j}!}\bigg[\frac{\partial^{k_{n-j}}}{\partial z^{k_{n-j}}}G_{0}(z;x_{n-j-1},x_{n-j})\bigg]

\displaystyle\hskip 35.56593pt\times V(x_{n-j})\frac{1}{(k_{1}+k_{n-j+1}+1)!}\bigg[\frac{\partial^{k_{1}+k_{n-j+1}+1}}{\partial z^{k_{1}+k_{n-j+1}+1}}G_{0}(z;x_{n-j},x_{1})\bigg]\bigg\|_{{\mathcal{B}}({\mathbb{C}}^{N})}

\displaystyle\quad\leqslant C_{n,j,\delta}|z|^{-(n-j)\delta}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{1}\cdots\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{n-j-1}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{n-j}

\displaystyle\hskip 39.83368pt\times\langle x_{1}\rangle^{-n-1-\varepsilon}\big\{|x_{1}-x_{2}|^{k_{2}+1-\delta-n}\chi_{[0,1]}(|z||x_{1}-x_{2}|)

\displaystyle\hskip 45.52458pt\quad+|z|^{(n-1+2\delta)/2}\big[|x_{1}|^{(2k_{2}+1-n)/2}+|x_{2}|^{(2k_{2}+1-n)/2}\big]\chi_{[1,\infty)}(|z||x_{1}-x_{2}|)\big\}

\displaystyle\hskip 39.83368pt\;\,\vdots

\displaystyle\hskip 39.83368pt\times\langle x_{n-j-1}\rangle^{-n-1-\varepsilon}\big\{|x_{n-j-1}-x_{n-j}|^{k_{n-j}+1-\delta-n}\chi_{[0,1]}(|z||x_{n-j-1}-x_{n-j}|)

\displaystyle\hskip 39.83368pt\quad+|z|^{(n-1+2\delta)/2}\big[|x_{n-j-1}|^{(2k_{n-j}+1-n)/2}+|x_{n-j}|^{(2k_{n-j}+1-n)/2}\big]

\displaystyle\hskip 39.83368pt\qquad\times\chi_{[1,\infty)}(|z||x_{n-j-1}-x_{n-j}|)\big\}

\displaystyle\hskip 39.83368pt\times\langle x_{n-j}\rangle^{-n-1-\varepsilon}\big\{|x_{n-j}-x_{1}|^{k_{1}+k_{n-j+1}+2-\delta-n}\chi_{[0,1]}(|z||x_{n-j}-x_{1}|)

\displaystyle\hskip 39.83368pt\quad+|z|^{(n-1+2\delta)/2}\big[|x_{n-j}|^{(2k_{1}+2k_{n-j+1}+3-n)/2}+|x_{1}|^{(2k_{1}+2k_{n-j+1}+3-n)/2}\big]

\displaystyle\hskip 39.83368pt\qquad\times\chi_{[1,\infty)}(|z||x_{n-j}-x_{1}|)\big\}

\displaystyle\quad\leqslant\widetilde{C}_{n,j,\delta}|z|^{-(n-j)\delta}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{1}\cdots\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{n-j-1}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{n-j}

(11.33)

\displaystyle\hskip 39.83368pt\times\langle x_{1}\rangle^{-n-1-\varepsilon}\big\{|x_{1}-x_{2}|^{k_{2}+1-\delta-n}

\displaystyle\hskip 39.83368pt\quad+|z|^{(n-1+2\delta)/2}\big[|x_{1}|^{(2k_{2}+1-n)/2}+|x_{2}|^{(2k_{2}+1-n)/2}\big]\big\}

\displaystyle\hskip 39.83368pt\;\,\vdots

\displaystyle\hskip 39.83368pt\times\langle x_{n-j-1}\rangle^{-n-1-\varepsilon}\big\{|x_{n-j-1}-x_{n-j}|^{k_{n-j}+1-\delta-n}

\displaystyle\hskip 39.83368pt\quad+|z|^{(n-1+2\delta)/2}\big[|x_{n-j-1}|^{(2k_{n-j}+1-n)/2}+|x_{n-j}|^{(2k_{n-j}+1-n)/2}\big]\big\}

\displaystyle\hskip 39.83368pt\times\langle x_{n-j}\rangle^{-n-1-\varepsilon}\big\{|x_{n-j}-x_{1}|^{k_{1}+k_{n-j+1}+2-\delta-n}

\displaystyle\hskip 39.83368pt\quad+|z|^{(n-1+2\delta)/2}\big[|x_{n-j}|^{(2k_{1}+2k_{n-j+1}+3-n)/2}+|x_{1}|^{(2k_{1}+2k_{n-j+1}+3-n)/2}\big]\big\},

\displaystyle\hskip 324.36124ptz\in{\mathbb{C}}_{+},

where
C_{n,j,\delta},\widetilde{C}_{n,j,\delta}\in(0,\infty)
are suitable constants and we removed all characteristic functions in the last step (again, a very crude estimate, but sufficient for our purpose).

We claim that for each bounded subset
\Omega\subset{\mathbb{C}}_{+}
, the integrand under the iterated integral on the right-hand side in (
11.33
) is uniformly bounded with respect to
z\in\Omega
by an integrable function of the variables
x_{1},...,x_{n-j}
. Since
|z|^{(n-1+2\delta)/2}
is locally bounded, to justify the claim, it suffices to establish convergence of the following integral:

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{1}\cdots\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{n-j-1}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{n-j}

(11.34)

\displaystyle\hskip 39.83368pt\times\langle x_{1}\rangle^{-n-1-\varepsilon}\big\{|x_{1}-x_{2}|^{k_{2}+1-\delta-n}

\displaystyle\hskip 39.83368pt\quad+\big[|x_{1}|^{(2k_{2}+1-n)/2}+|x_{2}|^{(2k_{2}+1-n)/2}\big]\big\}

\displaystyle\hskip 39.83368pt\;\,\vdots

\displaystyle\hskip 39.83368pt\times\langle x_{n-j-1}\rangle^{-n-1-\varepsilon}\big\{|x_{n-j-1}-x_{n-j}|^{k_{n-j}+1-\delta-n}

\displaystyle\hskip 39.83368pt\quad+\big[|x_{n-j-1}|^{(2k_{n-j}+1-n)/2}+|x_{n-j}|^{(2k_{n-j}+1-n)/2}\big]\big\}

\displaystyle\hskip 39.83368pt\times\langle x_{n-j}\rangle^{-n-1-\varepsilon}\big\{|x_{n-j}-x_{1}|^{k_{1}+k_{n-j+1}+2-\delta-n}

\displaystyle\hskip 39.83368pt\quad+|\big[|x_{n-j}|^{(2k_{1}+2k_{n-j+1}+3-n)/2}+|x_{1}|^{(2k_{1}+2k_{n-j+1}+3-n)/2}\big]\big\}.

In turn, as in the argument for the proof of part
(i)
, it suffices to focus on the most singular term in (
11.34
) and thus disregard the terms originally multiplied by the factor
|z|^{(n-1+2\delta)/2}
in (
11.33
) (following the same line of reasoning used throughout (
11.14
)–(
11.16
)). With this simplification, the claim reduces to establishing convergence of the integral

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{1}\cdots\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{n-j-1}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{n-j}

(11.35)

\displaystyle\hskip 39.83368pt\times\langle x_{1}\rangle^{-n-1-\varepsilon}|x_{1}-x_{2}|^{k_{2}+1-\delta-n}

\displaystyle\hskip 44.10185pt\vdots

\displaystyle\hskip 39.83368pt\times\langle x_{n-j-1}\rangle^{-n-1-\varepsilon}|x_{n-j-1}-x_{n-j}|^{k_{n-j}+1-\delta-n}

\displaystyle\hskip 39.83368pt\times\langle x_{n-j}\rangle^{-n-1-\varepsilon}|x_{n-j}-x_{1}|^{k_{1}+k_{n-j+1}+2-\delta-n}.

The integrals over the inner variables
x_{2},\ldots,x_{n-j-1}
in (
11.35
) can be estimated successively as follows. Beginning with the integral with respect to
x_{2}
, an application of (
11.18
) with the choices

\alpha=k_{2}+1-\delta,\quad\beta=k_{3}+1-\delta,\quad\gamma=n+1,

(11.36)

implies

\displaystyle\int d^{n}x_{2}\,|x_{1}-x_{2}|^{k_{2}+1-\delta-n}\langle x_{2}\rangle^{-n-1-\varepsilon}|x_{2}-x_{3}|^{k_{3}+1-\delta-n}

(11.37)

\displaystyle\quad\leqslant c_{2,n,\delta}\Big[|x_{1}-x_{3}|^{k_{2}+k_{3}+2(1-\delta)-n}+1\Big],

for some
c_{2,n,\delta}\in(0,\infty)
. The conditions on
\alpha
and
\beta
in (
11.18
) are satisfied by the choices in (
11.36
) since (
11.30
) implies

0\leqslant k_{2}+1-\delta\leqslant n\quad\text{and}\quad 0<k_{3}+1-\delta\leqslant n,

(11.38)

together with

\displaystyle(\alpha+\beta)-n

\displaystyle=k_{2}+k_{3}+2(1-\delta)-n\leqslant n-2\delta<n+1=\gamma.

(11.39)

The inequality in (
11.32
) with
\ell=n-j-1
implies
1-2\delta\leqslant 0
, so that

\displaystyle k_{2}+k_{3}+2(1-\delta)\leqslant n-1+2(1-\delta)=n+1-2\delta\leqslant n,

(11.40)

which yields

\min(n,\alpha+\beta)=\min(n,k_{2}+k_{3}+2(1-\delta))=k_{2}+k_{3}+2(1-\delta),

(11.41)

and the estimate in (
11.37
) follows. If
j=n-3
, then
x_{2}
is the only inner variable, and the integration over the inner variables is complete with (
11.37
). For
j\leqslant n-4
the process continues and there are
n-j-3
remaining inner integrals to estimate. Applying (
11.37
) in (
11.35
), the next inner integral is with respect to
x_{3}
:

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{2}\,\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{3}\,|x_{1}-x_{2}|^{k_{2}+1-\delta-n}\langle x_{2}\rangle^{-n-1-\varepsilon}|x_{2}-x_{3}|^{k_{3}+1-\delta-n}

(11.42)

\displaystyle\hskip 82.51282pt\times\langle x_{3}\rangle^{-n-1-\varepsilon}|x_{3}-x_{4}|^{k_{4}+1-\delta-n}

\displaystyle\quad\leqslant c_{2,n,\delta}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{3}\,\Big[|x_{1}-x_{3}|^{k_{2}+k_{3}+2(1-\delta)-n}+1\Big]\langle x_{3}\rangle^{-n-1-\varepsilon}|x_{3}-x_{4}|^{k_{4}+1-\delta-n}

\displaystyle\quad=c_{2,n,\delta}\bigg[\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{3}\,|x_{1}-x_{3}|^{k_{2}+k_{3}+2(1-\delta)-n}\langle x_{3}\rangle^{-n-1-\varepsilon}|x_{3}-x_{4}|^{k_{4}+1-\delta-n}

\displaystyle\hskip 51.21504pt+\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{3}\,\langle x_{3}\rangle^{-n-1-\varepsilon}|x_{3}-x_{4}|^{k_{4}+1-\delta-n}\bigg]

\displaystyle\quad=:c_{2,n,\delta}[{\mathcal{I}}_{1}(x_{1},x_{4})+{\mathcal{I}}_{2}(x_{4})].

(11.43)

An application of (
11.18
) with the choices

\alpha=n,\quad\beta=k_{4}+1-\delta,\quad\gamma=n+1,

(11.44)

immediately yields (note that in this case
\min(n,\alpha+\beta)=n
)

{\mathcal{I}}_{2}(x_{4})\leqslant c_{3,n,\delta}^{\prime\prime},\quad x_{4}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n},

(11.45)

for some
c_{3,n,\delta}^{\prime\prime}\in(0,\infty)
. Another application of (
11.18
), this time with the choices

\alpha=k_{2}+k_{3}+2(1-\delta),\quad\beta=k_{4}+1-\delta,\quad\gamma=n+1,

(11.46)

implies

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}_{1}(x_{1},x_{4})\leqslant c_{3,n,\delta}^{\prime}\Big[|x_{1}-x_{4}|^{k_{2}+k_{3}+k_{4}+3(1-\delta)-n}+1\Big]

(11.47)

for some
c_{3,n,\delta}^{\prime}\in(0,\infty)
. The conditions on
\alpha
and
\beta
in (
11.18
) are satisfied by the choices in (
11.46
) since (cf. (
11.39
))

0<k_{2}+k_{3}+2(1-\delta)\leqslant n-1+2-2\delta=n+1-2\delta\leqslant n

(11.48)

and

0<k_{4}+1-\delta\leqslant n-\delta\leqslant n

(11.49)

together with

\displaystyle(\alpha+\beta)-n

\displaystyle=\underbrace{k_{2}+k_{3}}_{\leqslant n-1}+\underbrace{k_{4}}_{\leqslant n-1}+3(1-\delta)-n

(11.50)

\displaystyle\leqslant 2(n-1)+3(1-\delta)-n

\displaystyle=n+1-3\delta

\displaystyle<n+1=\gamma.

The inequality in (
11.32
) with
\ell=n-j-2
implies
2-3\delta\leqslant 0
, so that

\displaystyle k_{2}+k_{3}+k_{4}+3(1-\delta)\leqslant n-1+3(1-\delta)=n+2-3\delta\leqslant n,

(11.51)

which yields

\min(n,\alpha+\beta)=\min(n,k_{2}+k_{3}+k_{4}+3(1-\delta))=k_{2}+k_{3}+k_{4}+3(1-\delta),

(11.52)

and the estimate in (
11.47
) follows. Finally, combining (
11.43
), (
11.45
), and (
11.47
), one obtains

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{2}\,\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{3}\,|x_{1}-x_{2}|^{k_{2}+1-\delta-n}\langle x_{2}\rangle^{-n-1-\varepsilon}|x_{2}-x_{3}|^{k_{3}+1-\delta-n}

\displaystyle\hskip 82.51282pt\times\langle x_{3}\rangle^{-n-1-\varepsilon}|x_{3}-x_{4}|^{k_{4}+1-\delta-n}

(11.53)

\displaystyle\quad\leqslant c_{3,n,\delta}\Big[|x_{1}-x_{4}|^{k_{2}+k_{3}+k_{4}+3(1-\delta)-n}+1\Big]

for some
c_{3,n,\delta}\in(0,\infty)
. Continuing systematically in this way, one obtains

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{2}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{3}\cdots\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{n-j-1}

(11.54)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\;\;\,\times|x_{1}-x_{2}|^{k_{2}+1-\delta-n}\langle x_{2}\rangle^{-n-1-\varepsilon}|x_{2}-x_{3}|^{k_{3}+1-\delta-n}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\;\;\,\times\langle x_{3}\rangle^{-n-1-\varepsilon}|x_{3}-x_{4}|^{k_{4}+1-\delta-n}

\displaystyle\qquad\;\;\,\hskip 14.22636pt\vdots

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\;\;\,\times\langle x_{n-j-1}\rangle^{-n-1-\varepsilon}|x_{n-j-1}-x_{n-j}|^{k_{n-j}+1-\delta-n}

\displaystyle\quad\leqslant c_{n-j-2,n,\delta}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{n-j-1}\,\Big[|x_{1}-x_{n-j-1}|^{k_{2}+\cdots+k_{n-j-1}+1(n-j-2)(1-\delta)-n}+1\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 96.73918pt\times\langle x_{n-j-1}\rangle^{-n-1-\varepsilon}|x_{n-j-1}-x_{n-j}|^{k_{n-j}+1-\delta-n}

\displaystyle\quad=c_{n-j-2,n,\delta}\bigg[\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{n-j-1}\,|x_{1}-x_{n-j-1}|^{k_{2}+\cdots+k_{n-j-1}+1(n-j-2)(1-\delta)-n}

\displaystyle\hskip 102.43008pt\times\langle x_{n-j-1}\rangle^{-n-1-\varepsilon}|x_{n-j-1}-x_{n-j}|^{k_{n-j}+1-\delta-n}

\displaystyle\hskip 71.13188pt+\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{n-j-1}\,\langle x_{n-j-1}\rangle^{-n-1-\varepsilon}|x_{n-j-1}-x_{n-j}|^{k_{n-j}+1-\delta-n}\bigg]

\displaystyle\quad=:c_{n-j-2,n,\delta}[{\mathcal{I}}_{1}(x_{1},x_{n-j})+{\mathcal{I}}_{2}(x_{n-j})]

for some
c_{n-j-2,n,\delta}\in(0,\infty)
. An application of (
11.18
) with the choices

\alpha=n,\quad\beta=k_{n-j}+1-\delta,\quad\gamma=n+1

(11.55)

immediately yields (note that in this case
\min(n,\alpha+\beta)=n
)

{\mathcal{I}}_{2}(x_{n-j})\leqslant c_{n-j-1,n,\delta}^{\prime\prime},\quad x_{n-j}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n},

(11.56)

for some
c_{n-j-1,n,\delta}^{\prime\prime}\in(0,\infty)
. Another application of (
11.18
), this time with the choices

\alpha=k_{2}+\cdots+k_{n-j-1}+(n-j-2)(1-\delta),\quad\beta=k_{n-j}+1-\delta,\quad\gamma=n+1,

(11.57)

implies

{\mathcal{I}}_{1}(x_{1},x_{n-j})\leqslant c_{n-j-1,n,\delta}^{\prime}\Big[|x_{1}-x_{n-j}|^{k_{2}+\cdots+k_{n-j}+(n-j-1)(1-\delta)-n}+1\Big]

(11.58)

for some
c_{n-j-1,n,\delta}^{\prime}\in(0,\infty)
. The conditions on
\alpha
and
\beta
in (
11.18
) are satisfied by the choices in (
11.57
) since

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle<k_{2}+\cdots+k_{n-j-1}+(n-j-2)(1-\delta)

\displaystyle\leqslant n-1+(n-j-2)(1-\delta)

\displaystyle=n+(n-j-3)-(n-j-2)\delta

\displaystyle\leqslant n,

(11.59)

and

0<k_{n-j}+1-\delta\leqslant n-\delta\leqslant n.

(11.60)

The final inequality in (
11.59
) follows by choosing
\ell=3
in (
11.32
). In addition,

\displaystyle(\alpha+\beta)-n

\displaystyle=k_{2}+\cdots+k_{n-j}+(n-j-1)(1-\delta)-n

(11.61)

\displaystyle=\underbrace{k_{2}+\cdots+k_{n-j}}_{\leqslant n-1}-(j+1)-(n-j-1)(1-\delta)

\displaystyle\leqslant n-2-j-(n-j-2)(1-\delta)

\displaystyle<n+1

\displaystyle=\gamma.

The inequality in (
11.33
) with
\ell=2
implies
(n-j-2)-(n-j-1)\delta\leqslant 0
, so that

\displaystyle k_{2}+\cdots+k_{n-j}+(n-j-1)\delta

\displaystyle=k_{2}+\cdots+k_{n-j}+(n-j-1)+(n-j-1)\delta

\displaystyle\leqslant n+(n-j-2)-(n-j-1)\delta

\displaystyle\leqslant n,

(11.62)

which yields

\min(n,\alpha+\beta)=k_{2}+\cdots+k_{n-j}+(n-j-1)\delta,

(11.63)

and the estimate in (
11.58
) follows. Finally, combining (
11.54
), (
11.56
), and (
11.58
), one obtains

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{2}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{3}\cdots\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{n-j-1}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\;\;\,\times|x_{1}-x_{2}|^{k_{2}+1-\delta-n}\langle x_{2}\rangle^{-n-1-\varepsilon}|x_{2}-x_{3}|^{k_{3}+1-\delta-n}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\;\;\,\times\langle x_{3}\rangle^{-n-1-\varepsilon}|x_{3}-x_{4}|^{k_{4}+1-\delta-n}

\displaystyle\hskip 41.82558pt\vdots

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\;\;\,\times\langle x_{n-j-1}\rangle^{-n-1-\varepsilon}|x_{n-j-1}-x_{n-j}|^{k_{n-j}+1-\delta-n}

\displaystyle\quad\;\;\,\leqslant c_{n-j-1,n,\delta}\Big[|x_{1}-x_{n-j}|^{k_{2}+\cdots+k_{n-j}+(n-j-1)(1-\delta)-n}+1\Big]

(11.64)

for some
c_{n-j-1,n,\delta}\in(0,\infty)
.

The estimate in (
11.64
) implies

(
11.35
)

\displaystyle\leqslant c_{n-j-1,n,\delta}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{1}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{n-j}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\times\langle x_{1}\rangle^{-n-1-\varepsilon}\Big[|x_{1}-x_{n-j}|^{k_{2}+\cdots+k_{n-j}+(n-j-1)(1-\delta)-n}+1\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\times\langle x_{n-j}\rangle^{-n-1-\varepsilon}|x_{n-j}-x_{1}|^{k_{1}+k_{n-j+1}+2-\delta-n}.

(11.65)

Focusing on the integral over
x_{n-j}
in (
11.65
),

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{n-j}\,\Big[|x_{1}-x_{n-j}|^{k_{2}+\cdots+k_{n-j}+(n-j-1)(1-\delta)-n}+1\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 55.48277pt\times\langle x_{n-j}\rangle^{-n-1-\varepsilon}|x_{n-j}-x_{1}|^{k_{1}+k_{n-j+1}+2-\delta-n}

\displaystyle\quad=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{n-j}\,|x_{1}-x_{n-j}|^{(n-j)(1-\delta)-n}\langle x_{n-j}\rangle^{-n-1-\varepsilon}

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{n-j}\,\langle x_{n-j}\rangle^{-n-1-\varepsilon}|x_{n-j}-x_{1}|^{k_{1}+k_{n-j+1}+2-\delta-n}

\displaystyle\quad={\mathcal{I}}_{1}(x_{1})+{\mathcal{I}}_{2}(x_{1}),

(11.66)

an application of (
11.18
) with the choices

\alpha=(n-j)(1-\delta),\quad\beta=n,\quad\gamma=n+1,

(11.67)

yields

{\mathcal{I}}_{1}(x_{1})\leqslant c_{n-j,n,\delta}^{\prime},\quad x_{1}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n},

(11.68)

for some
c_{n-j,n,\delta}^{\prime}\in(0,\infty)
. The conditions on
\alpha
and
\beta
in (
11.18
) are satisfied by the choices in (
11.67
) since
\alpha=(n-j)(1-\delta),\beta=n\in(0,n]
and

(\alpha+\beta)-n=\alpha=(n-j)(1-\delta)\leqslant n<n+1=\gamma.

(11.69)

Since
\min(n,\alpha+\beta)=n
, the estimate in (
11.18
) results in (
11.68
). To estimate

{\mathcal{I}}_{2}(\,\cdot\,)
, one applies (
11.18
) with the choices

\alpha=n,\quad\beta=k_{1}+k_{n-j+1}+2-\delta,\quad\gamma=n+1,

(11.70)

to obtain

{\mathcal{I}}_{2}(x_{1})\leqslant c_{n-j,n,\delta}^{\prime\prime},\quad x_{1}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n},

(11.71)

for some
c_{n-j,n,\delta}^{\prime\prime}\in(0,\infty)
. The conditions on
\alpha
and
\beta
in (
11.18
) are satisfied by the choices in (
11.70
) since
\alpha=n\in(0,n]
,

0<\beta=\underbrace{k_{1}+k_{n-j+1}}_{\leqslant n-2}+2-\delta\leqslant n-\delta\leqslant n,

(11.72)

and
(\alpha+\beta)-n=\beta\leqslant n<n+1
. In this case,
\min(n,\alpha+\beta)=n
, and (
11.18
) results in (
11.71
). Combining (
11.66
), (
11.68
), and (
11.71
), one obtains

\eqref{11.65}\leqslant c_{n-j,n,\delta},\quad x_{1}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n},

(11.73)

for some
c_{n-j,n,\delta}\in(0,\infty)
. As a consequence,

\eqref{11.64}\leqslant c_{n-j-1,n,\delta}c_{n-j,n,\delta}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{1}\,\langle x_{1}\rangle^{-n-1-\varepsilon}<\infty.

(11.74)

Hence, this establishes the claim that for each bounded subset
\Omega\subset{\mathbb{C}}_{+}
, the integrand under the iterated integral on the right-hand side in (
11.33
) is uniformly bounded with respect to
z\in\Omega
by an integrable function of the variables
x_{1},...,x_{n-j}
. As a consequence of this claim, (
11.33
) implies that for each bounded subset
\Omega\subset{\mathbb{C}}_{+}
, the following estimate holds:

\eqref{11.31}\leqslant C_{n,j,\delta,\Omega}|z|^{-(n-j)\delta},\quad z\in\Omega,

(11.75)

for some
C_{n,j,\delta,\Omega}\in(0,\infty)
.
In summary, for
Case 1
, one has that for any bounded subset
\Omega\subset{\mathbb{C}}_{+}
,

\displaystyle\bigg\|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{1}\cdots\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{n-j-1}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{n-j}\,V(x_{1})\frac{1}{{k_{2}}!}\bigg[\frac{\partial^{k_{2}}}{\partial z^{k_{2}}}G_{0}(z;x_{1},x_{2})\bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad\,\cdots\times V(x_{n-j-1})\frac{1}{k_{n-j}!}\bigg[\frac{\partial^{k_{n-j}}}{\partial z^{k_{n-j}}}G_{0}(z;x_{n-j-1},x_{n-j})\bigg]

\displaystyle\hskip 35.56593pt\times V(x_{n-j})\frac{1}{(k_{1}+k_{n-j+1}+1)!}\bigg[\frac{\partial^{k_{1}+k_{n-j+1}+1}}{\partial z^{k_{1}+k_{n-j+1}+1}}G_{0}(z;x_{n-j},x_{1})\bigg]\bigg\|_{{\mathcal{B}}({\mathbb{C}}^{N})}

\displaystyle\quad\leqslant C_{n,j,\delta,\Omega}|z|^{-(n-j)\delta},\quad z\in\Omega,

(11.76)

where
\delta
is defined by (
11.31
). In addition, since
|z|^{-(n-j)\delta}
is bounded in
\overline{\Omega}
if
0\notin\overline{\Omega}
, Lebesgue’s dominated convergence theorem implies that

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{1}\cdots\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{n-j-1}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{n-j}\,V(x_{1})\frac{1}{{k_{2}}!}\bigg[\frac{\partial^{k_{2}}}{\partial z^{k_{2}}}G_{0}(z;x_{1},x_{2})\bigg]

(11.77)

\displaystyle\qquad\,\cdots\times V(x_{n-j-1})\frac{1}{k_{n-j}!}\bigg[\frac{\partial^{k_{n-j}}}{\partial z^{k_{n-j}}}G_{0}(z;x_{n-j-1},x_{n-j})\bigg]

\displaystyle\hskip 35.56593pt\times V(x_{n-j})\frac{1}{(k_{1}+k_{n-j+1}+1)!}\bigg[\frac{\partial^{k_{1}+k_{n-j+1}+1}}{\partial z^{k_{1}+k_{n-j+1}+1}}G_{0}(z;x_{n-j},x_{1})\bigg],\quad z\in\Omega,

is analytic in
\Omega
and extends continuously to
\overline{\Omega}
if
0\notin\overline{\Omega}
. This settles
Case 1
.

Next, we treat
Case 2
. The assumptions in
Case 2
imply

\text{$k_{\ell}=0$ for all $2\leqslant\ell\leqslant n-j$ and $k_{1}+k_{n-j+1}+1=n$}.

(11.78)

Let
\delta\in(0,1)
be fixed. Applying the final estimate in (
C.34
), one obtains:

\displaystyle\bigg\|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{1}\cdots\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{n-j-1}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{n-j}\,V(x_{1})\frac{1}{{k_{2}}!}\bigg[\frac{\partial^{k_{2}}}{\partial z^{k_{2}}}G_{0}(z;x_{1},x_{2})\bigg]

\displaystyle\qquad\,\cdots\times V(x_{n-j-1})\frac{1}{k_{n-j}!}\bigg[\frac{\partial^{k_{n-j}}}{\partial z^{k_{n-j}}}G_{0}(z;x_{n-j-1},x_{n-j})\bigg]

\displaystyle\hskip 35.56593pt\times V(x_{n-j})\frac{1}{(k_{1}+k_{n-j+1}+1)!}\bigg[\frac{\partial^{k_{1}+k_{n-j+1}+1}}{\partial z^{k_{1}+k_{n-j+1}+1}}G_{0}(z;x_{n-j},x_{1})\bigg]\bigg\|_{{\mathcal{B}}({\mathbb{C}}^{N})}

\displaystyle\quad=\bigg\|\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{1}\cdots\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{n-j-1}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{n-j}\,V(x_{1})G_{0}(z;x_{1},x_{2})

\displaystyle\hskip 68.28644pt\cdots\times V(x_{n-j-1})G_{0}(z;x_{n-j-1},x_{n-j})

\displaystyle\hskip 81.3749pt\times V(x_{n-j})\frac{1}{n!}\bigg[\frac{\partial^{n}}{\partial z^{n}}G_{0}(z;x_{n-j},x_{1})\bigg]\bigg\|_{{\mathcal{B}}({\mathbb{C}}^{N})}

\displaystyle\quad\leqslant C_{n,j,\delta}|z|^{-(n-j-1)\delta-1}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{1}\cdots\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{n-j-1}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x_{n-j}
