|\zeta-\zeta_{0}|<\varepsilon(\zeta_{0})\end{subarray}}\bigg|\int_{0}^{1}dt\,f_{u,p,\nu}(\zeta,t)\bigg|<\infty.

(B.33)

Therefore, condition
(iii)
in Lemma
B.2
holds, and it follows that
a_{u,p,\nu}
is analytic in
\Omega_{0}
.
∎

Proposition B.4
.

Assume Hypothesis
B.1
, let
p\in{\mathbb{N}}
, and let
\Omega_{0}
be defined as in (
B.26
). The following statements hold:

(i)
If
\text{\rm Re}(\nu-p-(1/2))\leqslant 0
, then the function
R_{\nu,p}^{(1)}:\Omega_{0}\to{\mathbb{C}}
defined by (
B.19
) is analytic in
\Omega_{0}
.

(ii)
If
\text{\rm Re}(\nu-p-(1/2))>0
, then the function
\widetilde{R}_{\nu,p,q}^{(1)}:\Omega_{0}\to{\mathbb{C}}
defined by (
B.23
) is analytic in
\Omega_{0}
for every
q\in{\mathbb{N}}
such that
\text{\rm Re}(\nu-q-(1/2))<0
.

Proof.

Let
p\in{\mathbb{N}}
and suppose
\text{\rm Re}(\nu-p-(1/2))\leqslant 0
. We begin with the proof of
(i)
. It suffices to show that the function
b_{p,\nu}:\Omega_{0}\to{\mathbb{C}}
defined by (cf. (
B.27
))

\displaystyle b_{p,\nu}(\zeta)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}du\,e^{-u}u^{p+\nu-(1/2)}\int_{0}^{1}dt\,(1-t)^{p-1}\bigg(1-\frac{ut}{2i\zeta}\bigg)^{\nu-p-(1/2)}

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{\infty}du\,e^{-u}u^{p+\nu-(1/2)}a_{u,p,\nu}(\zeta),\quad\zeta\in\Omega_{0},

(B.34)

is analytic in
\Omega_{0}
. The function
e^{-u}u^{p+\nu-(1/2)}a_{u,p,\nu}(\zeta)
is a measurable function of
u\in(0,\infty)
for each
\zeta\in\Omega_{0}
and is, by Proposition
B.3
, an analytic function of
\zeta\in\Omega_{0}
for each
u\in(0,\infty)
. Therefore, by Lemma
B.2
, it suffices to prove that for each
\zeta_{0}\in\Omega_{0}
, there exists
\varepsilon(\zeta_{0})\in(0,\infty)
such that

\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\zeta\in\Omega_{0}\\
|\zeta-\zeta_{0}|<\varepsilon(\zeta_{0})\end{subarray}}\bigg|\int_{0}^{\infty}du\,e^{-u}u^{p+\nu-(1/2)}a_{u,p,\nu}(\zeta)\bigg|<\infty.

(B.35)

To this end, let
\zeta_{0}\in\Omega_{0}
. Choose
\delta\in(0,\pi/2)
such that

\zeta_{0}\in\Omega_{\delta}:=\{\zeta\in{\mathbb{C}}\,|\,|\arg(\zeta)-(\pi/2)|<\pi-\delta\}.

(B.36)

An application of (
B.31
) yields the following estimate:

\displaystyle\bigg|\int_{0}^{\infty}du\,e^{-u}u^{p+\nu-(1/2)}a_{u,p,\nu}(\zeta)\bigg|

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{C_{\nu,p,\delta}}{p}\int_{0}^{\infty}du\,e^{-u}u^{\text{\rm Re}(p+\nu-(1/2))}

(B.37)

\displaystyle=\frac{C_{\nu,p,\delta}}{p}\Gamma\bigg(\text{\rm Re}\bigg(p+\nu+(1/2)\bigg)\bigg),\quad\zeta\in\Omega_{\delta}.

(B.38)

Thus, one obtains (
B.35
) by choosing
\varepsilon(\zeta_{0})\in(0,1)
so small that

\{\zeta\in{\mathbb{C}}\,|\,|\zeta-\zeta_{0}|<\varepsilon(\zeta_{0})\}\subset\Omega_{\delta}.

(B.39)

Finally, to prove item
(ii)
, suppose that
\text{\rm Re}(\nu-p-(1/2))>0
and
q\in{\mathbb{N}}
with
\text{\rm Re}(\nu-q-(1/2))<0
. The first term on the right-hand side in (
B.23
) is analytic in
{\mathbb{C}}\backslash\{0\}
, while the second term on the right-hand side in (
B.23
) is analytic in
\Omega_{0}
by the statement in
(i)
. Hence, the statement in
(ii)
follows from the subspace property of analytic functions.
∎

Remark B.5
.

Of course, analyticity of
R_{\nu,p}^{(1)}
(resp.,
\widetilde{R}_{\nu,p,q}^{(1)}
) follows immediately from (
B.18
) (resp., (
B.22
)). However, the proof of Proposition
B.4
shows that the
\zeta
-derivatives of
R_{\nu,p}^{(1)}
may be computed by differentiating under the integrals in (
B.19
). In fact, as a consequence of (
B.28
) and the proof of Proposition
B.4
, one infers that under the assumptions of Proposition
B.4
,

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{n}}{\partial^{n}\zeta}b_{p,\nu}(\zeta)=\int_{0}^{\infty}du\,e^{-u}u^{p+\nu-(1/2)}\int_{0}^{1}dt\,(1-t)^{p-1}\frac{\partial^{n}}{\partial^{n}\zeta}\bigg(1-\frac{ut}{2i\zeta}\bigg)^{\nu-p-(1/2)},

\displaystyle\hskip 241.84842pt\zeta\in\Omega_{0},\;n\in{\mathbb{N}}.

(B.40)

\diamond

In order to state the next result, we introduce
\widetilde{O}
-notation. Recall that if
\Omega\subseteq{\mathbb{C}}
and
f,g:\Omega\to{\mathbb{C}}
, then one writes

f(\zeta)=O(g(\zeta)),\quad\zeta\in\Omega,

(B.41)

if and only if there exists a constant
C\in(0,\infty)
(independent of
\zeta\in\Omega
) such that

|f(\zeta)|\leqslant C|g(\zeta)|,\quad\zeta\in\Omega.

(B.42)

One writes

f(\zeta)=\widetilde{O}(g(\zeta)),\quad\zeta\in\Omega,

(B.43)

if and only if for each
n\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}
,

\frac{d^{n}f}{d\zeta^{n}}=O\bigg(\frac{d^{n}g}{d\zeta^{n}}\bigg),\quad\zeta\in\Omega.

(B.44)

It is understood that the constant corresponding to (
B.44
) will, in general, depend on
n\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}
.

The principal asymptotic result for
H_{\nu}^{(1)}(\zeta)
as
|\zeta|\to\infty
can be summarized as follows:

Lemma B.6
.

Assume Hypothesis
B.1
holds. If
\delta\in(0,\pi/2)
, then

H_{\nu}^{(1)}(\zeta)=e^{i\zeta}\omega_{\nu}(\zeta),\quad\zeta\in\Omega_{\delta},

(B.45)

where

\omega_{\nu}(\zeta)\underset{|\zeta|\to\infty}{=}\widetilde{O}\big((1+|\zeta|)^{-1/2}\big),\quad\zeta\in\Omega_{\delta}\cap\{z\in{\mathbb{C}}\,|\,|z|\geqslant 1\}.

(B.46)

Proof.

Assume Hypothesis
B.1
holds. We distinguish two cases:
\text{\rm Re}(\nu-(3/2))\leqslant 0
and
\text{\rm Re}(\nu-(3/2))>0
. If
\text{\rm Re}(\nu-(3/2))\leqslant 0
, then one may take
p=1
in (
B.18
) to obtain

\displaystyle H_{\nu}^{(1)}(\zeta)

\displaystyle=\bigg(\frac{2}{\pi\zeta}\bigg)^{1/2}e^{i[\zeta-(\pi/2)\nu-(\pi/4)]}\big[1+R_{\nu,1}^{(1)}(\zeta)\big]

\displaystyle=e^{i\zeta}\omega_{\nu}(\zeta),\quad\zeta\in\Omega_{\delta},

(B.47)

where

\omega_{\nu}(\zeta)=\bigg(\frac{2}{\pi\zeta}\bigg)^{1/2}e^{i[-(\pi/2)\nu-(\pi/4)]}\big[1+R_{\nu,1}^{(1)}(\zeta)\big],\quad\zeta\in\Omega_{\delta}.

(B.48)

It remains to prove
\omega_{\nu}(\,\cdot\,)
defined by (
B.48
) satisfies (
B.46
). To prove this, it suffices to show

\zeta^{-1/2}\big[1+R_{\nu,1}^{(1)}(\zeta)\big]\underset{|\zeta|\to\infty}{=}\widetilde{O}\big((1+|\zeta|)^{-1/2}\big),\quad\zeta\in\Omega_{\delta}\cap\{z\in{\mathbb{C}}\,|\,|z|\geqslant 1\};

(B.49)

that is,

\displaystyle\frac{d^{n}}{d\zeta^{n}}\zeta^{-1/2}\big[1+R_{\nu,1}^{(1)}(\zeta)\big]\underset{|\zeta|\to\infty}{=}O\bigg(\frac{d^{n}}{d\zeta^{n}}(1+|\zeta|)^{-1/2}\bigg),

(B.50)

\displaystyle\zeta\in\Omega_{\delta}\cap\{z\in{\mathbb{C}}\,|\,|z|\geqslant 1\},\;n\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}.

For
n=0
, the relation in (
B.50
) follows immediately from (
B.20
). To treat the derivatives in (
B.50
), one differentiates under the integrals in (
B.19
). For simplicity, we only treat the case
n=1
and omit the details for
n\geqslant 2
. One computes

\displaystyle\frac{d}{d\zeta}\zeta^{-1/2}\big[1+R_{\nu,1}^{(1)}(\zeta)\big]=-\frac{1}{2\zeta^{3/2}}\big[1+R_{\nu,1}^{(1)}(\zeta)\big]+\zeta^{-1/2}\frac{d}{d\zeta}R_{\nu,1}^{(1)}(\zeta)

\displaystyle\quad\underset{|\zeta|\to\infty}{=}O\big((1+|\zeta|)^{-3/2}\big)

\displaystyle\;\;\quad\qquad-\zeta^{-5/2}\frac{((1/2)-\nu)_{1}}{2i\Gamma(\nu+(1/2))}\int_{0}^{\infty}du\,e^{-u}u^{\nu+(1/2)}\int_{0}^{1}dt\,\bigg(1-\frac{ut}{2i\zeta}\bigg)^{\nu-(3/2)}

\displaystyle\;\;\quad\qquad-\zeta^{-7/2}\frac{((1/2)-\nu)_{1}(\nu-(3/2))}{4\Gamma(\nu+(1/2))}\int_{0}^{\infty}du\,e^{-u}u^{\nu+(3/2)}

\displaystyle\hskip 176.407pt\times\int_{0}^{1}dt\,t\bigg(1-\frac{ut}{2i\zeta}\bigg)^{\nu-(5/2)}

\displaystyle\quad\underset{|\zeta|\to\infty}{=}O\big((1+|\zeta|)^{-3/2}\big),\quad\zeta\in\Omega_{\delta}\cap\{z\in{\mathbb{C}}\,|\,|z|\geqslant 1\}.

(B.51)

To obtain the final equality in (
B.51
), one applies (
B.17
) to bound the two (
\zeta
-dependent) integrals with respect to
t\in(0,1)
. This settles the case when
\text{\rm Re}(\nu-(3/2))\leqslant 0
.

If
\text{\rm Re}(\nu-(3/2))>0
, one chooses
q\in{\mathbb{N}}
such that
\text{\rm Re}(\nu-q-(1/2))<0
. Then

\displaystyle H_{\nu}^{(1)}(\zeta)

\displaystyle=\bigg(\frac{2}{\pi\zeta}\bigg)^{1/2}e^{i[\zeta-(\pi/2)\nu-(\pi/4)]}\big[1+\widetilde{R}_{\nu,1,q}^{(1)}(\zeta)\big]

\displaystyle=e^{i\zeta}\omega_{\nu}(\zeta),\quad\zeta\in\Omega_{\delta},

(B.52)

where

\omega_{\nu}(\zeta)=\bigg(\frac{2}{\pi\zeta}\bigg)^{1/2}e^{i[-(\pi/2)\nu-(\pi/4)]}\big[1+\widetilde{R}_{\nu,1,q}^{(1)}(\zeta)\big],\quad\zeta\in\Omega_{\delta}.

(B.53)

Then, as a consequence of (
B.23
) and (
B.49
), one obtains

\zeta^{-1/2}\big[1+\widetilde{R}_{\nu,1,q}^{(1)}(\zeta)\big]\underset{|\zeta|\to\infty}{=}\widetilde{O}\big((1+|\zeta|)^{-1/2}\big),\quad\zeta\in\Omega_{\delta}\cap\{z\in{\mathbb{C}}\,|\,|z|\geqslant 1\}.

(B.54)

∎

B.2.
Asymptotics of
H_{\nu}^{(1)}(\zeta)
as
|\zeta|\to 0

Since the asymptotics derived here will be applied to Dirac operators, we only consider
\nu\in[0,\infty)
from this point on. We distinguish two cases:

(i)

\nu\in(0,\infty)\backslash{\mathbb{N}}
,

and

(ii)

\nu\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}
.

Case
(i)
:
\nu\in(0,\infty)\backslash{\mathbb{N}}
.

In this case, one has the following representation for
H_{\nu}^{(1)}
in terms of Bessel functions:

H_{\nu}^{(1)}(\zeta)=[1+i\cot(\nu\pi)]J_{\nu}(\zeta)-i[\sin(\nu\pi)]^{-1}J_{-\nu}(\zeta),\quad\zeta\in{\mathbb{C}}.

(B.55)

Repeated term-by-term differentiation of the series representations for
J_{\pm\nu}
reveals

\displaystyle\frac{d^{k}}{d\zeta^{k}}J_{\pm\nu}(\zeta)

\displaystyle\underset{|\zeta|\to 0}{=}\bigg(\frac{d^{k}}{d\zeta^{k}}\bigg[\frac{1}{\Gamma(1\pm\nu)}(\zeta/2)^{\pm\nu}\bigg]\bigg)\big[1+O\big(|\zeta|^{2}\big)\big]

(B.56)

\displaystyle\underset{|\zeta|\to 0}{=}\frac{(\pm\nu)_{k}}{2^{k}\Gamma(1\pm\nu)}(\zeta/2)^{\pm\nu-k}\big[1+O\big(|\zeta|^{2}\big)\big],\quad|\zeta|\leqslant 1,\;k\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}.

As a result,

\displaystyle\frac{d^{k}}{d\zeta^{k}}H_{\nu}^{(1)}(\zeta)\underset{|\zeta|\to 0}{=}[1+i\cot(\nu\pi)]\frac{(\nu)_{k}}{2^{k}\Gamma(\nu+1)}(\zeta/2)^{\nu-k}\big[1+O\big(|\zeta|^{2}\big)\big]

(B.57)

\displaystyle\quad-i[\sin(\nu\pi)]^{-1}\frac{(-\nu)_{k}}{2^{k}\Gamma(1-\nu)}(\zeta/2)^{-\nu-k}\big[1+O\big(|\zeta|^{2}\big)\big],\quad|\zeta|\leqslant 1,\;k\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0},

which settles Case
(i)
.

Case
(ii)
:
\nu\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}
.

Since
\nu
is a nonnegative integer, we write

\widetilde{n}:=\nu\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}.

(B.58)

First, we treat the case
\widetilde{n}\in{\mathbb{N}}
. Then,

\displaystyle J_{\widetilde{n}}(\zeta)=\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\frac{(-1)^{m}}{m!(\widetilde{n}+m)!}(\zeta/2)^{2m+\widetilde{n}},\quad\zeta\in{\mathbb{C}},

(B.59)

and

\displaystyle Y_{\widetilde{n}}(\zeta)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{\pi}(\zeta/2)^{-\widetilde{n}}\sum_{m=0}^{\widetilde{n}-1}\frac{(\widetilde{n}-m-1)!}{m!}(\zeta/2)^{2m}+\frac{2}{\pi}\text{\rm ln}(\zeta/2)J_{\widetilde{n}}(\zeta)

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{\pi}(\zeta/2)^{\widetilde{n}}\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}[\psi(m+1)+\psi(\widetilde{n}+m+1)]\frac{(-1)^{m}}{m!(\widetilde{n}+m)!}(\zeta/2)^{2m}

\displaystyle=:Y_{1,\widetilde{n}}(\zeta)+Y_{2,\widetilde{n}}(\zeta)+Y_{3,\widetilde{n}}(\zeta),\quad\zeta\in{\mathbb{C}}\backslash\{0\}.

(B.60)

Repeated differentiation of the series representation for
J_{\widetilde{n}}
yields

\displaystyle\frac{d^{k}}{d\zeta^{k}}J_{\widetilde{n}}(\zeta)\underset{|\zeta|\to 0}{=}\frac{(\widetilde{n})_{k}}{2^{k}\widetilde{n}!}(\zeta/2)^{\widetilde{n}-k}\big[1+O\big(|\zeta|^{2}\big)\big],\quad|\zeta|\leqslant 1,\;k\in{\mathbb{N}}\cap[0,\widetilde{n}],

(B.61)

and

\displaystyle\frac{d^{k}}{d\zeta^{k}}J_{\widetilde{n}}(\zeta)\underset{|\zeta|\to 0}{=}\begin{cases}\frac{(-1)^{(k-\widetilde{n})/2}k!}{2^{k}\left((k-\widetilde{n})/2\right)!\left((k+\widetilde{n})/2\right)!}\big[1+O\big(|\zeta|^{2}\big)\big],&\text{$\widetilde{n}+k$ even},\\[5.69054pt]
\frac{(-1)^{(k-\widetilde{n}+1)/2}(k+1)!}{2^{k+1}\left((k-\widetilde{n}+1)/2\right)!\left((k+\widetilde{n}+1)/2\right)!}\zeta\big[1+O\big(|\zeta|^{2}\big)\big],&\text{$\widetilde{n}+k$ odd},\end{cases}

(B.62)

\displaystyle|\zeta|\leqslant 1,\,k\in{\mathbb{N}}\cap(\widetilde{n},\infty).

Repeated differentiation of
Y_{1,\widetilde{n}}
yields

\displaystyle\frac{d^{k}}{d\zeta^{k}}Y_{1,\widetilde{n}}(\zeta)

\displaystyle\underset{|\zeta|\to 0}{=}-\pi^{-1}\bigg(\frac{d^{k}}{d\zeta^{k}}\big[(\zeta/2)^{-\widetilde{n}}\big]\bigg)\big[1+O\big(|\zeta|^{2}\big)\big]

\displaystyle\underset{|\zeta|\to 0}{=}-\frac{(-\widetilde{n})_{k}}{2^{k}\pi}(\zeta/2)^{-\widetilde{n}-k}\big[1+O\big(|\zeta|^{2}\big)\big],\quad|\zeta|\leqslant 1,\;k\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}.

(B.63)

In view of (
B.59
),

\displaystyle Y_{2,\widetilde{n}}(\zeta)\underset{|\zeta|\to 0}{=}\frac{2}{\pi\widetilde{n}!}\text{\rm ln}(\zeta/2)(\zeta/2)^{\widetilde{n}}+\widetilde{O}\big(\zeta^{\widetilde{n}+2}\text{\rm ln}(\zeta)\big),\quad|\zeta|\leqslant 1.

(B.64)

Differentiation of the series representation of
Y_{3,\widetilde{n}}
yields

\displaystyle\frac{d^{k}}{d\zeta^{k}}Y_{3,\widetilde{n}}(\zeta)\underset{|\zeta|\to 0}{=}-\frac{1}{\pi}\frac{[\psi(\widetilde{n}+1)-\gamma](\widetilde{n})_{k}}{2^{k}\widetilde{n}!}(\zeta/2)^{\widetilde{n}-k}\big[1+O\big(|\zeta|^{2}\big)\big],

(B.65)

\displaystyle|\zeta|\leqslant 1,\;k\in{\mathbb{N}}\cap[0,\widetilde{n}],

and

\displaystyle\frac{d^{k}}{d\zeta^{k}}Y_{3,\widetilde{n}}(\zeta)

(B.66)

\displaystyle\quad\underset{|\zeta|\to 0}{=}\begin{cases}-\frac{1}{\pi}\frac{\left[\psi\left([(k-\widetilde{n})/2]+1\right)+\psi\left([(k+\widetilde{n})/2]+1\right)\right](-1)^{(k-\widetilde{n})/2}k!}{2^{k}\left((k-\widetilde{n})/2\right)!\left((k+\widetilde{n})/2\right)!}\big[1+O\big(|\zeta|^{2}\big)\big],\\
\hskip 200.59201pt\text{$\widetilde{n}+k$ even},\\
-\frac{1}{\pi}\frac{\left[\psi\left([(k-\widetilde{n}+1)/2]+1\right)+\psi\left([(k+\widetilde{n}+1)/2]+1\right)\right](-1)^{(k-\widetilde{n}+1)/2}k!}{2^{k}\left((k-\widetilde{n}+1)/2\right)!\left((k+\widetilde{n}+1)/2\right)!}\zeta\big[1+O\big(|\zeta|^{2}\big)\big],\\
\hskip 237.58067pt\text{$\widetilde{n}+k$ odd},\end{cases}

\displaystyle\hskip 226.19931pt|\zeta|\leqslant 1,\;k\in{\mathbb{N}}\cap(\widetilde{n},\infty).

In the remaining case
\widetilde{n}=0
, one obtains (cf., e.g.,
[
67
, (11), (12)]
)

\displaystyle J_{0}(\zeta)\underset{|\zeta|\to 0}{=}1+O\big(\zeta^{2}\big),\quad|\zeta|\leqslant 1,

(B.67)

with

\displaystyle\frac{d^{k}}{d\zeta^{k}}J_{0}(\zeta)\underset{|\zeta|\to 0}{=}\begin{cases}\frac{(-1)^{k/2}k!}{2^{k}[(k/2)!]^{2}}\big[1+O\big(|\zeta|^{2}\big)\big],&\text{$k$ even},\\[5.69054pt]
\frac{(-1)^{(k+1)/2}(k+1)!}{2^{k+1}[((k+1)/2)!]^{2}}\zeta\big[1+O\big(|\zeta|^{2}\big)\big],&\text{$k$ odd},\end{cases}

(B.68)

\displaystyle|\zeta|\leqslant 1,\;k\in{\mathbb{N}},

and

\displaystyle Y_{0}(\zeta)\underset{|\zeta|\to 0}{=}\frac{2}{\pi}\text{\rm ln}(\zeta/2)+\frac{2\gamma}{\pi}+\widetilde{O}\big(\zeta^{2}\text{\rm ln}(\zeta)\big),\quad|\zeta|\leqslant 1.

(B.69)

Finally, to obtain expressions for the derivatives of
H_{\widetilde{n}}^{(1)}
, one applies the representation

H_{\widetilde{n}}^{(1)}(\zeta)=J_{\widetilde{n}}(\zeta)+iY_{\widetilde{n}}(\zeta),\quad\zeta\in{\mathbb{C}}\backslash\{0\},\;\widetilde{n}\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}.

(B.70)

If
\widetilde{n}\in{\mathbb{N}}
, then

\displaystyle\frac{d^{k}}{d\zeta^{k}}H_{\widetilde{n}}^{(1)}(\zeta)\underset{|\zeta|\to 0}{=}\frac{(\widetilde{n})_{k}}{2^{k}\widetilde{n}!}(\zeta/2)^{\widetilde{n}-k}\big[1+O\big(|\zeta|^{2}\big)\big]

\displaystyle\quad-i\frac{(-\widetilde{n})_{k}}{2^{k}\pi}(\zeta/2)^{-\widetilde{n}-k}\big[1+O\big(|\zeta|^{2}\big)\big]

\displaystyle\quad+i\frac{2}{\pi\widetilde{n}!}\frac{d^{k}}{d\zeta^{k}}\big[\text{\rm ln}(\zeta/2)(\zeta/2)^{\widetilde{n}}\big]+O\bigg(\frac{d^{k}}{d\zeta^{k}}\big[\zeta^{\widetilde{n}+2}\text{\rm ln}(\zeta)\big]\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{i}{\pi}\frac{[\psi(\widetilde{n}+1)-\gamma](\widetilde{n})_{k}}{2^{k}\widetilde{n}!}(\zeta/2)^{\widetilde{n}-k}\big[1+O\big(|\zeta|^{2}\big)\big],\quad|\zeta|\leqslant 1,\;k\in{\mathbb{N}}\cap[0,\widetilde{n}],

while

\displaystyle\frac{d^{k}}{d\zeta^{k}}H_{\widetilde{n}}^{(1)}(\zeta)\underset{|\zeta|\to 0}{=}\frac{(-1)^{(k-\widetilde{n})/2}k!}{2^{k}\left((k-\widetilde{n})/2\right)!\left((k+\widetilde{n})/2\right)!}\big[1+O\big(|\zeta|^{2}\big)\big]

\displaystyle\quad-i\frac{(-\widetilde{n})_{k}}{2^{k}\pi}(\zeta/2)^{-\widetilde{n}-k}\big[1+O\big(|\zeta|^{2}\big)\big]

\displaystyle\quad+i\frac{2}{\pi\widetilde{n}!}\frac{d^{k}}{d\zeta^{k}}\big[(\zeta/2)^{\widetilde{n}}\text{\rm ln}(\zeta/2)\big]+O\bigg(\frac{d^{k}}{d\zeta^{k}}\big[\zeta^{\widetilde{n}+2}\text{\rm ln}(\zeta)\big]\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{i}{\pi}\frac{\left[\psi\left([(k-\widetilde{n})/2]+1\right)+\psi\left([(k+\widetilde{n})/2]+1\right)\right](-1)^{(k-\widetilde{n})/2}k!}{2^{k}\left((k-\widetilde{n})/2\right)!\left((k+\widetilde{n})/2\right)!}\big[1+O\big(|\zeta|^{2}\big)\big],

\displaystyle\hskip 176.407pt|\zeta|\leqslant 1,\;k\in{\mathbb{N}}\cap(\widetilde{n},\infty),\;\text{$\widetilde{n}+k$ even},

and

\displaystyle\frac{d^{k}}{d\zeta^{k}}H_{\widetilde{n}}^{(1)}(\zeta)\underset{|\zeta|\to 0}{=}\frac{(-1)^{(k-\widetilde{n}+1)/2}(k+1)!}{2^{k+1}\left((k-\widetilde{n}+1)/2\right)!\left((k+\widetilde{n}+1)/2\right)!}\zeta\big[1+O\big(|\zeta|^{2}\big)\big]

\displaystyle\quad-i\frac{(-\widetilde{n})_{k}}{2^{k}\pi}(\zeta/2)^{-\widetilde{n}-k}\big[1+O\big(|\zeta|^{2}\big)\big]

\displaystyle\quad+i\frac{2}{\pi\widetilde{n}!}\frac{d^{k}}{d\zeta^{k}}\big[(\zeta/2)^{\widetilde{n}}\text{\rm ln}(\zeta/2)\big]+O\bigg(\frac{d^{k}}{d\zeta^{k}}\big[\zeta^{\widetilde{n}+2}\text{\rm ln}(\zeta)\big]\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{i}{\pi}\frac{\left[\psi\left([(k-\widetilde{n}+1)/2]+1\right)+\psi\left([(k+\widetilde{n}+1)/2]+1\right)\right](-1)^{(k-\widetilde{n}+1)/2}k!}{2^{k}\left((k-\widetilde{n}+1)/2\right)!\left((k+\widetilde{n}+1)/2\right)!}\zeta

\displaystyle\qquad\times\big[1+O\big(|\zeta|^{2}\big)\big],\quad|\zeta|\leqslant 1,\;k\in{\mathbb{N}}\cap(\widetilde{n},\infty),\;\text{$\widetilde{n}+k$ odd}.

In the case
\widetilde{n}=0
,

\displaystyle\frac{d^{k}}{d\zeta^{k}}H_{0}^{(1)}(\zeta)

\displaystyle\underset{|\zeta|\to 0}{=}\frac{(-1)^{k/2}k!}{2^{k}[(k/2)!]^{2}}\big[1+O\big(|\zeta|^{2}\big)\big]

\displaystyle\qquad\;\;+i\frac{d^{k}}{d\zeta^{k}}\bigg[\frac{2}{\pi}\text{\rm ln}(\zeta/2)+\frac{2\gamma}{\pi}\bigg]+O\bigg(\frac{d^{k}}{d\zeta^{k}}\big[\zeta^{2}\text{\rm ln}(\zeta)\big]\bigg),

(B.71)

\displaystyle\hskip 125.19194pt|\zeta|\leqslant 1,\;k\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0},\,\text{$k$ even},

and

\displaystyle\frac{d^{k}}{d\zeta^{k}}H_{0}^{(1)}(\zeta)

\displaystyle\underset{|\zeta|\to 0}{=}\frac{(-1)^{(k+1)/2}(k+1)!}{2^{k+1}[((k+1)/2)!]^{2}}\zeta\big[1+O\big(|\zeta|^{2}\big)\big]

\displaystyle\qquad\;\;+i\frac{d^{k}}{d\zeta^{k}}\bigg[\frac{2}{\pi}\text{\rm ln}(\zeta/2)+\frac{2\gamma}{\pi}\bigg]+O\bigg(\frac{d^{k}}{d\zeta^{k}}\big[\zeta^{2}\text{\rm ln}(\zeta)\big]\bigg),

(B.72)

\displaystyle\hskip 133.72786pt|\zeta|\leqslant 1,\;k\in{\mathbb{N}},\,\text{$k$ odd}.

Appendix C
Expansions and estimates of the Free Dirac Green’s Matrix

In this section, we investigate the behavior of the Green’s function (
5.15
) of the massless Dirac operator and certain of its partial derivatives with respect to the energy parameter
z\in{\mathbb{C}}\backslash{\mathbb{R}}
. Throughout, we assume that
n\in{\mathbb{N}}\backslash\{1\}
.

By (
5.15
),

\displaystyle G_{0}(z;x,y)

\displaystyle=i2^{-1-(n/2)}\pi^{1-(n/2)}z^{n/2}|x-y|^{1-(n/2)}H_{(n/2)-1}^{(1)}(z|x-y|)I_{N}

(C.1)

\displaystyle\quad-2^{-1-(n/2)}\pi^{1-(n/2)}z^{n/2}|x-y|^{1-(n/2)}H_{n/2}^{(1)}(z|x-y|)\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|}.

Due to the distinct difference in the behavior of the Hankel function
H_{\nu}^{(1)}
for integer and fractional values of
\nu
, we treat separately the cases where:
(\textbf{I})

n
is odd,
(\textbf{II})

n\in{\mathbb{N}}\backslash\{2\}
is even, and
(\textbf{III})

n=2
.

(\textbf{I})
If
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
is odd, then
H_{(n/2)-1}^{(1)}
and
H_{n/2}^{(1)}
are fractional (half-integer) Hankel functions. Applying the identity (cf., e.g.,
[
1
, 9.1.3]
)

H_{\nu}^{(1)}(\zeta)=i[\sin(\nu\pi)]^{-1}\big[e^{-\nu\pi i}J_{\nu}(\zeta)-J_{-\nu}(\zeta)\big],\quad\nu\in{\mathbb{C}},\;\zeta\in{\mathbb{C}}\backslash\{0\},

(C.2)

one obtains

\displaystyle H_{(n/2)-1}^{(1)}(\zeta)

\displaystyle=i[\sin(((n/2)-1)\pi)]^{-1}\Big[e^{-((n/2)-1)\pi i}J_{(n/2)-1}(\zeta)-J_{-(n/2)+1}(\zeta)\Big]

\displaystyle=i(-1)^{(n+1)/2}\Big[-i(-1)^{(n+1)/2}J_{(n/2)-1}(\zeta)-J_{-(n/2)+1}(\zeta)\Big]

\displaystyle=(-1)^{n+1}J_{(n/2)-1}(\zeta)-i(-1)^{(n+1)/2}J_{-(n/2)+1}(\zeta)

\displaystyle=J_{(n/2)-1}(\zeta)-i(-1)^{(n+1)/2}J_{-(n/2)+1}(\zeta).

(C.3)

Similarly,

\displaystyle H_{n/2}^{(1)}(\zeta)

\displaystyle=i[\sin((n/2)\pi)]^{-1}\Big[e^{-n\pi i/2}J_{n/2}(\zeta)-J_{-n/2}(\zeta)\Big]

\displaystyle=i(-1)^{(n-1)/2}\Big[-i(-1)^{(n-1)/2}J_{n/2}(\zeta)-J_{-n/2}(\zeta)\Big]

\displaystyle=(-1)^{n-1}J_{n/2}(\zeta)+i(-1)^{(n+1)/2}J_{-n/2}(\zeta)

\displaystyle=J_{n/2}(\zeta)+i(-1)^{(n+1)/2}J_{-n/2}(\zeta).

(C.4)

The series representation for
J_{\nu}(\zeta)
in (
B.2
) then yields the following expansion:

\displaystyle G_{0}(z;x,y)=i2^{-n}\pi^{1-(n/2)}z^{n-1}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{(-4)^{-k}z^{2k}|x-y|^{2k}}{k!\,\Gamma((n/2)+k))}I_{N}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+4^{-1}(-1)^{(n+1)/2}\pi^{1-(n/2)}z|x-y|^{2-n}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{(-4)^{-k}z^{2k}|x-y|^{2k}}{k!\,\Gamma(-(n/2)+k+2)}I_{N}

\displaystyle\quad\quad-2^{-1-n}\pi^{1-(n/2)}z^{n}|x-y|\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{(-4)^{-k}z^{2k}|x-y|^{2k}}{k!\,\Gamma((n/2)+k+1)}\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|}

\displaystyle\quad\quad-i2^{-1}(-1)^{(n+1)/2}\pi^{1-(n/2)}|x-y|^{1-n}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{(-4)^{-k}z^{2k}|x-y|^{2k}}{k!\,\Gamma(-(n/2)+k+1)}\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|}

\displaystyle\quad=i2^{-n}\pi^{1-(n/2)}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{(-4)^{-k}z^{2k+n-1}|x-y|^{2k}}{k!\,\Gamma((n/2)+k)}I_{N}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+4^{-1}(-1)^{(n+1)/2}\pi^{1-(n/2)}|x-y|^{2-n}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{(-4)^{-k}z^{2k+1}|x-y|^{2k}}{k!\,\Gamma(-(n/2)+k+2)}I_{N}

\displaystyle\quad\quad-2^{-1-n}\pi^{1-(n/2)}|x-y|\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{(-4)^{-k}z^{2k+n}|x-y|^{2k}}{k!\,\Gamma((n/2)+k+1)}\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|}

(C.5)

\displaystyle\quad\quad-i2^{-1}(-1)^{(n+1)/2}\pi^{1-(n/2)}|x-y|^{1-n}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{(-4)^{-k}z^{2k}|x-y|^{2k}}{k!\,\Gamma(-(n/2)+k+1)}\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|}.

The identity in (
C.5
) implies

\displaystyle G_{0}(z;x,y)=-\frac{i(-1)^{(n+1)/2}\pi^{1-(n/2)}}{2\,\Gamma(1-(n/2))}|x-y|^{1-n}\big[1+O\big(z^{2}|x-y|^{2}\big)\big]\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad+\frac{(-1)^{(n+1)/2}\pi^{1-(n/2)}}{4\,\Gamma(2-(n/2))}|x-y|^{2-n}z\big[1+O\big(z^{2}|x-y|^{2}\big)\big]I_{N}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad+\frac{i\pi^{1-(n/2)}}{2^{n}\Gamma(n/2)}z^{n-1}\big[1+O\big(z^{2}|x-y|^{2}\big)\big]I_{N}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad-\frac{\pi^{1-(n/2)}}{2^{1+n}\Gamma(1+(n/2))}|x-y|z^{n}\big[1+O\big(z^{2}|x-y|^{2}\big)\big]\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|}

(C.6)

as
z\to 0
,
z\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}}\big\backslash\{0\}
,
x,y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
,
x\neq y
,
n\in{\mathbb{N}}\backslash\{1\}
odd.

One notes that (
C.6
) implies, together with the identity,

\Gamma(1-z)\Gamma(z)=\frac{\pi}{\sin(\pi z)},\quad z\in{\mathbb{C}}\backslash{\mathbb{Z}},

(C.7)

that

\displaystyle\lim_{\begin{subarray}{c}z\to 0,\\
z\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}}\backslash\{0\}\end{subarray}}G_{0}(z;x,y)

\displaystyle=-\frac{i(-1)^{(n+1)/2}\pi^{1-(n/2)}}{2\,\Gamma(1-(n/2))}|x-y|^{1-n}\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|}

\displaystyle=-\frac{i(-1)^{(n+1)/2}\pi^{1-(n/2)}}{2\sin(n\pi/2)}\Gamma(n/2)|x-y|^{1-n}\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|}

\displaystyle=i2^{-1}\pi^{-n/2}\Gamma(n/2)\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|^{n}},

(C.8)

\displaystyle\hskip-5.69054ptx,y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n},\;x\neq y,\;n\in{\mathbb{N}}\backslash\{1\}\text{ odd,}

consistent with (
5.16
).

Partial derivatives of
G_{0}(z;x,y)
with respect to
z
may be computed by differentiating the series representations in (
C.5
) term-by-term. For
n\in{\mathbb{N}}\backslash\{1\}
odd and
r\in{\mathbb{N}}
with
1\leqslant r\leqslant n
, one obtains

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{r}}{\partial z^{r}}G_{0}(z;x,y)=i2^{-n}\pi^{1-(n/2)}\sum_{k=\delta_{n}(r)}^{\infty}\frac{(-4)^{-k}(2k+n-1)!\,z^{2k+n-1-r}|x-y|^{2k}}{k!\,(2k+n-1-r)!\,\Gamma((n/2)+k)}I_{N}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+4^{-1}(-1)^{(n+1)/2}\pi^{1-(n/2)}|x-y|^{2-n}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\times\sum_{k=k_{-}(r)}^{\infty}\frac{(-4)^{-k}(2k+1)!\,z^{2k+1-r}|x-y|^{2k}}{k!\,(2k+1-r)!\,\Gamma(-(n/2)+k+2)}I_{N}

\displaystyle\quad\quad-2^{-1-n}\pi^{1-(n/2)}|x-y|\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{(-4)^{-k}(2k+n)!\,z^{2k+n-r}|x-y|^{2k}}{k!\,(2k+n-r)!\,\Gamma((n/2)+k+1)}\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|}

\displaystyle\quad\quad-i2^{-1}(-1)^{(n+1)/2}\pi^{1-(n/2)}|x-y|^{1-n}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\times\sum_{k=k_{+}(r)}^{\infty}\frac{(-4)^{-k}(2k)!\,z^{2k-r}|x-y|^{2k}}{k!\,(2k-r)!\,\Gamma(-(n/2)+k+1)}\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|},

(C.9)

where

k_{\pm}(r):=\begin{cases}(r\pm 1)/2,&\text{$r$ odd},\\
r/2,&\text{$r$ even},\end{cases}\quad 1\leqslant r\leqslant n,

(C.10)

and
\delta_{n}
is the Kronecker delta function,

\delta_{n}(r)=\begin{cases}1,&r=n,\\
0,&1\leqslant r\leqslant n-1,\end{cases}\quad 1\leqslant r\leqslant n.

(C.11)

The expansion in (
C.9
) implies the following asymptotics of
\frac{\partial^{r}}{\partial z^{r}}G_{0}(z;x,y)
as
z\to 0
:

(i)
If
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
is odd and
1\leqslant r\leqslant n-2
is odd, then

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{r}}{\partial z^{r}}G_{0}(z;x,y)=\frac{i2^{-n}\pi^{1-(n/2)}(n-1)!}{(n-1-r)!\Gamma(n/2)}z^{n-1-r}\big[1+O\big(z^{2}|x-y|^{2}\big)\big]I_{N}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\frac{(-1)^{(n+1)/2}\pi^{1-(n/2)}(-4)^{-(r+1)/2}r!}{[(r-1)/2]!\,\Gamma(-(n/2)+((r-1)/2)+2)}|x-y|^{1+r-n}\big[1+O\big(z^{2}|x-y|^{2}\big)\big]I_{N}

\displaystyle\quad\quad-\frac{2^{-1-n}\pi^{1-(n/2)}n!}{(n-r)!\,\Gamma(1+(n/2))}|x-y|z^{n-r}\big[1+O\big(z^{2}|x-y|^{2}\big)\big]\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|}

\displaystyle\quad\quad-\frac{i2^{-1}(-1)^{(n+1)/2}\pi^{1-(n/2)}(-4)^{-(r+1)/2}(r+1)!}{[(r+1)/2]!\,\Gamma(-(n/2)+((r+1)/2)+1)}|x-y|^{2+r-n}z

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\times\big[1+O\big(z^{2}|x-y|^{2}\big)\big]\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|}

(C.12)

\displaystyle\,\text{as $z\to 0$, $z\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}}\big\backslash\{0\}$, $x,y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}$, $x\neq y$}.

(ii)
If
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
is odd and
1\leqslant r\leqslant n-1
is even, then

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{r}}{\partial z^{r}}G_{0}(z;x,y)=\frac{i2^{-n}\pi^{1-(n/2)}(n-1)!}{(n-1-r)!\Gamma(n/2)}z^{n-1-r}\big[1+O\big(z^{2}|x-y|^{2}\big)\big]I_{N}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\frac{4^{-1}(-1)^{(n+1)/2}\pi^{1-(n/2)}(-4)^{-r/2}(r+1)!}{(r/2)!\,\Gamma(-(n/2)+(r/2)+2)}|x-y|^{2+r-n}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\times z\big[1+O\big(z^{2}|x-y|^{2}\big)\big]I_{N}

\displaystyle\quad\quad-\frac{2^{-1-n}\pi^{1-(n/2)}n!}{(n-r)!\,\Gamma(1+(n/2))}|x-y|z^{n-r}\big[1+O\big(z^{2}|x-y|^{2}\big)\big]\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|}

\displaystyle\quad\quad-\frac{i2^{-1}(-1)^{(n+1)/2}\pi^{1-(n/2)}(-4)^{-r/2}r!}{(r/2)!\,\Gamma(-(n/2)+(r/2)+1)}|x-y|^{1+r-n}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\times\big[1+O\big(z^{2}|x-y|^{2}\big)\big]\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|}

(C.13)

\displaystyle\,\text{as $z\to 0$, $z\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}}\big\backslash\{0\}$, $x,y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}$, $x\neq y$}.

(iii)
If
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
is odd, then

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{n}}{\partial z^{n}}G_{0}(z;x,y)=-\frac{i2^{-n}\pi^{1-(n/2)}}{4\Gamma(1+(n/2))}|x-y|^{2}z\big[1+O\big(z^{2}|x-y|^{2}\big)\big]I_{N}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\frac{2(-1)^{(n+1)/2}\pi^{(1-n)/2}(-4)^{-(n+1)/2}n!}{[(n-1)/2]!}|x-y|\big[1+O\big(z^{2}|x-y|^{2}\big)\big]I_{N}

\displaystyle\quad\quad-\frac{2^{-1-n}\pi^{1-(n/2)}n!}{\Gamma(1+(n/2))}|x-y|\big[1+O\big(z^{2}|x-y|^{2}\big)\big]\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|}

\displaystyle\quad\quad-\frac{i(-1)^{(n+1)/2}\pi^{(1-n)/2}(-4)^{-(n+1)/2}(n+1)!}{[(n+1)/2]!}|x-y|^{2}z

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\times\big[1+O\big(z^{2}|x-y|^{2}\big)\big]\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|}

(C.14)

\displaystyle\,\text{as $z\to 0$, $z\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}}\big\backslash\{0\}$, $x,y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}$, $x\neq y$}.

(\textbf{II})
If
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
is even, then the indices of the Hankel functions
H_{(n/2)-1}^{(1)}
and
H_{n/2}^{(1)}
are nonnegative integers. Due to the the difference in behavior of
Y_{n}
,
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
, and
Y_{0}
(cf. (
B.4
) and (
B.5
)) we distinguish two cases:
n\geqslant 4
and
n=2
. First we treat the case
n\geqslant 4
.

Combining (
B.1
), (
B.2
), and (
B.4
), one obtains for
n\geqslant 4
:

\displaystyle H_{(n/2)-1}^{(1)}(\zeta)=J_{(n/2)-1}(\zeta)+iY_{(n/2)-1}(\zeta)

\displaystyle\quad=2^{1-(n/2)}\zeta^{(n/2)-1}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{(-4)^{-k}\zeta^{2k}}{k!\,\Gamma((n/2)+k)}

\displaystyle\qquad-i2^{(n/2)-1}\pi^{-1}\zeta^{1-(n/2)}\sum_{k=0}^{(n/2)-2}\frac{((n/2)-k-2)!4^{-k}\zeta^{2k}}{k!}

\displaystyle\qquad+i2\pi^{-1}\text{\rm ln}(\zeta/2)2^{1-(n/2)}\zeta^{(n/2)-1}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{(-4)^{-k}\zeta^{2k}}{k!\,\Gamma((n/2)+k)}

\displaystyle\qquad-i2^{1-(n/2)}\pi^{-1}\zeta^{(n/2)-1}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}[\psi(k+1)+\psi((n/2)+k)]\frac{(-4)^{-k}\zeta{2k}}{k!\,((n/2)-1+k)!}

\displaystyle\quad=2^{1-n/2}\zeta^{(n/2)-1}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{(-4)^{-k}\zeta^{2k}}{k!\,((n/2)+k-1)!}

\displaystyle\qquad-i2^{(n/2)-1}\pi^{-1}\zeta^{1-(n/2)}\sum_{k=0}^{(n/2)-2}\frac{((n/2)-k-2)!4^{-k}\zeta^{2k}}{k!}

\displaystyle\qquad+i2\pi^{-1}\text{\rm ln}(\zeta/2)2^{1-(n/2)}\zeta^{(n/2)-1}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{(-4)^{-k}\zeta^{2k}}{k!\,((n/2)+k-1)!}

(C.15)

\displaystyle\qquad-i2^{1-(n/2)}\pi^{-1}\zeta^{(n/2)-1}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}[\psi(k+1)+\psi((n/2)+k)]\frac{(-4)^{-k}\zeta{2k}}{k!\,((n/2)-1+k)!}.

Next, for any even
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
,

\displaystyle H_{(n/2)}^{(1)}(\zeta)

\displaystyle=2^{-n/2}\zeta^{n/2}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{(-4)^{-k}\zeta^{2k}}{k!\,((n/2)+k)!}

\displaystyle\quad-i2^{n/2}\pi^{-1}\zeta^{-n/2}\sum_{k=0}^{(n/2)-1}\frac{((n/2)-k-1)!4^{-k}\zeta^{2k}}{k!}

\displaystyle\quad+i2^{1-(n/2)}\pi^{-1}\text{\rm ln}(\zeta/2)\zeta^{n/2}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{(-4)^{-k}\zeta^{2k}}{k!\,((n/2)+k)!}

(C.16)

\displaystyle\quad-i2^{-n/2}\pi^{-1}\zeta^{n/2}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}[\psi(k+1)+\psi(n/2+k+1)]\frac{(-4)^{-k}\zeta^{2k}}{k!\,((n/2)+k)!}.

Substitution of (
C.15
) and (
C.16
) into (
C.1
) then yields for even
n\geqslant 4
,

\displaystyle G_{0}(z;x,y)=i2^{-n}\pi^{1-(n/2)}z^{n-1}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{(-4)^{-k}z^{2k}|x-y|^{2k}}{k!\,((n/2)+k-1)!}I_{N}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+4^{-1}\pi^{-n/2}z|x-y|^{2-n}\sum_{k=0}^{(n/2)-2}\frac{((n/2)-k-2)!\,4^{-k}z^{2k}|x-y|^{2k}}{k!}I_{N}

\displaystyle\quad\quad-2^{1-n}\pi^{-n/2}z^{n-1}\text{\rm ln}(z|x-y|/2)\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{(-4)^{-k}z^{2k}|x-y|^{2k}}{k!\,((n/2)+k-1)!}I_{N}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+2^{-n}\pi^{-n/2}z^{n-1}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}[\psi(k+1)+\psi((n/2)+k)]\frac{(-4)^{-k}z^{2k}|x-y|^{2k}}{k!\,((n/2)-1+k)!}I_{N}

\displaystyle\quad\quad-2^{-1-n}\pi^{1-(n/2)}z^{n}|x-y|\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{(-4)^{-k}z^{2k}|x-y|^{2k}}{k!\,((n/2)+k)!}\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+i2^{-1}\pi^{-n/2}|x-y|^{1-n}\sum_{k=0}^{(n/2)-1}\frac{((n/2)-k-1)!\,4^{-k}z^{2k}|x-y|^{2k}}{k!}\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|}

\displaystyle\quad\quad-i2^{-n}\pi^{-n/2}z^{n}|x-y|\text{\rm ln}(z|x-y|/2)\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{(-4)^{-k}z^{2k}|x-y|^{2k}}{k!\,((n/2)+k)!}\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+i2^{-1-n}\pi^{-n/2}z^{n}|x-y|\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}[\psi(k+1)+\psi((n/2)+k+1)]\frac{(-4)^{-k}z^{2k}|x-y|^{2k}}{k!\,((n/2)+k)!}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\times\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|}

\displaystyle\quad=i2^{-n}\pi^{1-(n/2)}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{(-4)^{-k}z^{2k+n-1}|x-y|^{2k}}{k!\,((n/2)+k-1)!}I_{N}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+4^{-1}\pi^{-n/2}|x-y|^{2-n}\sum_{k=0}^{(n/2)-2}\frac{((n/2)-k-2)!\,4^{-k}z^{2k+1}|x-y|^{2k}}{k!}I_{N}

\displaystyle\quad\quad-2^{1-n}\pi^{-n/2}\text{\rm ln}(z|x-y|/2)\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{(-4)^{-k}z^{2k+n-1}|x-y|^{2k}}{k!\,((n/2)+k-1)!}I_{N}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+2^{-n}\pi^{-n/2}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}[\psi(k+1)+\psi((n/2)+k)]\frac{(-4)^{-k}z^{2k+n-1}|x-y|^{2k}}{k!\,((n/2)-1+k)!}I_{N}

\displaystyle\quad\quad-2^{-1-n}\pi^{1-(n/2)}|x-y|\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{(-4)^{-k}z^{2k+n}|x-y|^{2k}}{k!\,((n/2)+k)!}\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+i2^{-1}\pi^{-n/2}|x-y|^{1-n}\sum_{k=0}^{(n/2)-1}\frac{((n/2)-k-1)!\,4^{-k}z^{2k}|x-y|^{2k}}{k!}\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|}

\displaystyle\quad\quad-i2^{-n}\pi^{-n/2}|x-y|\text{\rm ln}(z|x-y|/2)\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{(-4)^{-k}z^{2k+n}|x-y|^{2k}}{k!\,((n/2)+k)!}\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+i2^{-1-n}\pi^{-n/2}|x-y|\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}[\psi(k+1)+\psi((n/2)+k+1)]\frac{(-4)^{-k}z^{2k+n}|x-y|^{2k}}{k!\,((n/2)+k)!}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\times\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|}.

(C.17)

The identity in (
C.17
) implies

\displaystyle G_{0}(z;x,y)=\frac{i}{2^{n}\pi^{(n/2)-1}((n/2)-1)!}z^{n-1}\big[1+O\big(z^{2}|x-y|^{2}\big)\big]I_{N}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\frac{((n/2)-2)!}{4\pi^{n/2}}|x-y|^{2-n}z\big[1+O\big(z^{2}|x-y|^{2}\big)\big]I_{N}

\displaystyle\quad\quad-\frac{1}{2^{n}\pi^{n/2}((n/2)-1)!}\text{\rm ln}(z|x-y|/2)z^{n-1}\big[1+O\big(z^{2}|x-y|^{2}\big)\big]I_{N}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\frac{\psi(1)+\psi(n/2)}{2^{n}\pi^{n/2}}z^{n-1}\big[1+O\big(z^{2}|x-y|^{2}\big)\big]I_{N}

\displaystyle\quad\quad-\frac{1}{2^{1+n}\pi^{(n/2)-1}(n/2)!}|x-y|z^{n}\big[1+O\big(z^{2}|x-y|^{2}\big)\big]\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\frac{i((n/2)-1)!}{2\pi^{n/2}}|x-y|^{1-n}\big[1+O\big(z^{2}|x-y|^{2}\big)\big]\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|}

\displaystyle\quad\quad-\frac{i}{2^{n}\pi^{n/2}(n/2)!}|x-y|z^{n}\text{\rm ln}(z|x-y|/2)\big[1+O\big(z^{2}|x-y|^{2}\big)\big]\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\frac{i[\psi(1)+\psi((n/2)+1)]}{2^{1+n}\pi^{n/2}(n/2)!}|x-y|z^{n}\big[1+O\big(z^{2}|x-y|^{2}\big)\big]\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|}

(C.18)

as
z\to 0
,
z\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}}\big\backslash\{0\}
,
x,y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
,
x\neq y
, and
n\in{\mathbb{N}}\backslash\{2\}
even.

One notes that (
C.18
) implies, together with
(n/2-1)!=\Gamma(n/2)
, that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\lim_{\begin{subarray}{c}z\to 0,\\
z\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}}\backslash\{0\}\end{subarray}}G_{0}(z;x,y)&=i2^{-1}\pi^{-n/2}\Gamma(n/2)\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|^{n}},\\
&\hskip-8.53581ptx,y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n},\;x\neq y,\;n\in{\mathbb{N}}\backslash\{2\}\text{ even,}\end{split}

(C.19)

consistent with (
5.16
).

For
n\in{\mathbb{N}}\backslash\{2\}
even and
r\in{\mathbb{N}}
with
1\leqslant r\leqslant n
, term-by-term differentiation of (
C.17
) implies

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{r}}{\partial z^{r}}G_{0}(z;x,y)=\Bigg[i2^{-n}\pi^{1-(n/2)}\sum_{k=\delta_{n}(r)}^{\infty}\frac{(-4)^{-k}(2k+n-1)!\,z^{2k+n-1-r}|x-y|^{2k}}{k!\,(2k+n-1-r)!\,((n/2)+k-1)!}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\frac{|x-y|^{2-n}}{4\pi^{n/2}}\chi_{{}_{\leqslant n-3}}(r)\sum_{k=k_{-}(r)}^{(n/2)-2}\frac{((n/2)-k-2)!\,4^{-k}(2k+1)!\,z^{2k+1-r}|x-y|^{2k}}{k!\,(2k+1-r)!}

\displaystyle\quad\quad-2^{1-n}\pi^{-n/2}\text{\rm ln}(z|x-y|/2)\sum_{k=\delta_{n}(r)}^{\infty}\frac{(-4)^{-k}(2k+n-1)!\,z^{2k+n-1-r}|x-y|^{2k}}{k!\,(2k+n-1-r)!\,((n/2)+k-1)!}

\displaystyle\quad\quad-2^{1-n}\pi^{-n/2}\sum_{\ell=0}^{r-1}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\begin{pmatrix}r\\
\ell\end{pmatrix}(-1)^{1+r-\ell}(r-\ell-1)!

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\times\frac{(-4)^{-k}(2k+n-1)!\,z^{2k+n-1-r}|x-y|^{2k}}{k!\,(2k+n-1-\ell)!\,((n/2)+k-1)!}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+2^{-n}\pi^{-n/2}\sum_{k=\delta_{n}(r)}^{\infty}[\psi(k+1)+\psi((n/2)+k)]

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\times\frac{(-4)^{-k}(2k+n-1)!\,z^{2k+n-1-r}|x-y|^{2k}}{k!\,(2k+n-1-r)!\,((n/2)+k-1)!}\Bigg]I_{N}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\Bigg[-2^{-1-n}\pi^{1-(n/2)}|x-y|\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{(-4)^{-k}(2k+n)!\,z^{2k+n-r}|x-y|^{2k}}{k!\,(2k+n-r)!\,((n/2)+k)!}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad+i\frac{|x-y|^{1-n}}{2\pi^{n/2}}\chi_{{}_{\leqslant n-2}}(r)\sum_{k=k_{+}(r)}^{(n/2)-1}\frac{((n/2)-k-1)!\,4^{-k}(2k)!\,z^{2k-r}|x-y|^{2k}}{k!\,(2k-r)!}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad-i2^{-n}\pi^{-n/2}|x-y|\text{\rm ln}(z|x-y|/2)\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{(-4)^{-k}(2k+n)!\,z^{2k+n-r}|x-y|^{2k}}{k!\,((n/2)+k)!\,(2k+n-r)!}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad-i2^{-n}\pi^{-n/2}|x-y|\sum_{\ell=0}^{r-1}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\begin{pmatrix}r\\
\ell\end{pmatrix}(-1)^{1+r-\ell}(r-\ell-1)!

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\times\frac{(-4)^{-k}(2k+n)!\,z^{2k+n-r}|x-y|^{2k}}{k!\,((n/2)+k)!\,(2k+n-\ell)!}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad+i2^{-1-n}\pi^{-n/2}|x-y|\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}[\psi(k+1)+\psi((n/2)+k+1)]

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\times\frac{(-4)^{-k}(2k+n)!z^{2k+n-r}|x-y|^{2k}}{k!\,((n/2)+k)!}\Bigg]\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|},

(C.20)

where
\chi_{{}_{\leqslant a}}
,
a\in{\mathbb{R}}
, denotes the characteristic (i.e., indicator) function of the interval
(-\infty,a]
. That is,

\chi_{{}_{\leqslant a}}(x)=\begin{cases}1,&x\in(-\infty,a],\\
0,&x\in(a,\infty),\end{cases}\quad x\in{\mathbb{R}}.

(C.21)

The expansion in (
C.20
) implies the following asymptotics of
\frac{\partial^{r}}{\partial z^{r}}G_{0}(z;x,y)
as
z\to 0
:

(i)
If
n\in{\mathbb{N}}\backslash\{2\}
is even and
1\leqslant r\leqslant n-1
is odd, then

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{r}}{\partial z^{r}}G_{0}(z;x,y)=\frac{i2^{-n}\pi^{1-(n/2)}(n-1)!}{(n-1-r)!\,((n/2)-1)!}z^{n-1-r}\big[1+O\big(z^{2}|x-y|^{2}\big)\big]I_{N}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\chi_{{}_{\leqslant n-3}}(r)\frac{4^{-(1+r)/2}\pi^{-n/2}[(n-r-3)/2]!\,r!}{[(r-1)/2]!}|x-y|^{1+r-n}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\times\big[1+O\big(z^{2}|x-y|^{2}\big)\big]I_{N}

\displaystyle\quad\quad-\frac{2^{1-n}\pi^{-n/2}(n-1)!}{(n-1-r)!\,((n/2)-1)!}z^{n-1-r}\text{\rm ln}(z|x-y|/2)\big[1+O\big(z^{2}|x-y|^{2}\big)\big]I_{N}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\frac{2^{1-n}}{\pi^{n/2}}\sum_{\ell=0}^{r-1}\begin{pmatrix}r\\
\ell\end{pmatrix}(-1)^{r-\ell}\frac{(r-\ell-1)!\,(n-1)!}{(n-1-\ell)!\,((n/2)-1)!}z^{n-1-r}

\displaystyle\hskip 73.97733pt\times\big[1+O\big(z^{2}|x-y|^{2}\big)\big]I_{N}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\frac{2^{-n}\pi^{-n/2}[\psi(1)+\psi(n/2)](n-1)!}{(n-1-r)!\,((n/2)-1)!}z^{n-1-r}\big[1+O\big(z^{2}|x-y|^{2}\big)\big]I_{N}

\displaystyle\quad\quad-\frac{2^{-(1+n)}\pi^{1-(n/2)}n!}{(n-r)!\,(n/2)!}|x-y|z^{n-r}\big[1+O\big(z^{2}|x-y|^{2}\big)\big]\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\chi_{{}_{\leqslant n-2}}(r)\frac{i\pi^{-n/2}[(n-r-3)/2]!\,(r+1)!}{4^{1+(r/2)}[(r+1)/2]!}|x-y|^{r+2-n}z

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\times\big[1+O\big(z^{2}|x-y|^{2}\big)\big]\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|}

\displaystyle\quad\quad-\frac{i2^{-n}\pi^{-n/2}n!}{(n/2)!(n-r)!}|x-y|z^{n-r}\text{\rm ln}(z|x-y|/2)\big[1+O\big(z^{2}|x-y|^{2}\big)\big]\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+i2^{-n}\pi^{-n/2}\sum_{\ell=0}^{r-1}\begin{pmatrix}r\\
\ell\end{pmatrix}\frac{(-1)^{r-\ell}(r-\ell-1)!\,n!}{(n/2)!\,(n-\ell)!}|x-y|z^{n-r}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\times\big[1+O\big(z^{2}|x-y|^{2}\big)\big]\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\frac{i[\psi(1)+\psi((n/2)+1)]n!}{2^{1+n}\pi^{n/2}(n/2)!}|x-y|z^{n-r}\big[1+O\big(z^{2}|x-y|^{2}\big)\big]\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|}

(C.22)

\displaystyle\hskip 147.95424pt\text{as $z\to 0$, $z\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}}\big\backslash\{0\}$, $x,y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}$, $x\neq y$}.

(ii)
If
n\in{\mathbb{N}}\backslash\{2\}
is even and
1\leqslant r\leqslant n-2
is even with
r\neq n
, then

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{r}}{\partial z^{r}}G_{0}(z;x,y)=\frac{i2^{-n}\pi^{1-(n/2)}(n-1)!}{(n-1-r)!\,((n/2)-1)!}z^{n-1-r}\big[1+O\big(z^{2}|x-y|^{2}\big)\big]I_{N}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\chi_{{}_{\leqslant n-3}}(r)\frac{((n/2)-(r/2)-2)!\,(r+1)!}{4^{1+(r/2)}\pi^{n/2}(r/2)!}|x-y|^{2+r-n}z\big[1+O\big(z^{2}|x-y|^{2}\big)\big]I_{N}

\displaystyle\quad\quad-\frac{2^{1-n}\pi^{-n/2}(n-1)!}{(n-1-r)!\,((n/2)-1)!}z^{n-1-r}\text{\rm ln}(z|x-y|/2)\big[1+O\big(z^{2}|x-y|^{2}\big)\big]I_{N}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\frac{2^{1-n}}{\pi^{n/2}}\sum_{\ell=0}^{r-1}\begin{pmatrix}r\\
\ell\end{pmatrix}(-1)^{r-\ell}\frac{(r-\ell-1)!\,(n-1)!}{(n-1-\ell)!\,((n/2)-1)!}z^{n-1-r}

\displaystyle\hskip 73.97733pt\times\big[1+O\big(z^{2}|x-y|^{2}\big)\big]I_{N}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\frac{2^{-n}\pi^{-n/2}[\psi(1)+\psi(n/2)](n-1)!}{(n-1-r)!\,((n/2)-1)!}z^{n-1-r}\big[1+O\big(z^{2}|x-y|^{2}\big)\big]I_{N}

\displaystyle\quad\quad-\frac{2^{-(1+n)}\pi^{1-(n/2)}n!}{(n-r)!\,(n/2)!}|x-y|z^{n-r}\big[1+O\big(z^{2}|x-y|^{2}\big)\big]\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\chi_{{}_{\leqslant n-2}}(r)\frac{i((n/2)-(r/2)-1)!\,r!}{4^{(r+1)/2}\pi^{n/2}(r/2)!}|x-y|^{1+r-n}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\times\big[1+O\big(z^{2}|x-y|^{2}\big)\big]\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|}

\displaystyle\quad\quad-\frac{i2^{-n}\pi^{-n/2}n!}{(n/2)!(n-r)!}|x-y|z^{n-r}\text{\rm ln}(z|x-y|/2)\big[1+O\big(z^{2}|x-y|^{2}\big)\big]\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+i2^{-n}\pi^{-n/2}\sum_{\ell=0}^{r-1}\begin{pmatrix}r\\
\ell\end{pmatrix}\frac{(-1)^{r-\ell}(r-\ell-1)!\,n!}{(n/2)!\,(r-\ell)!}|x-y|z^{n-r}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\times\big[1+O\big(z^{2}|x-y|^{2}\big)\big]\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\frac{i[\psi(1)+\psi((n/2)+1)]n!}{2^{1+n}\pi^{n/2}(n/2)!}|x-y|z^{n-r}\big[1+O\big(z^{2}|x-y|^{2}\big)\big]\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|}

(C.23)

\displaystyle\hskip 146.53151pt\text{as $z\to 0$, $z\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}}\big\backslash\{0\}$, $x,y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}$, $x\neq y$}.

(iii)
If
n\in{\mathbb{N}}\backslash\{2\}
is even, then

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{n}}{\partial z^{n}}G_{0}(z;x,y)=-\frac{i\pi^{1-(n/2)}(n+1)!}{2^{n+2}(n/2)!}|x-y|^{2}z\big[1+O\big(z^{2}|x-y|^{2}\big)\big]I_{N}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\frac{2^{1-n}\pi^{-n/2}4^{-1}(n+1)!}{(n/2)!}|x-y|^{2}z\text{\rm ln}(z|x-y|/2)\big[1+O\big(z^{2}|x-y|^{2}\big)\big]I_{N}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\frac{(n-1)!}{2^{n-1}\pi^{n/2}(\frac{n}{2}-1)!}\Bigg(\sum_{\ell=0}^{n-1}\begin{pmatrix}n\\
\ell\end{pmatrix}(-1)^{\ell}\Bigg)z^{-1}\big[1+O\big(z^{2}|x-y|^{2}\big)\big]I_{N}

\displaystyle\quad\quad-\frac{[\psi(2)+\psi((n/2)+1)](n+1)!}{2^{n+2}\pi^{n/2}(n/2)!}|x-y|^{2}z\big[1+O\big(z^{2}|x-y|^{2}\big)\big]I_{N}

\displaystyle\quad\quad-\frac{\pi^{1-(n/2)}n!}{2^{n+1}(n/2)!}|x-y|\big[1+O\big(z^{2}|x-y|^{2}\big)\big]\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|}

\displaystyle\quad\quad-\frac{i2^{-n}\pi^{-n/2}n!}{(n/2)!}|x-y|\text{\rm ln}(z|x-y|/2)\big[1+O\big(z^{2}|x-y|^{2}\big)\big]\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\frac{i2^{-n}\pi^{-n/2}n!}{(n/2)!}\Bigg(\sum_{\ell=0}^{n-1}\begin{pmatrix}n\\
\ell\end{pmatrix}\frac{(-1)^{\ell}}{n-\ell}\Bigg)|x-y|

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\times\big[1+O\big(z^{2}|x-y|^{2}\big)\big]\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\frac{i[\psi(1)+\psi((n/2)+1)]n!}{2^{1+n}\pi^{n/2}(n/2)!}|x-y|\big[1+O\big(z^{2}|x-y|^{2}\big)\big]\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|}

(C.24)

\displaystyle\hskip 126.61467pt\text{as $z\to 0$, $z\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}}\big\backslash\{0\}$, $x,y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}$, $x\neq y$}.

(\textbf{III})
If
n=2
, then (
B.1
), (
B.2
), and (
B.5
) imply

\displaystyle H_{(n/2)-1}^{(1)}(\zeta)=H_{0}^{(1)}(\zeta)=J_{0}(\zeta)+iY_{0}(\zeta)

\displaystyle\quad=i\frac{2}{\pi}[\text{\rm ln}(\zeta/2)+\gamma_{E-M}-i(\pi/2)]J_{0}(\zeta)-\frac{2}{\pi}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\bigg(\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\frac{1}{\ell}\bigg)\frac{(-4)^{-k}\zeta^{2k}}{(k!)^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad=i\frac{2}{\pi}[\text{\rm ln}(\zeta/2)+\gamma_{E-M}-i(\pi/2)]\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{(-4)^{-k}\zeta^{2k}}{(k!)^{2}}-\frac{2}{\pi}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\bigg(\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\frac{1}{\ell}\bigg)\frac{(-4)^{-k}\zeta^{2k}}{(k!)^{2}}.

(C.25)

Similarly, by combining (
C.1
), (
C.16
) (which is valid for
n=2
), and (
C.25
), one obtains for
n=2
:

\displaystyle G_{0}(z;x,y)=i4^{-1}zH_{0}^{(1)}(z|x-y|)I_{N}-4^{-1}zH_{1}^{(1)}(z|x-y|)\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|}

\displaystyle\quad=-\frac{1}{2\pi}[\text{\rm ln}(z|x-y|/2)+\gamma_{E-M}-i(\pi/2)]\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{(-4)^{-k}z^{2k+1}|x-y|^{2k}}{(k!)^{2}}I_{N}

\displaystyle\quad\quad-\frac{i}{2\pi}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\bigg(\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\frac{1}{\ell}\bigg)\frac{(-4)^{-k}z^{2k+1}|x-y|^{2k}}{(k!)^{2}}I_{N}

\displaystyle\quad\quad-\frac{1}{8}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{(-4)^{-k}z^{2k+2}|x-y|^{2k+1}}{k!(k+1)!}\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\frac{i}{2\pi}|x-y|^{-1}\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|}

\displaystyle\quad\quad-\frac{i}{4\pi}\text{\rm ln}(z|x-y|/2)\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{(-4)^{-k}z^{2k+2}|x-y|^{2k+1}}{k!\,(k+1)!}\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\frac{i}{8\pi}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}[\psi(k+1)+\psi(k+2)]\frac{(-4)^{-k}z^{2k+2}|x-y|^{2k+1}}{k!\,(k+1)!}\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|}.

(C.26)

The identity in (
C.26
) implies

\displaystyle G_{0}(z;x,y)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2\pi}z\text{\rm ln}(z|x-y|/2)\big[1+O\big(z^{2}|x-y|^{2}\big)\big]I_{N}

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{2\pi}[\gamma_{E-M}-i(\pi/2)]z\big[1+O\big(z^{2}|x-y|^{2}\big)\big]I_{N}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{i}{8\pi}z^{3}|x-y|^{2}\big[1+O\big(z^{2}|x-y|^{2}\big)\big]I_{N}

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{1}{8}z^{2}|x-y|\big[1+O\big(z^{2}|x-y|^{2}\big)\big]\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{i}{2\pi}|x-y|^{-1}\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|}

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{i}{4\pi}z^{2}|x-y|\text{\rm ln}(z|x-y|/2)\big[1+O\big(z^{2}|x-y|^{2}\big)\big]\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{i}{8\pi}[\psi(1)+\psi(2)]z^{2}|x-y|\big[1+O\big(z^{2}|x-y|^{2}\big)\big]\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|}

(C.27)

as
z\to 0
,
z\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}}\big\backslash\{0\}
,
x,y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
,
x\neq y
, and
n=2
.

One notes that (
C.27
) implies

\lim_{\begin{subarray}{c}z\to 0,\\
z\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}}\backslash\{0\}\end{subarray}}G_{0}(z;x,y)=\frac{i}{2\pi}\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|^{2}},\quad x,y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2},\;x\neq y,

(C.28)

which is consistent with (
5.16
).

Finally, one employs (
C.26
) to compute:

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial z}G_{0}(z;x,y)=-\frac{1}{2\pi}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{(-4)^{-k}z^{2k}|x-y|^{2k}}{(k!)^{2}}I_{N}

\displaystyle\quad\quad-\frac{1}{2\pi}[\text{\rm ln}(z|x-y|/2)+\gamma_{E-M}-i(\pi/2)]\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{(-4)^{-k}(2k+1)z^{2k}|x-y|^{2k}}{(k!)^{2}}I_{N}

\displaystyle\quad\quad-\frac{i}{2\pi}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\bigg(\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\frac{1}{\ell}\bigg)\frac{(-4)^{-k}(2k+1)z^{2k}|x-y|^{2k}}{(k!)^{2}}I_{N}

\displaystyle\quad\quad-\frac{1}{8}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{(-4)^{-k}(2k+2)z^{2k+1}|x-y|^{2k+1}}{k!\,(k+1)!}\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|}

\displaystyle\quad\quad-\frac{i}{4\pi}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{(-4)^{-k}z^{2k+1}|x-y|^{2k+1}}{k!\,(k+1)!}\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|}

\displaystyle\quad\quad-\frac{i}{4\pi}\text{\rm ln}(z|x-y|/2)\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{(-4)^{-k}(2k+2)z^{2k+1}|x-y|^{2k+1}}{k!\,(k+1)!}\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|}

(C.29)

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\frac{i}{8\pi}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}[\psi(k+1)+\psi(k+2)]\frac{(-4)^{-k}(2k+2)z^{2k+1}|x-y|^{2k+1}}{k!\,(k+1)!}\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|},

and

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial z^{2}}G_{0}(z;x,y)=-\frac{1}{2\pi}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\frac{(-4)^{-k}(2k)z^{2k-1}|x-y|^{2k}}{(k!)^{2}}I_{N}

\displaystyle\quad\quad-\frac{1}{2\pi}z^{-1}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{(-4)^{-k}(2k+1)z^{2k}|x-y|^{2k}}{(k!)^{2}}I_{N}

\displaystyle\quad\quad-\frac{1}{2\pi}[\text{\rm ln}(z|x-y|/2)+\gamma_{E-M}-i(\pi/2)]

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\times\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\frac{(-4)^{-k}(2k+1)(2k)z^{2k-1}|x-y|^{2k}}{(k!)^{2}}I_{N}

\displaystyle\quad\quad-\frac{i}{2\pi}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\bigg(\sum_{\ell=1}^{k}\frac{1}{\ell}\bigg)\frac{(-4)^{-k}(2k+1)(2k)z^{2k-1}|x-y|^{2k}}{(k!)^{2}}I_{N}

\displaystyle\quad\quad-\frac{1}{8}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{(-4)^{-k}(2k+2)(2k+1)z^{2k}|x-y|^{2k+1}}{k!\,(k+1)!}\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|}

\displaystyle\quad\quad-\frac{i}{4\pi}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{(-4)^{-k}(2k+1)z^{2k}|x-y|^{2k+1}}{k!\,(k+1)!}\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|}

\displaystyle\quad\quad-\frac{i}{4\pi}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{(-4)^{-k}(2k+2)z^{2k}|x-y|^{2k+1}}{k!\,(k+1)!}\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|}

\displaystyle\quad\quad-\frac{i}{4\pi}\text{\rm ln}(z|x-y|/2)\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{(-4)^{-k}(2k+2)(2k+1)z^{2k}|x-y|^{2k+1}}{k!\,(k+1)!}\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\frac{i}{8\pi}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}[\psi(k+1)+\psi(k+2)]\frac{(-4)^{-k}(2k+2)(2k+1)z^{2k}|x-y|^{2k+1}}{k!\,(k+1)!}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\times\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|}.

(C.30)

Finally, the expansions in (
C.29
) and (
C.30
) imply the following asymptotics of

\frac{\partial^{r}}{\partial z^{r}}G_{0}(z;x,y)
,
1\leqslant r\leqslant 2
, as
z\to 0
:

(i)
If
n=2
,
r=1
, then

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial z}G_{0}(z;x,y)=-\frac{1}{2\pi}[\text{\rm ln}(z|x-y|/2)+1+\gamma_{E-M}-i(\pi/2)]\big[1+O\big(z^{2}|x-y|^{2}\big)\big]I_{N}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\frac{3i}{8\pi}z^{2}|x-y|^{2}\big[1+O\big(z^{2}|x-y|^{2}\big)\big]I_{N}

\displaystyle\quad\quad-\frac{1}{4\pi}\big\{\pi+i-i[\psi(1)+\psi(2)]\big\}z|x-y|\big[1+O\big(z^{2}|x-y|^{2}\big)\big]\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|}

\displaystyle\quad\quad-\frac{i}{2\pi}z|x-y|\text{\rm ln}(z|x-y|/2)\big[1+O\big(z^{2}|x-y|^{2}\big)\big]\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|}

(C.31)

as
z\to 0
,
z\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}}\big\backslash\{0\}
,
x,y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
,
x\neq y
.

(ii)
If
n=2
,
r=2
, then

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{2}}{\partial z^{2}}G_{0}(z;x,y)=\frac{1}{4\pi}[1+3(\gamma_{E-M}+i)-(3i\pi/2)]z|x-y|^{2}\big[1+O\big(z^{2}|x-y|^{2}\big)\big]I_{N}

\displaystyle\quad\quad-\frac{1}{2\pi}z^{-1}\big[1+O\big(z^{2}|x-y|^{2}\big)\big]I_{N}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\frac{3}{4\pi}z|x-y|^{2}\text{\rm ln}(z|x-y|/2)\big[1+O\big(z^{2}|x-y|^{2}\big)\big]I_{N}

\displaystyle\quad\quad-\frac{\pi+3i}{4\pi}|x-y|\big[1+O\big(z^{2}|x-y|^{2}\big)\big]\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|}

\displaystyle\quad\quad-\frac{i}{2\pi}\text{\rm ln}(z|x-y|/2)|x-y|\big[1+O\big(z^{2}|x-y|^{2}\big)\big]\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\frac{i}{4\pi}[\psi(1)+\psi(2)]|x-y|\big[1+O\big(z^{2}|x-y|^{2}\big)\big]\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|}

(C.32)

as
z\to 0
,
z\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}}\big\backslash\{0\}
,
x,y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
,
x\neq y
.

Given the results in Appendices
B
and
C
, we can summarize the estimates on
G_{0}(z;\,\cdot\,,\,\cdot\,)
as follows:

Theorem C.1
.

Let
r\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}
,
0\leqslant r\leqslant n
,
z\in\overline{{\mathbb{C}}_{+}}
, and
x,y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
,
x\neq y
.

(i)
For
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
odd,
n\geqslant 3
, one has the estimate

\displaystyle\bigg\|\frac{\partial^{r}}{\partial z^{r}}G_{0}(z;x,y)\bigg\|_{{\mathcal{B}}({\mathbb{C}}^{N})}

\displaystyle\quad\leqslant\widehat{c}_{n}\begin{cases}|x-y|^{r+1-n},&|z||x-y|\leqslant 1,\\
|z|^{(n-1)/2}|x-y|^{(2r+1-n)/2}e^{-\text{\rm Im}(z)|x-y|},&|z||x-y|\geqslant 1\end{cases}

\displaystyle\quad\leqslant\widetilde{c}_{n}|x-y|^{r+1-n}\big[1+|z|^{(n-1)/2}|x-y|^{(n-1)/2}\big]

\displaystyle\quad\leqslant\widetilde{C}_{n}\big\{|x-y|^{r+1-n}\chi_{[0,1]}(|z||x-y|)

\displaystyle\hskip 45.52458pt+|z|^{(n-1)/2}|x-y|^{(2r+1-n)/2}\chi_{[1,\infty)}(|z||x-y|)\big\}

\displaystyle\quad\leqslant C_{n}\big\{|x-y|^{r+1-n}\chi_{[0,1]}(|z||x-y|)

\displaystyle\hskip 45.52458pt+|z|^{(n-1)/2}\big[|x|^{(2r+1-n)/2}+|y|^{(2r+1-n)/2}\big]\chi_{[1,\infty)}(|z||x-y|)\big\}

\displaystyle\quad\leqslant c_{n}\big\{|x-y|^{r+1-n}\chi_{[0,1]}(|z||x-y|)

(C.33)

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt+|z|^{(n-1)/2}[1+|x|]^{(2r+1-n)/2}[1+|y|]^{(2r+1-n)/2}\chi_{[1,\infty)}(|z||x-y|)\big\},

where
\widehat{c}_{n},\widetilde{c}_{n},\widetilde{C}_{n},C_{n},c_{n}\in(0,\infty)
are appropriate constants.

(ii)
For
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
even, one has the following estimate. For every
\delta\in(0,1)
,

\displaystyle\bigg\|\frac{\partial^{r}}{\partial z^{r}}G_{0}(z;x,y)\bigg\|_{{\mathcal{B}}({\mathbb{C}}^{N})}

(C.34)

\displaystyle\quad\leqslant c_{n}\begin{cases}|x-y|^{r+1-n}\big[1+|\text{\rm ln}(z|x-y|/2)|\big],&|z||x-y|\leqslant 1,\,r\neq n,\\
|x-y|\big[1+|\text{\rm ln}(z|x-y|/2)|\big]+|z|^{-1},&|z||x-y|\leqslant 1,\,r=n,\\
|z|^{(n-1)/2}|x-y|^{(2r+1-n)/2}e^{-\text{\rm Im}(z)|x-y|},&|z||x-y|\geqslant 1\end{cases}

\displaystyle\quad\leqslant\widetilde{c}_{n,\delta}\begin{cases}|z|^{-\delta}|x-y|^{r+1-\delta-n},&|z||x-y|\leqslant 1,\,r\neq n,\\
|z|^{-\delta}|x-y|^{1-\delta}+|z|^{-1},&|z||x-y|\leqslant 1,\,r=n,\\
|z|^{(n-1)/2}|x-y|^{(2r+1-n)/2}e^{-\text{\rm Im}(z)|x-y|},&|z||x-y|\geqslant 1\end{cases}

\displaystyle\quad=\widetilde{c}_{n,\delta}\begin{cases}|z|^{-\delta}|x-y|^{r+1-\delta-n},&|z||x-y|\leqslant 1,\,r\neq n,\\
|z|^{-1}\big[|z|^{1-\delta}|x-y|^{1-\delta}+1\big],&|z||x-y|\leqslant 1,\,r=n,\\
|z|^{(n-1)/2}|x-y|^{(2r+1-n)/2}e^{-\text{\rm Im}(z)|x-y|},&|z||x-y|\geqslant 1\end{cases}

\displaystyle\quad\leqslant C_{n,\delta}\begin{cases}|z|^{-\delta}|x-y|^{r+1-\delta-n},&|z||x-y|\leqslant 1,\,r\neq n,\\
|z|^{-1},&|z||x-y|\leqslant 1,\,r=n,\\
|z|^{(n-1)/2}|x-y|^{(2r+1-n)/2}e^{-\text{\rm Im}(z)|x-y|},&|z||x-y|\geqslant 1\end{cases}

where
c_{n},\widetilde{c}_{n,\delta},C_{n,\delta}\in(0,\infty)
are appropriate constants.

Proof.

The first estimate in (
C.33
) (resp., in (
C.34
)) follows immediately in the regime
|z||x-y|\leqslant 1
from (
C.12
), (
C.13
), and (
C.14
) (resp., (
C.22
), (
C.23
), and (
C.24
) and (
C.27
), (
C.31
), and (
C.32
)). One employs Lemma
B.6
in conjunction with (
C.1
) to obtain the first estimate in (
C.33
), and (
C.34
) in the regime
|z||x-y|\geqslant 1
. In fact, by Lemma
B.6
and (
C.1
),
G_{0}(z;\,\cdot\,,\,\cdot\,)
is of the form

\displaystyle G_{0}(z;x,y)

\displaystyle=c_{1}z^{n/2}|x-y|^{1-(n/2)}H_{(n/2)-1}^{(1)}(z|x-y|)I_{N}

(C.35)

\displaystyle\quad+c_{2}z^{n/2}|x-y|^{1-(n/2)}H_{n/2}^{(1)}(z|x-y|)\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|}

\displaystyle=z^{n/2}|x-y|^{1-(n/2)}e^{iz|x-y|}

\displaystyle\quad\times\bigg[c_{1}\omega_{(n/2)-1}(z|x-y|)I_{N}+c_{2}\omega_{n/2}(z|x-y|)\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|}\bigg],

\displaystyle\hskip 152.22241ptx,y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n},\,x\neq y,\,z\in{\mathbb{C}}_{+},

for an appropriate pair of constants
c_{1},c_{2}\in{\mathbb{C}}
. The constants
c_{1}
and
c_{2}
are independent of
(z,x,y)
, and their precise values are immaterial for the purpose at hand. Differentiating throughout (
C.35
) with respect to
z
, one obtains

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{r}}{\partial z^{r}}G_{0}(z;x,y)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j,k,\ell\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}\\
j+k+\ell=r\end{subarray}}c_{j,k,\ell}z^{(n/2)-j}|x-y|^{r-j+1-(n/2)}e^{iz|x-y|}

(C.36)

\displaystyle\quad\times\bigg[c_{1}\omega_{(n/2)-1}^{(\ell)}(z|x-y|)I_{N}+c_{2}\omega_{n/2}^{(\ell)}(z|x-y|)\alpha\cdot\frac{(x-y)}{|x-y|}\bigg],

\displaystyle\hskip 152.22241ptx,y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n},\,x\neq y,\,z\in{\mathbb{C}}_{+},

where the
c_{j,k,\ell}
are constants which do not depend upon
(z,x,y)
. By (
C.36
),

\displaystyle\bigg\|\frac{\partial^{r}}{\partial z^{r}}G_{0}(z;x,y)\bigg\|_{{\mathcal{B}}({\mathbb{C}}^{N})}

\displaystyle\quad\leqslant\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j,k,\ell\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}\\
j+k+\ell=r\end{subarray}}\widetilde{c}_{j,k,\ell}|z|^{(n/2)-j}|x-y|^{r-j+1-(n/2)}e^{-\text{\rm Im}(z)|x-y|}|z|^{-(1/2)-\ell}|x-y|^{-(1/2)-\ell}

\displaystyle\quad\leqslant\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}j,k,\ell\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}\\
j+k+\ell=r\end{subarray}}\widetilde{c}_{j,k,\ell}|z|^{[(n-1)/2]-(j+\ell)}|x-y|^{r-(j+\ell)+(1/2)-(n/2)}e^{-\text{\rm Im}(z)|x-y|}

\displaystyle\quad\leqslant\widetilde{C}|z|^{[(n-1)/2]}\big[|z|^{j+\ell}|x-y|^{j+\ell}\big]^{-1}|x-y|^{(2r+1-n)/2}e^{-\text{\rm Im}(z)|x-y|}

\displaystyle\quad\leqslant\widetilde{C}|z|^{[(n-1)/2]}|x-y|^{(2r+1-n)/2}e^{-\text{\rm Im}(z)|x-y|},

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274ptx,y\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n},\,x\neq y,\,z\in{\mathbb{C}}_{+},\,|z||x-y|\geqslant 1,

where the
\widetilde{c}_{j,k,\ell}
are constants which do not depend upon
(z,x,y)
.
∎

Appendix D
A Product Formula for Modified Fredholm Determinants

The purpose of this appendix is to prove a product formula for regularized (modified) Fredholm determinants extending the well-known Hilbert–Schmidt case.

The result we have in mind is a quantitative version of the following fact:

Theorem D.1
.

Let
k\in{\mathbb{N}}
, and suppose
A,B\in{\mathcal{B}}_{k}({\mathcal{H}})
. Then

\displaystyle{\det}_{{\mathcal{H}},k}((I_{{\mathcal{H}}}-A)(I_{{\mathcal{H}}}-B))={\det}_{{\mathcal{H}},k}(I_{{\mathcal{H}}}-A){\det}_{{\mathcal{H}},k}(I_{{\mathcal{H}}}-B)\exp(\tr_{{\mathcal{H}}}(X_{k}(A,B))),

(D.1)

where
X_{k}(\,\cdot\,,\,\cdot\,)\in{\mathcal{B}}_{1}({\mathcal{H}})
is of the form

\displaystyle\begin{split}X_{1}(A,B)&=0,\\
X_{k}(A,B)&=\sum_{j_{1},\dots,j_{2k-2}=0}^{k-1}c_{j_{1},\dots,j_{2k-2}}C_{1}^{j_{1}}\cdots C_{2k-2}^{j_{2k-2}},\quad k\geqslant 2,\end{split}

(D.2)

with

\displaystyle\begin{split}&c_{j_{1},\dots,j_{2k-2}}\in{\mathbb{Q}},\\
&C_{\ell}=A\text{ or }B,\quad 1\leqslant\ell\leqslant 2k-2,\\
&k\leqslant\sum_{\ell=1}^{2k-2}j_{\ell}\leqslant 2k-2,\quad k\geqslant 2.\end{split}

(D.3)

Explicitly, one obtains:

\displaystyle X_{1}(A,B)

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle X_{2}(A,B)

\displaystyle=-AB,

\displaystyle X_{3}(A,B)

\displaystyle=2^{-1}\big[(AB)^{2}-AB(A+B)-(A+B)AB\big],

(D.4)

\displaystyle X_{4}(A,B)

\displaystyle=2^{-1}(AB)^{2}-3^{-1}\big[AB(A+B)^{2}+(A+B)^{2}AB+(A+B)AB(A+B)\big]

\displaystyle\quad+3^{-1}\big[(AB)^{2}(A+B)+(A+B)(AB)^{2}+AB(A+B)AB\big]

\displaystyle\quad-3^{-1}(AB)^{3},

etc.

When taking traces (what is actually needed in (
D.1
)), this simplifies to

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\tr_{{\mathcal{H}}}(X_{1}(A,B))=0,\\
&\tr_{{\mathcal{H}}}(X_{2}(A,B))=-\tr_{{\mathcal{H}}}(AB),\\
&\tr_{{\mathcal{H}}}(X_{3}(A,B))=-\tr_{{\mathcal{H}}}\big(ABA+BAB-2^{-1}(AB)^{2}\big),\\
&\tr_{{\mathcal{H}}}(X_{4}(A,B))=-\tr_{{\mathcal{H}}}\big(A^{3}B+A^{2}B^{2}+AB^{3}+2^{-1}(AB)^{2}\\
&\hskip 108.12054pt-(AB)^{2}A-B(AB)^{2}+3^{-1}(AB)^{3}\big),\\
&\quad\text{etc.}\end{split}

(D.5)

In the rest of this appendix we will detail the characterization of
X_{k}(A,B)
following the paper
[
36
]
. We also refer to
[
71
]
,
[
89
]
,
[
90
]
for related, but somewhat
different product formulas for regularized determinants.

To prove a quantitative version of Theorem
D.1
and hence derive a formula for
X_{k}(A,B)
, we first need to recall some facts on the commutator subspace of an algebra of noncommutative polynomials.

Let
{\rm Pol_{2}}
be the free polynomial algebra in
2
(noncommuting) variables,
A
and
B
. Let
W
be the set of noncommutative monomials (words in the alphabet
\{A,B\}
). (We recall that the set
W
is a semigroup with respect to concatenation,
1
is the neutral element of this semigroup, that is,
1
is an empty word in this alphabet.) Every
x\in{\rm Pol_{2}}
can be written as a sum

x=\sum_{w\in W}\widehat{x}(w)w.

(D.6)

Here the coefficients
\widehat{x}(w)
vanish for all but finitely many
w\in W
.

Let
[{\rm Pol_{2}},{\rm Pol_{2}}]
be the commutator subspace of
{\rm Pol_{2}}
, that is, the linear span of commutators
[x_{1},x_{2}]
,
x_{1},x_{2}\in{\rm Pol_{2}}
.

Lemma D.2
.

One has
x\in[{\rm Pol_{2}},{\rm Pol_{2}}]
provided that

\sum_{m=1}^{L(w)}\widehat{x}\bigl(\sigma^{m}(w)\bigr)=0,\quad w\in W.

(D.7)

Here,

L(w)
is the length of each word
w=w_{1}w_{2}\cdots w_{L(w)}
,

\sigma
is the cyclic shift given by
\sigma(w)=w_{2}\cdots w_{L(w)}w_{1}
.

Proof.

One notes that

x=\sum_{w\in W}\widehat{x}(w)w=\widehat{x}(1)+\sum_{w\neq 1}L(w)^{-1}\sum_{m=1}^{L(w)}\widehat{x}\bigl(\sigma^{m}(w)\bigr)\sigma^{m}(w).

(D.8)

Obviously,
(\sigma^{m}(w)-w)\in[{\rm Pol_{2}},{\rm Pol_{2}}]
for each positive integer
m
and thus,

x\in\bigg(\widehat{x}(1)+\sum_{w\neq 1}L(w)^{-1}\sum_{m=1}^{L(w)}\widehat{x}\bigl(\sigma^{m}(w)\bigr)w+[{\rm Pol_{2}},{\rm Pol_{2}}]\bigg).

(D.9)

By hypothesis,
\widehat{x}(1)=0
and

\sum_{m=1}^{L(w)}\widehat{x}\bigl(\sigma^{m}(w)\bigr)=0,\quad 1\neq w\in W,

(D.10)

completing the proof.
∎

Next, we need some notation.
Let
k_{1},k_{2}\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}={\mathbb{N}}\cup\{0\}
, and set

z_{k_{1},k_{2}}=\begin{cases}0,&k_{1}=k_{2}=0,\\
k_{1}^{-1}A^{k_{1}},&k_{1}\in{\mathbb{N}},\,k_{2}=0,\\
k_{2}^{-1}B^{k_{2}},&k_{1}=0,\,k_{2}\in{\mathbb{N}},\\
\sum_{j=1}^{k_{1}+k_{2}}j^{-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\pi\in S_{j},\,|\pi|=3\\
|\pi_{1}|+|\pi_{3}|=k_{1}\\
|\pi_{2}|+|\pi_{3}|=k_{2}\end{subarray}}(-1)^{|\pi_{3}|}z_{\pi},&k_{1},k_{2}\in{\mathbb{N}}.\end{cases}

(D.11)

Here,
S_{j}
is the set of all partitions of the set
\{1,\cdots,j\}
,
1\leqslant j\leqslant k_{1}+k_{2}
.
(The symbol
|\,\cdot\,|
abbreviating the cardinality of a subset of
{\mathbb{Z}}
.)
The condition
|\pi|=3
means that
\pi
breaks the set
\{1,\cdots,j\}
into exactly
3
pieces denoted by
\pi_{1}
,
\pi_{2}
, and
\pi_{3}
(some of them can be empty). The element
z_{\pi}
denotes the product

z_{\pi}=\prod_{m=1}^{j}z_{m,\pi},\quad z_{m,\pi}=\begin{cases}A,&m\in\pi_{1},\\
B,&m\in\pi_{2},\\
AB,&m\in\pi_{3}.\end{cases}

(D.12)

Finally, let
W_{k_{1},k_{2}}
be the collection of all words with
k_{1}
letters
A
and
k_{2}
letters
B
.

Using this notation we now establish a combinatorial fact.

Lemma D.3
.

Let
k_{1},k_{2}\in{\mathbb{N}}
. Then

z_{k_{1},k_{2}}=\sum_{w\in W_{k_{1},k_{2}}}\Bigg(\sum_{\ell=0}^{n(w)}\frac{(-1)^{\ell}}{k_{1}+k_{2}-\ell}\binom{n(w)}{\ell}\Bigg)w,

(D.13)

where

n(w)=|S(w)|,\quad S(w)=\{1\leqslant\ell\leqslant L(w)-1\,|\,w_{\ell}=A,\,w_{\ell+1}=B\}.

(D.14)

Proof.

For each
j\in\{1,\ldots,k_{1}+k_{2}\}
, let

\displaystyle\Pi_{j}

\displaystyle=\{\pi\in S_{j}\,|\,|\pi|=3,\,\,|\pi_{1}|+|\pi_{3}|=k_{1},\,|\pi_{2}|+|\pi_{3}|=k_{2}\},

(D.15)

\displaystyle\Pi_{j,w}

\displaystyle=\{\pi\in\Pi_{j}\,|\,z_{\pi}=w\},\quad w\in W_{k_{1},k_{2}}.

(D.16)

One observes that
|\pi_{3}|\leqslant n(w)\leqslant\min\{k_{1},k_{2}\}
and that

j=|\pi_{1}|+|\pi_{2}|+|\pi_{3}|=k_{1}+k_{2}-|\pi_{3}|.

(D.17)

For any partition
\pi\in\Pi_{j,w}
, let
I\subseteq S(w)
indicate which subwords
AB
in
w
arise from elements in
\pi_{3}
. Then
|I|=|\pi_{3}|=k_{1}+k_{2}-j
. Therefore, each partition in
\pi\in\Pi_{j,w}
is determined by a unique choice of
I
and each such choice of
I
determines the choice of
\pi
uniquely. This implies that

|\Pi_{j,w}|=\binom{n(w)}{k_{1}+k_{2}-j}.

(D.18)

Thus,

\displaystyle z_{k_{1},k_{2}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{w\in W_{k_{1},k_{2}}}\sum_{j=1}^{k_{1}+k_{2}}j^{-1}\sum_{\pi\in\Pi_{j,w}}(-1)^{|\pi_{3}|}w

\displaystyle=\sum_{w\in W_{k_{1},k_{2}}}\sum_{j=1}^{k_{1}+k_{2}}(-1)^{k_{1}+k_{2}-j}j^{-1}|\Pi_{j,w}|w

\displaystyle=\sum_{w\in W_{k_{1},k_{2}}}\sum_{j=1}^{k_{1}+k_{2}}(-1)^{k_{1}+k_{2}-j}j^{-1}\binom{n(w)}{k_{1}+k_{2}-j}w.

(D.19)

Taking into account that

\binom{n(w)}{k_{1}+k_{2}-j}=0,\quad k_{1}+k_{2}-j\notin\{0,\cdots,n(w)\},

(D.20)

it follows that

\displaystyle z_{k_{1},k_{2}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{w\in W_{k_{1},k_{2}}}\sum_{j=k_{1}+k_{2}-n(w)}^{k_{1}+k_{2}}(-1)^{k_{1}+k_{2}-j}j^{-1}\binom{n(w)}{k_{1}+k_{2}-j}w

\displaystyle=\sum_{w\in W_{k_{1},k_{2}}}\Bigg(\sum_{\ell=0}^{n(w)}\frac{(-1)^{\ell}}{k_{1}+k_{2}-\ell}\binom{n(w)}{\ell}\Bigg)w.

(D.21)

∎

We can now prove the main fact about the commutator subspace of
{\rm Pol_{2}}
needed later on.

Lemma D.4
.

For every
k_{1},k_{2}\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
z_{k_{1},k_{2}}\in[{\rm Pol_{2}},{\rm Pol_{2}}]
.

Proof.

Let
w
be any element in
W_{k_{1},k_{2}}
and let
m
be any positive integer.
If
\sigma^{m}(w)
starts with the subword
AB
,
then
\sigma^{m+1}(w)
has the form
B\cdots A
and therefore has one fewer subwords
AB
than
\sigma^{m}(w)
;
that is,
n\bigl(\sigma^{m+1}(w)\bigr)=n\bigl(\sigma^{m}(w)\bigr)-1
.
If, however,
\sigma^{m}(w)
does not start with the subword
AB
,
then the
AB
subwords of
\sigma^{m+1}(w)
are precisely the
AB
subwords of
\sigma^{m}(w)
each shifted once;
hence,
n\bigl(\sigma^{m+1}(w)\bigr)=n\bigl(\sigma^{m}(w)\bigr)
.

Now, to calculate
\sum_{m=1}^{L(w)}\widehat{z_{k_{1},k_{2}}}\bigl(\sigma^{m}(w)\bigr)
,
one may assume, by applying cyclic shifts, that
w
starts with
AB
.
Then there are
n(w)
shifted words
\sigma^{m}(w)
which start with the subword
AB
,
and it follows that
n(w)
of the numbers
\{n\bigl(\sigma^{m}(w)\bigr)\>:\>1\leqslant m\leqslant L(w)\}
equal
n(w)-1

and that the remaining
L(w)-n(w)=k_{1}+k_{2}-n(w)
numbers equal
n(w)
.
Lemma
D.3
therefore implies that

\displaystyle\sum_{m=1}^{L(w)}\widehat{z_{k_{1},k_{2}}}\bigl(\sigma^{m}(w)\bigr)

\displaystyle=\sum_{m=1}^{L(w)}\Bigg(\sum_{\ell=0}^{n(\sigma^{m}(w))}\frac{(-1)^{\ell}}{k_{1}+k_{2}-\ell}\binom{n\bigl(\sigma^{m}(w)\bigr)}{\ell}\Bigg)

\displaystyle=n(w)\Bigg(\sum_{\ell=0}^{n(w)-1}\frac{(-1)^{\ell}}{k_{1}+k_{2}-\ell}\binom{n(w)-1}{\ell}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\quad+(k_{1}+k_{2}-n(w))\Bigg(\sum_{\ell=0}^{n(w)}\frac{(-1)^{\ell}}{k_{1}+k_{2}-\ell}\binom{n(w)}{\ell}\Bigg).

(D.22)

Since

\binom{n(w)-1}{n(w)}=0,

(D.23)

it follows that

\displaystyle\sum_{m=1}^{L(w)}\widehat{z_{k_{1},k_{2}}}\bigl(\sigma^{m}(w)\bigr)

\displaystyle=n(w)\Bigg(\sum_{\ell=0}^{n(w)}\frac{(-1)^{\ell}}{k_{1}+k_{2}-\ell}\binom{n(w)-1}{\ell}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\quad+(k_{1}+k_{2}-n(w))\Bigg(\sum_{\ell=0}^{n(w)}\frac{(-1)^{\ell}}{k_{1}+k_{2}-\ell}\binom{n(w)}{\ell}\Bigg)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell=0}^{n(w)}\frac{(-1)^{\ell}}{k_{1}+k_{2}-\ell}\Bigg(n(w)\binom{n(w)-1}{\ell}

\displaystyle\quad+(k_{1}+k_{2}-n(w))\binom{n(w)}{\ell}\Bigg).

(D.24)

Clearly,

n(w)\binom{n(w)-1}{\ell}+(k_{1}+k_{2}-n(w))\binom{n(w)}{\ell}=(k_{1}+k_{2}-\ell)\binom{n(w)}{\ell},

(D.25)

and thus

\sum_{m=1}^{L(w)}\widehat{z_{k_{1},k_{2}}}\bigl(\sigma^{m}(w)\bigr)=\sum_{\ell=0}^{n(w)}(-1)^{\ell}\binom{n(w)}{\ell}=0.

(D.26)

Hence, Lemma
D.2
completes the proof.
∎

Next, we introduce some further notation. Let
k\in{\mathbb{N}}
and set

\displaystyle\begin{split}x_{1}&=0,\\
x_{k}&=\sum_{j=1}^{k-1}j^{-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\mathcal{A}}\subseteq\{1,\cdots,j\}\\
j+|{\mathcal{A}}|\geqslant k\end{subarray}}(-1)^{|{\mathcal{A}}|}y_{{\mathcal{A}}},\quad k\geqslant 2,\end{split}

(D.27)

\displaystyle\begin{split}y_{1}&=0,\\
y_{k}&=\sum_{j=1}^{k-1}j^{-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\mathcal{A}}\subseteq\{1,\cdots,j\}\\
j+|{\mathcal{A}}|\leqslant k-1\end{subarray}}(-1)^{|{\mathcal{A}}|}y_{{\mathcal{A}}},\quad k\geqslant 2,\end{split}

(D.28)

\displaystyle y_{{\mathcal{A}}}

\displaystyle=\prod_{m=1}^{j}y_{m,{\mathcal{A}}},\quad y_{m,{\mathcal{A}}}=\begin{cases}A+B,&m\notin{\mathcal{A}},\\
AB,&m\in{\mathcal{A}}.\end{cases}

(D.29)

In particular,

\sum_{j=1}^{k-1}j^{-1}(A+B-AB)^{j}=x_{k}+y_{k},

(D.30)

and one notes that the length of the word
y_{{\mathcal{A}}}
subject to
{\mathcal{A}}\subseteq\{1,\dots,j\}
, equals

L(y_{{\mathcal{A}}})=\big|{\mathcal{A}}^{c}\big|+2|{\mathcal{A}}|=j+|{\mathcal{A}}|,\quad 1\leqslant j\leqslant k-1,\;k\geqslant 2

(D.31)

(with
A^{c}=\{1,\dots,j\}\backslash{\mathcal{A}}
the complement of
{\mathcal{A}}
in
\{1,\dots,j\}
).

Using this notation we can now state the following fact:

Lemma D.5
.

Let
k\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
k\geqslant 2
, then

y_{k}\in\bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{k-1}\frac{1}{j}(A^{j}+B^{j})+[{\rm Pol_{2}},{\rm Pol_{2}}]\bigg).

(D.32)

Proof.

Employing

y_{k}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k_{1},k_{2}\geqslant 0\\
k_{1}+k_{2}\leqslant k-1\end{subarray}}z_{k_{1},k_{2}},

(D.33)

Lemma
D.4
yields

z_{k_{1},k_{2}}\in[{\rm Pol_{2}},{\rm Pol_{2}}],\quad k_{1},k_{2}\in{\mathbb{N}}.

(D.34)

Since by (
D.11
),

z_{0,0}=0,\quad z_{k_{1},0}=k_{1}^{-1}A^{k_{1}},\;k_{1}\in{\mathbb{N}},\quad z_{0,k_{2}}=k_{2}^{-1}B^{k_{2}},\;k_{2}\in{\mathbb{N}},

(D.35)

combining (
D.33
)–(
D.35
) completes the proof.
∎

After these preparations we are ready to return to the product formula for regularized determinants and specialize the preceding algebraic considerations to the context of
Theorem
D.1
.

First we recall that by (
D.27
) and (
D.31
),

x_{k}=\sum_{j=1}^{k-1}j^{-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}{\mathcal{A}}\subseteq\{1,\cdots,j\}\\
j+|{\mathcal{A}}|\geqslant k\end{subarray}}(-1)^{|{\mathcal{A}}|}y_{{\mathcal{A}}}:=X_{k}(A,B)\in{\mathcal{B}}_{1}({\mathcal{H}}),\quad k\geqslant 2,

(D.36)

since for
1\leqslant j\leqslant k-1
,
L(y_{{\mathcal{A}}})=j+|{\mathcal{A}}|\geqslant k
, and hence one obtains the inequality

\|x_{k}\|_{{\mathcal{B}}_{1}({\mathcal{H}})}\leqslant c_{k}\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leqslant k_{1},k_{2}<k\\
k_{1}+k_{2}\geqslant k\end{subarray}}\|A\|_{{\mathcal{B}}_{k}({\mathcal{H}})}^{k_{1}}\|B\|_{{\mathcal{B}}_{k}({\mathcal{H}})}^{k_{2}},\quad k\in{\mathbb{N}},\;k\geqslant 2,

(D.37)

for some
c_{k}>0
,
k\geqslant 2
. We also set (cf. (
D.27
)
X_{1}(A,B)=0
.

Theorem D.6
.

Let
k\in{\mathbb{N}}
and assume that
A,B\in{\mathcal{B}}_{k}({\mathcal{H}})
. Then

{\det}_{{\mathcal{H}},k}((I_{{\mathcal{H}}}-A)(I_{{\mathcal{H}}}-B))={\det}_{{\mathcal{H}},k}(I_{{\mathcal{H}}}-A){\det}_{{\mathcal{H}},k}(I_{{\mathcal{H}}}-B)\exp({\tr}_{{\mathcal{H}}}(X_{k}(A,B))).

(D.38)

Proof.

First, we suppose that
A,B\in{\mathcal{B}}_{1}({\mathcal{H}})
. Then it is well-known that

{\det}_{{\mathcal{H}},1}(I_{{\mathcal{H}}}-A){\det}_{{\mathcal{H}},1}(I_{{\mathcal{H}}}-B)={\det}_{{\mathcal{H}},1}((I_{{\mathcal{H}}}-A)(I_{{\mathcal{H}}}-B)),

(D.39)

consistent with
X_{1}(A,B)=0
. Without loss of generality we may assume that
k\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
k\geqslant 2
, in
the following. Employing

{\det}_{{\mathcal{H}},k}(I_{{\mathcal{H}}}-T)={\det}_{{\mathcal{H}}}(I_{{\mathcal{H}}}-T)\exp\bigg({\tr}_{{\mathcal{H}}}\bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{k-1}j^{-1}T^{j}\bigg)\bigg),\quad T\in{\mathcal{B}}_{1}({\mathcal{H}}),

(D.40)

one infers that

\displaystyle{\det}_{{\mathcal{H}},k}((I_{{\mathcal{H}}}-A)(I_{{\mathcal{H}}}-B))={\det}_{{\mathcal{H}},k}(I_{{\mathcal{H}}}-(A+B-AB))

\displaystyle\quad={\det}_{{\mathcal{H}}}(I_{{\mathcal{H}}}-(A+B-AB))\exp\bigg({\tr}_{{\mathcal{H}}}\bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{k-1}j^{-1}(A+B-AB)^{j}\bigg)\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad={\det}_{{\mathcal{H}}}(I_{{\mathcal{H}}}-A){\det}_{{\mathcal{H}}}(I_{{\mathcal{H}}}-B)\exp\bigg({\tr}_{{\mathcal{H}}}\bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{k-1}j^{-1}(A+B-AB)^{j}\bigg)\bigg)

\displaystyle\quad={\det}_{{\mathcal{H}},k}(I_{{\mathcal{H}}}-A){\det}_{{\mathcal{H}},k}(I_{{\mathcal{H}}}-B)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\exp\bigg({\tr}_{{\mathcal{H}}}\bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{k-1}j^{-1}\big[(A+B-AB)^{j}-A^{j}-B^{j}\big]\bigg)\bigg).

(D.41)

By (
D.30
) one concludes that

{\tr}_{{\mathcal{H}}}\bigg(\sum_{j=1}^{k-1}j^{-1}\big[(A+B-AB)^{j}-A^{j}-B^{j}\big]\bigg)={\tr}_{{\mathcal{H}}}(x_{k})+{\tr}_{{\mathcal{H}}}\bigg(y_{k}-\sum_{j=1}^{k-1}j^{-1}\big(A^{j}+B^{j}\big)\bigg).

(D.42)

By Lemma
D.5
,

y_{k}-\sum_{j=1}^{k-1}j^{-1}\big(A^{j}+B^{j}\big)

(D.43)

is a sum of commutators of polynomial expressions in
A
and
B
. Hence,

\bigg(y_{k}-\sum_{j=1}^{k-1}j^{-1}\big(A^{j}+B^{j}\big)\bigg)\subset[{\mathcal{B}}_{1}({\mathcal{H}}),{\mathcal{B}}_{1}({\mathcal{H}})],

(D.44)

and thus,

{\tr}_{{\mathcal{H}}}\bigg(y_{k}-\sum_{j=1}^{k-1}j^{-1}\big(A^{j}+B^{j}\big)\bigg)=0,

(D.45)

proving assertion (
D.38
) for
A,B\in{\mathcal{B}}_{1}({\mathcal{H}})
.

Since both, the right and left-hand sides in (
D.38
) are continuous with respect to the norm in

{\mathcal{B}}_{k}({\mathcal{H}})
, and
{\mathcal{B}}_{1}({\mathcal{H}})
is dense in
{\mathcal{B}}_{k}({\mathcal{H}})
, (
D.38
) holds for arbitrary

A,B\in{\mathcal{B}}_{k}({\mathcal{H}})
.
∎

Appendix E
Notational Conventions

For convenience of the reader we now summarize most of our notational conventions used
throughout this manuscript.

Basic Abbreviations:

We employ the shortcut
{\mathbb{N}}_{0}={\mathbb{N}}\cup\{0\}
.

\lfloor\,\cdot\,\rfloor
denotes the floor function on
{\mathbb{R}}
, that is,
\lfloor x\rfloor
characterizes the largest integer less than or equal to
x\in{\mathbb{R}}
. Similarly,
\lceil\,\cdot\,\rceil
denotes ceiling function, that is,

\lceil x\rceil
characterizes the smallest integer larger than or equal to
x\in{\mathbb{R}}
.

We abbreviate
{\mathbb{C}}_{\pm}=\{z\in{\mathbb{C}}\,|\,\pm\text{\rm Im}(z)>0\}
.

Vectors and Matrices:

Vectors in
{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
are denoted by
x=(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})^{\top}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
(with
\top
abbreviating the transpose operation) or
p=(p_{1},\ldots,p_{n})^{\top}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
,
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
. For
x=(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})^{\top}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
we abbreviate

\langle x\rangle=\big(1+|x|^{2}\big)^{1/2},

(E.1)

where
|x|=\big(x_{1}^{2}+\cdots+x_{n}^{2}\big)^{1/2}
denotes the standard Euclidean norm of
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
,
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
.

The dot symbol, “
\,\cdot\,
”, is used in three different ways: First, it denotes the standard scalar product in
{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
,

x\cdot y=\sum_{j=1}^{n}x_{j}y_{j},\quad x=(x_{1},\dots,x_{n})^{\top},\;y=(y_{1},\dots,y_{n})^{\top}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}.

(E.2)

Second, we will also use it for
n
-vectors of operators,
{\underline{A}}=(A_{1},\ldots,A_{n})^{\top}

and
{\underline{B}}=(B_{1},\ldots,B_{n})^{\top}
acting in the same Hilbert space in the form

{\underline{A}}\cdot{\underline{B}}=\sum_{j=1}^{n}A_{j}B_{j},

(E.3)

whenever it is obvious how to resolve the domain issues of the possibly unbounded operators involved.

For
X
a given space,
T
a linear operator in
X
, and

A=(a_{j,k})_{1\leqslant j,k\leqslant N}\in{\mathbb{C}}^{N\times N}
an
N\times N
matrix with constant complex-valued entries acting in
{\mathbb{C}}^{N}
,
N\in{\mathbb{N}}
, we will avoid tensor product notation as in

T\otimes A\,\text{ in }\,X\otimes{\mathbb{C}}^{N},

(E.4)

such that

X\otimes{\mathbb{C}}^{N}\,\text{ is identified with the symbol }\,X^{N}=(X,\dots,X)^{\top},

(E.5)

and

T\otimes A\,\text{ is identified with }\,TA=(Ta_{j,k})_{1\leqslant j,k\leqslant N}=(a_{j,k}T)_{1\leqslant j,k\leqslant N}=AT.

(E.6)

That is, we interpret
T\otimes A
as entrywise multiplication, resulting in an
N\times N
block operator matrix
TA=AT
. Thus, if
T=(T_{1},\dots,T_{n})
, with
T_{j}
,
1\leqslant j\leqslant n
, operators
in
{\mathcal{H}}
, and
A=(A_{1},\dots,A_{n})
, with
A_{j}\in{\mathbb{C}}^{N\times N}
,
1\leqslant j\leqslant n
,
N\times N
matrices with constant, complex-valued entries acting in
{\mathbb{C}}^{N}
, we will employ the dot symbol also in the form

T\cdot A=\sum_{j=1}^{n}T_{j}A_{j}=\sum_{j=1}^{n}A_{j}T_{j}=A\cdot T,

(E.7)

where
T_{j}A_{j}=A_{j}T_{j}
,
1\leqslant j\leqslant n
, are defined as in (
E.6
).

A\in X^{m\times n}
,
m,n\in{\mathbb{N}}
, represents an
m\times n
matrix

A=(A_{j,k})_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leqslant j\leqslant m\\
1\leqslant k\leqslant n\end{subarray}}
, with entries
A_{j,k}
in
X
,
1\leqslant j\leqslant m,\,1\leqslant k\leqslant n
. In particular,
F=(F_{1},\dots,F_{n})^{\top}\in X^{n}
is a vector with
n
components and
F_{j}\in X
denotes its
j
-th component,
1\leqslant j\leqslant n
.

The identity operator in
{\mathbb{C}}^{n}
is represented by
I_{n}
, ,
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
.

Special Functions and Function Spaces:

For special functions such as the Gamma function
\Gamma(\,\cdot\,)
, the digamma function
\psi(\,\cdot\,)=\Gamma^{\prime}(\,\cdot\,)/\Gamma(\,\cdot\,)
, Bessel functions of order
\nu
,
J_{\nu}(\,\cdot\,),Y_{\nu}(\,\cdot\,)
, Hankel functions of the first kind and order
\nu
,
H^{(1)}_{\nu}(\,\cdot\,)
, the Euler–Macheroni constant
\gamma_{E-M}
, etc., we refer to
[
1
]
.

To simplify notation, we frequently omit Lebesgue measure whenever possible and simply use
L^{p}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})
instead of

L^{p}({\mathbb{R}}^{n};d^{n}x)
,
p\in(0,\infty)\cup\{\infty\}
.

Weak
L^{p}
-spaces (i.e., Lorentz spaces

L^{p,q}({\mathbb{R}}^{n};d\rho)
with
q=\infty
), are denoted by

L^{p}_{weak}({\mathbb{R}}^{n};d\rho)
,
p\in(0,\infty)
. Here
({\mathbb{R}}^{n},d\rho)
represents a separable measure space and the measure
\rho
is assumed to be
\sigma
-finite. The seminorm on
L^{p}_{weak}({\mathbb{R}}^{n};d\rho)
is abbreviated by

\|f\|_{L^{p}_{weak}({\mathbb{R}}^{n};d\rho)}:=\sup_{t>0}\big(t[\mu_{f}(t)]^{1/p}\big),\quad p\in(0,\infty),

(E.8)

where

\mu_{f}(t)=\rho(\{x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n}\,|\,|f(x)|>t\}).

(E.9)

In particular,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|f+g\|_{L^{p}_{weak}({\mathbb{R}}^{n};d\rho)}\leqslant c_{p}\big[\|f\|_{L^{p}_{weak}({\mathbb{R}}^{n};d\rho)}+\|g\|_{L^{p}_{weak}({\mathbb{R}}^{n};d\rho)}\big],&\\
\ c_{p}=\max\big(2,2^{1/p}\big),\;p\in(0,\infty).&\end{split}

(E.10)

Again, we omit the measure
\rho
and just employ the notation

L^{p}_{weak}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})
in case
\rho
equals Lebesgue measure on
{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
.

If
n\in{\mathbb{N}}
and
N\in{\mathbb{N}}\backslash\{1\}
, we set

[L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}:=L^{2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n};{\mathbb{C}}^{N}),\quad[W^{1,2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})]^{N}:=W^{1,2}({\mathbb{R}}^{n};{\mathbb{C}}^{N}),\,\text{ etc.}

(E.11)

The symbol
{\mathcal{F}}
is used to denote the Fourier transform,
f^{\wedge}:={\mathcal{F}}f
, similarly
f^{\vee}:={\mathcal{F}}^{-1}f
,
f\in{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})
, with
{\mathcal{S}}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})
the Schwartz test function space, and
{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}({\mathbb{R}}^{n})
its dual with elements the tempered distributions. In particular,

\displaystyle f^{\wedge}(p)=(2\pi)^{-n/2}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}x\,e^{-ip\cdot x}f(x),\quad f^{\vee}(x)=(2\pi)^{-n/2}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{n}}d^{n}p\,e^{ip\cdot x}f(p),

\displaystyle\hskip 207.7052ptp,x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{n},\;f\in{\mathcal{S}}({\mathbb{R}}^{n}).

(E.12)

If
-\infty\leqslant a<b\leqslant\infty
and
F
maps
{\mathbb{C}}\backslash(a,b)
to a normed linear space, then the normal boundary values of
F
at
\lambda\in(a,b)
(when these values exist) are denoted by
F(\lambda\pm i0):=\lim_{\varepsilon\downarrow 0}F(\lambda\pm i\varepsilon)
.

For
n,k\in{\mathbb{N}}
and an open set
\Omega\subset{\mathbb{R}}^{n}
,
C^{k}(\Omega)
denotes the set of all
f:\Omega\to{\mathbb{C}}
that are
k
times continuously differentiable.

Linear Operators in Hilbert Spaces:

Let
{\mathcal{H}}
,
{\mathcal{K}}
be separable, complex Hilbert spaces,
(\,\cdot\,,\,\cdot\,)_{{\mathcal{H}}}
the scalar
product in
{\mathcal{H}}
(linear in the second argument),
\|\,\cdot\,\|_{{\mathcal{H}}}
the norm on
{\mathcal{H}}
,
and
I_{{\mathcal{H}}}
the identity operator in
{\mathcal{H}}
.

If
T
is a linear operator mapping (a subspace of) a Hilbert space into another, then

\dom(T)
and
\ker(T)
denote the domain and kernel (i.e., null space) of
T
.
The closure of a closable operator
A
is denoted by
\overline{A}
.
The set of closed linear operators with domain contained in
{\mathcal{H}}
and range contained in

{\mathcal{K}}
is denoted by
{\mathcal{C}}({\mathcal{H}},{\mathcal{K}})
(or simply by
{\mathcal{C}}({\mathcal{H}})
if
{\mathcal{H}}={\mathcal{K}}
).

The resolvent set, spectrum, and point spectrum (i.e., the set of eigenvalues) of a closed operator
T
are denoted by
\rho(T)
,
\sigma(T)
, and
\sigma_{p}(T)
, respectively.

If
S
is self-adjoint in
{\mathcal{H}}
, the family of strongly right-continuous spectral projections
associated with
S
is denoted by
E_{S}(\lambda)=E_{S}((-\infty,\lambda])
,
\lambda\in{\mathbb{R}}
, moreover, the singular, discrete, essential, absolutely continuous, and singularly continuous spectrum
of
S
are denoted by
\sigma_{s}(S)
,
\sigma_{d}(S)
,
\sigma_{ess}(S)
,
\sigma_{ac}(S)
,
and
\sigma_{sc}(S)
, respectively.

For a densely defined closed operator
S
in
{\mathcal{H}}
we employ the abbreviation
\langle S\rangle:=\big(I_{{\mathcal{H}}}+|S|^{2}\big)^{1/2}
, and
similarly, if
T=(T_{1},\dots,T_{n})^{\top}
, with
T_{j}
densely defined and closed
in
{\mathcal{H}}
,
1\leqslant j\leqslant n
,

\langle T\rangle=\big(I_{{\mathcal{H}}}+|T|^{2}\big)^{1/2},\quad|T|=\big(|T_{1}|^{2}+\cdots+|T_{n}|^{2}\big)^{1/2},

(E.13)

whenever it is obvious how to define
|T_{1}|^{2}+\cdots+|T_{n}|^{2}
as a self-adjoint operator.

The Banach spaces of bounded and compact linear operators on a separable complex Hilbert space
{\mathcal{H}}
are denoted by
{\mathcal{B}}({\mathcal{H}})
and
{\mathcal{B}}_{\infty}({\mathcal{H}})
, respectively; the corresponding
\ell^{p}
-based Schatten–von Neumann ideals are denoted by
{\mathcal{B}}_{p}({\mathcal{H}})
, with associated norm abbreviated by

\|\cdot\|_{{\mathcal{B}}_{p}({\mathcal{H}})}
,
p\geqslant 1
.

Following a standard practice in Mathematical Physics, we simplify the notation of operators of multiplication by a scalar or matrix-valued function
V
and hence use
V
rather than the more elaborate symbol
M_{V}
throughout this manuscript.

Extensions of index theory inspired by scattering theory
(17frg668), June 18–25, 2017, where part of this work was initiated.
